# Lessons: full entries

Why this project's APIs, formats and agreed behaviours had to change. `lessons.md` has one line per
entry: read that first, and look entries up here by date and name rather than reading this whole. New
entries go last, in the same commit as the change:

```markdown
## YYYY-MM-DD <short name>
- Changed: ...
- Why we had to change: ...
- Original design was wrong because: ...
- Lesson: ...
```

## 2026-10-23 annai's translation TSV: a note part for the translator
- Changed: `l10n/<tag>.tsv`'s note column keeps a translator's part (`Note: …`) after the part the strings script writes. Before, the script rewrote the whole column from the code on every run.
- Why we had to change: the first translator's reasons (why a sentence became "label: value", why "she" became "Shiori") were wiped by the next `npm run strings`.
- Original design was wrong because: one column was meant for both the script and people, but only the script could write it.
- Lesson: when a file is written by a tool and by people, give each writer a part the other never touches.

## 2026-10-26 annai: modules pass data, and words are made at display
- Changed: while annai's screens were localized, several interfaces between its UI modules changed:
  - `restoreFrom()`'s `say` gets a `progress` flag instead of callers matching "Getting files";
  - `readForm()` reports `schema: true`;
  - `removalWords()` returns parts;
  - `thingWords()` takes a sentence-start noun instead of a capital made in code;
  - `downloadedWhen()` has no leading space;
  - the stored "Shiori's answer" title and the author ids ("user", "shiori", "system") are mapped to words at display;
  - plugin pages stopped matching "Providers: ".
- Why we had to change: a translation can't be glued from English fragments, capitalised in code, or matched by its English text. Each of these broke as soon as the words were Japanese.
- Original design was wrong because: modules passed English phrases to each other, and some code tested those phrases, so the words were the data.
- Lesson: pass data between modules (kinds, flags, values, ids), and make whole sentences only where they are shown. Never match on words a person reads.

## 2026-10-27 annai: plain fields are English, `shown` holds the screen's words
- Changed: annai's built-in search providers had put the screen's words in `name` and `why` and English in a new `en` field, while MCP errors did the reverse (English `message`, screen `shown`). Both now follow one rule: plain fields are English, for Shiori and logs, and an optional `shown` holds the screen's words. This covers search providers' `name`/`why`, McpError, and what L7 adds for plugins.
- Why we had to change: with `en`, a plugin that translated its `why` and didn't know about `en` would silently send Shiori Japanese. The failure was invisible.
- Original design was wrong because: the safe default (English to the model) depended on a plugin remembering an extra field.
- Lesson: when one value has two audiences, make the field the machine or model reads the default, and the human-facing one the optional extra. A forgotten extra then shows up as English on screen, where people notice it, instead of leaking.

## 2026-10-27 annai: a new record always writes the field that tells it from an old one
- Changed: annai's backup holds (why backups to a place stopped) gained `said`, what the failure was, so screens make its words. A hold from a place's or a plugin's own refusal had no `said`, and a hold from before the change had none either. Now every new hold writes `said`, `null` when the words are the place's own, and an old hold is one with no `said` key at all.
- Why we had to change: the screens told old holds from new ones by a missing `said`. A plugin's refusal kept today looked old, so its reason was dropped and its words were marked as annai's English.
- Original design was wrong because: "absent" meant both "written before this field existed" and "nothing to say", two states with one look.
- Lesson: when later code must tell old records from new ones, write the field on every new record, with `null` for empty, and test for the key (`'said' in h`), not its value.

## 2026-10-27 annai: an error's optional parts go in one options object from the start
- Changed: annai's `PluginError(pluginId, message, cause)` became `PluginError(pluginId, message, { cause, shown })` when its errors gained the screen's words (`shown`). `ProviderError` already took a `more` object and only grew `said` inside it.
- Why we had to change: a second optional part had nowhere to go but a fourth position, or a change to what the third one meant. Every caller that passed `cause` had to be found and changed.
- Original design was wrong because: an optional argument was given its own position. The first optional part set the shape for every later one.
- Lesson: after the required arguments, anything optional goes in one options object, even when there is only one so far. A new optional part then changes no caller.

## 2026-10-27 annai: a choice keeps its value and its words apart
- Changed: the settings form's `Field.options` (src/ui/settings-form.js) went from values (`['basic', 'advanced']`) to `[value, words]` pairs, when plugins' settings gained translatable choices (JSON Schema `oneOf` with `const` and `title`).
- Why we had to change: the form showed an `enum`'s values as its words, but a translated screen needs words for a choice that differ from the value it saves.
- Original design was wrong because: one string was both what is stored and what a person reads, so the words could never be anything but the stored value.
- Lesson: give a choice two parts from the start, the value the code keeps and the words a person reads, even while they are the same.

## 2026-10-27 annai: an error carries which of its words are a developer's English
- Changed: annai's errors gained `en`: `true` when all of their words are annai's own developer English, or a string, the part of their `shown` words that is. `PluginError` takes it in its options object (`{ cause, shown, en }`; with neither `shown` nor `cause` it is `true`), and `EnglishError` is a plain Error with it set. `showError` and the notice card (`errors: [e]`) read it and mark the words `lang="en"` where they stand. Five package checks that put the screens' words in `message` now keep English there and the screens' words in `shown`.
- Why we had to change: a Japanese screen reader voiced about 40 plugin checks and Simulate's itinerary errors with a Japanese voice, and no screen could tell an English message from a translated one.
- Original design was wrong because: whether words were English was only a `/* en */` comment at the source. The error a screen got said nothing about it.
- Lesson: when the language of a value matters to whoever shows it, the value carries that fact (a flag, or the part that differs), set where it is made. A comment in the source reaches no reader.

## 2026-10-27 annai: a plugin's `api` is the oldest host it needs, not an exact match
- Changed: annai's plugin manifest `api` had to equal annai's own `API_VERSION` (1). It now means the API a plugin needs: annai accepts any `api` up to its own (2), and refuses a newer one in words ("…supports API 2 and older"). Plugins that use `annai.l10n` say `api: 2`.
- Why we had to change: official plugins started using `annai.l10n`. A page still running the older annai would install their new versions, and they then failed to start. With an exact match, raising the API would have refused every existing plugin instead.
- Original design was wrong because: one number meant both "what this plugin needs" and "what this host has". Any addition to the API then broke every plugin, so the number was never raised, and so it protected nothing.
- Lesson: a version a component declares about its host is the minimum it needs. The host accepts anything at or below its own, and the number is raised only when the component starts using something an older host lacks.

## 2026-10-27 annai: a name the screens show is read from what is stored, not from what happens to be running
- Changed: annai's `pluginName` (which names an official plugin in the screens' words) became async. It reads the installed plugin's own words from the device, where it used to read a cache filled only while the plugin runs, and before that a catalog fetch that each screen awaited.
- Why we had to change: the cache named a switched-off plugin in English, and the catalog fetch made provider pages wait on the network (20 s on a stalled connection). Each fix to one source broke a case the other had covered.
- Original design was wrong because: the words came from wherever was handy (a runtime cache, a network file), not from the one place that always holds them, the installed package.
- Lesson: read what a screen shows from the stored source of truth, even if that makes the call async. A cache or a network copy is only for when nothing is stored.

## 2026-10-28 annai: an official plugin's translations are not files of its package
- Changed: annai's official plugins stopped shipping `l10n/<tag>.json` and listing `l10n`. Each one's words in each language are `catalog/l10n/<tag>/<id>.json`, hashed in `catalog/l10n/<tag>.json` (`translations`). Each phone fetches only its screens' language and keeps it at the plugin's `l10n/<tag>.json`. `verifyOfficial` counts only the package's files, an official install keeps the stored words, and `annai.l10n` reads the current words.
- Why we had to change: with three languages, translations were a third of the plugins' bytes, mostly in languages a phone never shows. A fix to one language's words was a plugin update for every user.
- Original design was wrong because: content each user needs one of (one language of N) sat inside the unit that is versioned and verified whole (the package). So every language, and every change to any of them, cost everyone.
- Lesson: when a unit you version and verify whole carries parts each user needs only one of, or parts that change on their own schedule, give those parts their own hash and fetch path from the first version, keyed by what selects them.

## 2026-10-28 annai: what a model makes of a stored file is kept beside it, and an explicit ask redoes it
- Changed: a reading made for a model that can't take a photo or PDF (a description, or the PDF's pages as pictures) went into the one request that first needed it. It is now a `reading` log entry in the conversation, keyed by the file's content hash, with its page pictures as the entry's blobs. Every later request where a model meets the file gets the kept reading. An explicit `read` reads it afresh, and the newest reading of each kind wins.
- Why we had to change: the next step and the next turn lost what the file said, or paid to read it again. Then, once kept, a refusal or a misreading could never be corrected ("that's wrong, read it again" got the same text).
- Original design was wrong because: it attached derived content to the request that first needed it, not to the thing it was derived from. The first fix then made the kept copy final.
- Lesson: keep derived content that later requests need with its owner, named by what it was made from (a content hash), and let an explicit ask redo it, newest wins.

## 2026-10-29 annai: mark both ends of a move on their records
- Changed: Shiori's `move_file` and the trip tools moved a file as a delete in one place and a write in another, each logged on its own. Both entries now carry `data.moved` (`{ to, path }` on the delete, `{ from, path }` on the write), and Undo's own writes carry `data.undo`.
- Why we had to change: Undo of each side undid only its own half, so after Undo a file sat in two trips, or in a trip and in Waiting. Later rounds then judged "the user's own work" by those writes and either kept trips that should go or removed a trip with someone else's file in it.
- Original design was wrong because: it recorded one operation as two unrelated records, so nothing could tell a move from a deletion plus a new file, nor whose work a moved file was.
- Lesson: when one operation spans two records, write the relation on both, so either side can reverse the whole thing. Mark what an undo itself writes, so later decisions never mistake it for the user's own work.

## 2026-10-29 annai: keep a reading's uncertainty per field, where the user acts on it
- Changed: a booking Shiori read stopped waiting to be confirmed whole (`status: 'unverified'` until the user tapped Looks right). It now lists the fields she isn't sure of in `unsure: string[]`, and only those get a light "check" mark. `status` stays and marks bookings from before; a change she makes to one the user confirmed keeps it confirmed.
- Why we had to change: the user found the separate confirming step "bit extra" and wanted fewer taps ("No step; she asks only when unsure"). With one flag, the only way to clear it was to check every field.
- Original design was wrong because: one flag stood for both "Shiori read this" and "any field may be wrong", so clearing it meant checking everything, and any change she made set the whole booking back to unchecked.
- Lesson: record a model's uncertainty at the level the user acts on (here the field), not as one flag over the whole record. Require the field on every new write, so a record without it can only be an old one.

## 2026-10-30 annai: a provider's reply is kept as it came, beside annai's reading of it
- Changed: annai's provider contract gained a native reply: what the provider returned (Anthropic's content array, OpenRouter's reasoning_details), kept for one turn beside annai's parts and sent back where it can be read. Before, a signed `reasoning` part per thinking block was meant to carry what the provider needed.
- Why we had to change: Claude 5.5 binds each thinking block to everything sent before it, the earlier blocks of its own reply included. annai rebuilt replies from its own parts (text joined across blocks, citations and empty thinking blocks dropped), and changed earlier content between steps (each step's now block, each half's instructions), so every step after the first got a 400 on the phone.
- Original design was wrong because: it modelled a provider's reply as annai's parts and expected the provider to take those parts back, when the provider checks what it gets back against what it sent.
- Lesson: when a provider checks what is sent back, keep its reply exactly as it came, beside annai's own reading of it, and make the requests of one exchange append-only; annai's parts are for annai and for other models.

## 2026-10-30 annai: a native reply goes to the models that can read it, and a refusal is read by status
- Changed: a native is kept for an endpoint plus a scope its endpoint type gives each model (`nativeScope`; Chat Completions: the model's maker prefix), and a native counts toward size limits for what it adds (`covers` on its event). `reasoning` is made by the core from any 400 on a request that sent natives, no longer by each plugin matching the provider's words.
- Why we had to change: one OpenRouter endpoint serves every maker's models, so a native kept "for the endpoint" went to GPT on a fallback; matching Anthropic's wording in two plugins would break silently when the wording changes, and OpenRouter's error shape wasn't known.
- Original design was wrong because: it took the endpoint for the unit that can read a provider's reply, and the provider's words for the signal that it was refused.
- Lesson: scope what a provider returns to who can read it, not to where it was fetched; act on a request's status and on what was sent, not on an error's text.

## 2026-11-01 annai: a log's last number never goes back
- Changed: `forget` (src/core/forget.js) writes one `forget` entry after any removal, numbered after every entry it removed, and keeps only the newest such entry per scope. `forget`'s `history` matcher also gets the entry (`history(path, e)`), and `Match.mustFree` lets a caller free a path's content only when nothing else names it, rolling the whole transaction back otherwise (`kept: 'here' | 'history' | 'elsewhere'`).
- Why we had to change: Delete in Files frees a file by removing its history, which is the scope's newest entries, so the next entry took their numbers again; `changedSince` and `backupState` compare the last number, so a backup made before the Delete read as current and kept the deleted file.
- Original design was wrong because: the last sequence number served as the scope's change counter, while removing entries could lower it; nothing removed the newest entries until Delete did.
- Lesson: when anything compares a position in a log, removing entries must still move the position forward; give removal its own entry rather than rewinding.

## 2026-11-02 annai: live Koe gets `sleep` back (plan decision 29 reversed)
- Changed: the live voice half (Koe in a realtime session) has a `sleep` tool again, used only when the user asks or a note from Kokoro says so; it closes the session like a tap. Before, live Koe had no `sleep` and only the user's tap or a quiet spell closed a session.
- Why we had to change: on a drive with music over the car stereo the session never went quiet, so it never closed; the user asked whether Shiori could put herself to sleep, and chose "when I ask, or kokoro tells koe to go to sleep. often kokoro is much more capable than realtime models."
- Original design was wrong because: it assumed silence would always come, so a timer alone could end a session; in a car, sound never stops, and the only thing that can tell music from the user is a model reading what was heard.
- Lesson: an end condition built on the absence of a signal (silence, no fixes, no events) needs a second way out that doesn't wait for that absence: a decision by whoever can judge, or an explicit command.

## 2026-11-04 annai: a "moved" watch counts from when it was set, not from Shiori's last wake
- Changed: annai's `moved {km}` watch (`km0`) no longer starts its count again at every wake of hers. It counts from when the watch was set, or annai's last start (`km0` is memory only), and with `repeat` from its last firing. Its summary and firing text say so: "you have travelled 5.2 km since this watch started counting", no longer "since your last wake".
- Why we had to change: on a test drive a 3 km watch never fired after about 8 km. With live voice every spoken turn is a wake, so the count kept going back to zero.
- Original design was wrong because: it tied a promise made to one watch ("after this far") to an event that belongs to something else (Shiori waking), written when wakes were rare. It also made the watch mean "this far with nothing else happening", which nobody setting a distance asks for, and when voice made wakes frequent nothing failed visibly: the watch just stayed silent.
- Lesson: a watch or timer counts from its own start, and resets only on its own firing. Don't reset it from an unrelated event because that event seems to make it redundant: how often that event happens will change.

## 2026-11-05 annai: screen-on is one setting, and a tap in Auto holds voice for that exchange

- **Changed:** the setting `live.keepScreenOn` replaces the per-situation `live.awake`, and `Voice.hold`/`release` keeps voice on for one exchange after a tap in Auto.
- **Why:** the per-situation choice lived in a menu that one-tap GPS removed, and a tap in Auto opened a text box when the user expected to talk.
- **Original design was wrong because:** it gave each situation its own choice, when the user only ever wanted one answer, and it let "Auto" decide what a direct tap on the talk button meant.
- **Lesson:** a direct action outranks an automatic mode. An automatic setting decides what happens when the user does nothing, never what their tap means.

## 2026-11-05 annai: all a reload needs is one overwritten record

- **Changed:** the odometer, the place it counts from, the activity and when moving fast ended moved from `state` entries read back at start, and the open trip's last point, to one localStorage record through an injected `keep`. A `moved` watch records its odometer reading when set and at each firing.
- **Why:** an iPhone reloads a Home Screen web app often, and three review rounds each found another disagreement among those sources. A count rebuilt from logged points skipped the odometer's own filters.
- **Original design was wrong because:** one fact lived in several places, and an append-only log doubled as state.
- **Lesson:** state a restart needs is one record written from one method, with values that must agree written together. A log is evidence, not state. A count that must survive is kept as a counter, and each consumer records its own reading of it. Anything that wipes state stops its writers first.

## 2026-11-05 annai: voice, four changes from the second drive

- **Changed:** a learned voice level is the second-loudest of the last 5 answered turns (`voiceDb`), not a falling maximum. A provider's end of turn after a silent tool answer is awaited with a 2 s bound, and a late one is that turn's. `LiveKoe.send` became `write` then `send`. `armUnlock` stays armed, and a tap after a stall makes a new AudioContext.
- **Why:** a drive with music on had lyrics answered, and a stretch of silence with notes unsaid because the sound never played.
- **Original design was wrong because:** one reading was taken as truth; a wait depended on an event the other side may not send; one call both recorded in order and could wait on something slow; "unlocked once" was treated as permanent.
- **Lesson:** a threshold learned from outside sound never takes one sample as truth. Never let a wait depend on an event the other side may not send: bound it, and say what a late one means. Recording and sending are separate steps. "It worked once" isn't a state: sound can get stuck later, so keep the way back armed. And test a real tool call before shipping a model: a clean open proves nothing (Gemini 3.8 Live Extended Thinking opened, and never made a call).

## 2026-11-05 annai: a gate reads the conversation, not one line

- **Changed:** `gatePrompt(lastSaid)` became `gatePrompt(lines)`, the last few lines of the conversation.
- **Why:** real comments right after she spoke were marked chatter on a drive, and an answer to a question she had just asked aloud could be lost.
- **Original design was wrong because:** one line can't show that speech follows from what was just said.
- **Lesson:** give a classifier the context a person would need. And give it a way to be wrong cheaply: here a wrong "for her" costs a short reply, while a wrong "not for her" loses what the user said.

## 2026-11-05 annai: Auto voice removed; voice and Live are each one tap

- **Changed:** the setting is on or off, and a saved `auto` reads as off. `lastChoices` and the `voiceMode` route inputs are gone. Realtime and Classic became a Live chip, with `koeMode`'s stored values unchanged. Voice turns itself off when a drive ends and when the mic's own input disappears.
- **Why:** the user couldn't predict it ("do you think user will understand what it does?"), and the chip's reason took the whole line. In the trace the iPhone's audio read only as unknown or speaker; a car was never detected.
- **Original design was wrong because:** it decided from audio-device names that a phone's browser rarely gives, then fell back to a remembered choice per activity, so the same situation could give different answers and nobody could say why.
- **Lesson:** an automatic setting must rest on a signal seen working on the real device, and be sayable in one sentence. Keep one measured rule (driving, from GPS) and make the rest a switch the user owns. A change of state is an event told once from its source, not worked out again from a value that can change for other reasons.

## 2026-11-05 annai: a model its plugin dropped leaves Koe's Realtime list at the update
- Changed: `syncProviders` takes a model out of Koe · Realtime's saved list at the update whose description dropped it: the previous description named it and this one doesn't. A model the user puts back stays. A description that lists no models for that ability is partial and leaves the list alone. A drop that would empty the list puts in the description's recommended model for that ability (its first with a record, if it recommends none), so an id swapped by an update keeps Live working. The list goes in the same write as the provider (`saveProvider` takes the roles). The rest of the list keeps its order, and everything else stays: the model's record, Kokoro's picks, Koe · Classic, and models added by hand or from a list.
- Why we had to change: taking Gemini's Extended Thinking out of the plugin left it in saved lists, so each session after its 3-minute rest opened a refused session first, and a list holding only it said "Live is resting" at every use. The user had picked "no refused sessions". The first fix then took out any model the description didn't name at every update, so one the user put back left again, a description with an empty list cleared Koe's Live, and a swapped id left no Live at all.
- Original design was wrong because: "models gone from the description stay as they are" was written for chat models, where a dropped model may well still answer. It treated every list as the user's own choice, even when the entry only named something the source had since withdrawn. The first fix read "not named now" as "dropped now", when a drop is a change between two versions of the source.
- Lesson: removing something from a catalog isn't finished until you've read what saved settings do with it. List what names it, then decide each one's fate, and test through the update path, not only on fresh data. When two saves must go together and the store has a transaction, use it: there's then no order to get right. A rule that reacts to an update can't ship with that update: a page on the old code may apply it first, so the rule goes out a release ahead.

## 2026-11-05 annai: one path for a gap in GPS evidence
- Changed: the drive record gains a `stale` field ('owed', 'told' or null; an older record has none, and reads the same), and `ActivityTracker.restore` a fourth argument, `old`. A record too old to restore now goes through the same tracker path as a reload or an unlock, with Live showing unknown and voice not held until a reading decides, and no time bound. A change found on a stale record tells the triggers when it happened, and one from before a watch was set doesn't fire it. A reading worse than 100 m says nothing about the activity, and a bound's end is dated at the bound, not from the last fix. `#wasFast`, `#stopOwed`, `#changeHeard` and `#late === 'fast'` went from Live; `#late` is now a plain flag for the one late end that stays.
- Why we had to change: a reload, an unlock and a kill each judged "is this the same drive?" in their own way, in the tracker and again in Live. Every patch round left a route where they disagreed: false stops, doubled ends, a stop lost across a reload.
- Original design was wrong because: it treated an old record as a different case from a gap, so Live re-judged what the tracker had already decided, with flags to remember what it had decided.
- Lesson: one question, one place that answers it. When fixes keep finding another disagreement between two places, take one away instead of syncing them. Cases that look different often decide the same way and differ in a few things you can name. Here an old record differs in what shows, in voice, in having no time bound, and in one rule for a fast speed on a slow way. And a straight-line way can prove a drive went on, never that it ended: a city, a winding road and an errand loop all look slow, so a slow way ends nothing out loud. A fuzz with one road speed couldn't see that: give a model the variety the real thing has.

## 2026-11-06 annai: a hold's date is in the drive record, and goes with its hold
- Changed: the drive record gains `heldEnd`: the date a stale record's hold will end with, `null` when there is none (an older record has no such key and reads the same; a page on older code ignores it). `ActivityTracker.restore`'s optional arguments became one options object, `{ from, old, heldEnd }`, and the tracker gains a `heldEnd` getter. Live writes the record whenever the date comes or goes.
- Why we had to change: the date lived only in the tracker's memory, so a reload or a second kill in the minute after a parked reopening lost it. The same-drive rule then swallowed a true "starts" for 5 minutes, and a "when I stop" set in that minute was spent at the hold's end.
- Original design was wrong because: the date was treated as part of a moment that would soon pass, but a hold lasts a minute and a reload can land in it. "All a reload needs is one record" (2026-11-05) already said so; the date was left out to keep a fix pass small.
- Lesson: state that lasts past one event belongs in the record a restart reads from the start. A saved value that something can cancel is written when it is cancelled, not only when it is set: here a green light cancels the hold without any other reason to write, and a date left in the record would come back after a reload and lose a reminder at the real stop. A copy that outlives what it belongs to is worse than none.

## 2026-11-06 annai: what only tests call is unused
- Changed: `KoeWay` loses `using` and `koe`; `Live.pushNews`, `PluginHost.installZip`, `scaledClock`, `wavSeconds` and `announcement` are gone; `matchAt` and `rankEntry` moved into search.test.js as the test's own helpers; and `PluginHost`'s `maxPackageBytes` option is gone, since only a test could set it and the shipped limit (`inspectPackage`'s default) then had no test through `inspect` at all. `way.path` says what `using` said, `newsArrived` is the real way news comes in, a plugin zip is read by the Add screen before `install`, and `PlayClock` does what `scaledClock` did.
- Why we had to change: nothing shipped called them, and with no build step each line is sent to every phone. `pushNews` had the same body as `newsArrived`, so the tests proved a copy.
- Original design was wrong because: each began as the real path or a convenience, the real path moved, and the tests kept the old one alive, so nothing showed it was dead.
- Lesson: a member kept for tests is shipped code that no operator runs. Tests go through the real path, or keep their helper to themselves. A test seam stays only when it resets module state or is the one way into logic that does ship, as an option whose shipped default has its own test (`inspectPackage`'s `maxBytes`, tried small in packages.test.js and at the real limit through `host.inspect`).

## 2026-11-06 annai: a take-over belongs to the shell, not to the screen it leaves
- Changed: the hand-over to Driving, and to a plugin's `homeFor` screen, left `renderNow` and became one listener in the shell. It now runs from Trips, the Conversation, the Itinerary and Now, not while someone is typing, and with a trip open for Driving. A plugin's `homeFor` screen takes over from those four too (`docs/plugins.md`). `now-card` and `ui:now-section` also draw at the top of the Itinerary's today, with the same arguments. Driving's ← goes where it was entered from, no longer always to Now.
- Why we had to change: the timeline design ends the top bar, so Now can't be the one screen you are on as a drive begins. The user picked "Driving takes over, once" for the Conversation.
- Original design was wrong because: Now was the home screen, so a listener on Now looked like a listener on the app. It worked only while Now showed, and its `was` began again at each draw.
- Lesson: what must happen wherever the user is lives in the one place that is always there. A screen's listener only works while that screen shows, and says nothing about the others.

## 2026-11-06 Trip dates and last talked on the scope record
- Changed: a trip's scope record now carries `start` and `end` (absent when undated, copied only as `YYYY-MM-DD`) and `talked` (ms, `null` when nobody has spoken). `createTrip` writes them, `syncTripTitles`' hook keeps the dates and `record()` stamps `talked` for a user's own lines. `backfillTrips` fills records with no `talked` key once, at start and after an import. No key means "not mirrored yet". Archives don't carry the mirror: export leaves it out and import reads it again from what came.
- Why we had to change: live mode and the timeline can't read 500 trip.json files at each change, and live mode must choose the trip in play in memory.
- Original design was wrong because: a scope record named only what the list screen needed, so anything asking "which trips matter now" had to open every trip.
- Lesson: a record that lists things carries what deciding among them needs, not only what today's list shows. A mirror has one writer per field and a key that says it was filled, so filling old records is one pass that ends. What can be read again from the source doesn't travel: an archive's copy could only be stale, or claim something.

## 2026-11-06 annai: looking isn't doing
- Changed: `live.play` no longer follows the open conversation. Among happening trips it's the one last talked in, and a line typed or heard moves it at once: Live hears it on the agent's `event`. `reminders()` and the watches looked at leave out a happening trip that's open but not in play. With no trip happening, the open one is still in play.
- Why we had to change: with two trips overlapping, opening the other to check something took play with it, and the reminders, Driving and the relay all followed a glance. The user picked "Talking moves it".
- Original design was wrong because: it read "open" as "what the user is doing". An open screen only says where they're looking.
- Lesson: what the app acts on follows what the user did, not what's on screen. Looking changes nothing that fires, reminds or takes over.

## 2026-11-06 annai: a copy is held to its source, and to what stands still
- Changed: `Live.reminders`'s options became `{ waiting, held }`: `waiting` names the trips with copies at the relay, and `held` hears, by key, what the plans of those waiting to be talked in say. `reminders()` rejects if a read it needs fails, and the sync stops with nothing decided and tries again by itself; `readWatches` rejects the same way. `Mirrored` gains `says`, and an item in `/push.json` gains `says`, the digest of what its plan says of it. The mirror's `held`, `plan()`'s fourth argument, is now only keys: the copies that stay. Before, it mixed trips kept whole with one open trip's keys. Which trip is open no longer plays any part.
- Why we had to change: a stop renamed, moved to another place, or with its booking changed kept its old copy, so the phone pushed the old words. An edit made offline escaped if the user left the trip before the connection came back. The user picked "Call it off (Recommended)": "Same as a changed time: the waiting reminder goes, and wrong words never arrive."
- Original design was wrong because: `held` named trips, which answers "is the trip still there?". The key carries only an item's time, so nothing else in a plan could be seen, and reading the open trip's plan alone made the answer depend on what was on screen at sync time. `item.hash` can't take this job: it holds the screens' language and the trip's name, so changing either would call off a good copy, and it can't see a place moved under the same name. Pass 7 also gave `held` no way to say "I could not look", and `#planOf` caught every read failure as an absent stop. Pass 8 answered with a second argument naming the trips read short, so a plan could be part read, and everything downstream had to know: the next review found six more things a short plan could do (empty the plan, send short words, not wake, fire a false "off plan"). Each could be patched. The partial plan was the mistake.
- Lesson: a copy that outlives what it was made from carries a digest of what it was made from, and each look compares that with the source itself, whatever screen is open. Make the digest from data alone, and test that it stands still: a sentence, a language, a clock or a position would call off a good copy. A copy from before the digest takes the source's at the first look, since it can't be asked what it was made from: neither all called off nor kept for ever. A source that wasn't asked isn't a source that said nothing: a file whose read failed (not one that isn't JSON, which answered) is "could not look", its own answer apart from "no stop". Pass 7 swallowed every read failure, so one failed read called off a good copy for good (the user saw nothing), and the same swallow in the push mirror's own file turned notifications off. And "could not look" is never a part result: a read answers, or nothing changes. Whatever is read is read whole into locals and assigned at the end, a decision made from it stops whole if it can't be, and what failed tries again by itself, so nothing downstream has to know how much was read. One failed read then holds up everything that needs it for a moment, which loses nothing here: copies at the relay still push.

## 2026-11-06 annai: the conversation has its own address
- Changed: a conversation's address is `#/trip/<id>/talk` (the router's `talk`). `#/talk` and `#/plan` stay accepted: while a trip is open, they go to its own address by the shell's own replace (`replaceHash`); with none open, they still show the no-trip conversation and the Itinerary's empty state until stage 7. Moving to a trip's address (its conversation, Itinerary or Details) opens that trip; a redraw never does, and a redraw of another trip's conversation (Shiori moved it, an Undo) replaces the address with the open one's. An address whose trip is gone replaces itself with home.
- Why we had to change: `#/talk` named no conversation. Phone Back to it showed whichever conversation was open by then, not the one the user had been reading, and a notification couldn't say which conversation to open.
- Original design was wrong because: it took "the conversation" for a single place, with the open trip as hidden state beside the address. Once there is more than one, the address is the only thing history keeps, so it has to name which one. And opening on every draw, as Details did, would reopen the old trip after Shiori moved on, stopping her turn: `open` is never a no-op.
- Lesson: an address names the thing it shows, not "the current one". A screen opens what it shows only when the user moved there, never on a redraw. An old address goes where it now means by the shell's own replace (`location.replace` drops `history.state`, which now holds the entry's number), so Back can't land on it and bounce.

## 2026-11-06 annai: a tapped notification tells the open page which conversation
- Changed: the service worker's `openReminder` reads the notification's scope under the strict rule `^trip-[0-9a-z]+$`. With annai open and brought forward, it posts a new message, `{ type: 'annai.open', scope }`, and the page's `onReminderOpened` (push.js) shows that trip's conversation; with none open, it opens `/#/trip/<scope>/talk`. Anything else, `me` included, opens `/#/trips`, or with annai open posts nothing. `annai.push` and `onPushMessage` are unchanged.
- Why we had to change: a tap only focused an open annai, which stayed on whatever screen it showed, and opened `/` otherwise. With a conversation per trip, a reminder has to land in the trip it is about.
- Original design was wrong because: `annai.push` only says "something happened, read the news": it carries no scope, and the news is read by live mode, not by the screens. Telling the page where to go is a different job, so it is a message of its own rather than a field squeezed into the old one.
- Lesson: when two parts ship apart (a page and its service worker), each message type does one job and carries what its receiver needs to act. The payload is outside text: the sender checks it by a strict rule, and the receiver uses it only as an address, which names nothing that isn't there and goes to the safe default. (Fix pass 1 removed the page's second check: the worker is ours and same-origin, and `talkHref` and `openTrip` make any string safe.)

## 2026-11-07 annai: Back and Forward skip what is gone
- Changed: each history entry's `history.state` holds `annaiIdx` beside `annaiScroll`: a new entry is one past the entry drawn last, and the shell's own replaces (`replaceHash`, which replaced `location.replace` in the shell and `toTalk`) keep it. What other code opens over a screen (the photo viewer; on an iPhone, a question: `overlayEntry` in `dom.js`) takes the screen's `annaiScroll` and a number between the screen's and the next, so `annaiIdx` can be a fraction. One rule in the shell, before any screen draws, covers every `#/trip/<id>/…` address: a trip that isn't on this device has no screen (the screens' own "isn't on this device" pages and `ctx.goneTrip()` are gone; `openTrip` only opens the conversation). That entry is passed over, drawing nothing: reached by Back over an entry seen before, the shell goes `history.go(-1)` again; by Forward, `history.go(1)` while a newer entry is known ahead; at the first entry, as a new entry or with nothing ahead, the entry becomes Trips in place (the newest entry known, `top`, is kept in sessionStorage as it changes, so a reload still knows what is ahead). Once a move has passed one, it also passes entries at the address it left (they would show that screen again), and a popstate that lands on an entry with the same address (which fires no hashchange) draws it. Entries from before they were numbered carry no number and take the last case. A Forward that can't go on, with Trips shown or on an entry that repeats the screen shown, would draw Trips again or put Trips over a live entry, so it goes back to the entry it left (`history.go(-steps)`) and draws nothing; an overlay's entry reached from another address is passed too, as nothing is open over it then, and a Forward onto one goes on or back (each overlay closes itself at any popstate, so the shell needs no check of the DOM), once the screen's own entry is known as the newest (`top`) when what was open over it closes. A reload with an overlay's entry current draws its screen and goes back one entry, so Back doesn't land on the same drawing, and takes that entry as the newest, since its push cut off what was ahead. A Forward that returns draws nothing and keeps no scroll for the next redraw, which keeps its place. Undo's moves (to Trips, to the trip's conversation) yield to a screen the user went to while it ran, unless it is a screen of a trip Undo removed, which is left for the conversation the files went back to, or else as Remove trip leaves it. A question closes as Cancel at any move, also where the browser has CloseWatcher (a Forward, a tapped reminder), and when the shell replaces this entry with another screen (`replaceHash` closes the open questions: a replace fires no popstate). `ctx.removed(id)` walks only while the address is still that trip's, so a tap made while the removal runs stands. A read of the trip that fails is the screen's error, thrown in its try. Remove trip, and the Undo of the trip the user is in, call `ctx.removed()` in place of going to Trips: it goes back as Back does, over the trip's entries and over Trips, to the first other screen, where Trips is pushed (which cuts the trip's entries out of Forward); at the first entry, Trips there. A top-bar tab that names no trip (Itinerary, Conversation) goes straight to the open trip's address, and does nothing if it is shown.
- Why we had to change: after Remove trip, Back from Trips landed on Trips again once for each entry the removed trip had left in the history (four Backs in the review's steps; and still once for each file, booking, Recap or plugin tab), and the Undo of a trip Shiori started did it once. Remove itself pushed a Trips entry next to the Trips before the trip, so the first Back showed Trips twice. A tab tapped on its own screen added an entry that the redirect replaced with the screen already shown, so Back seemed to do nothing. An entry the photo viewer or a question left behind had no number, so Back over it stopped on nothing.
- Original design was wrong because: a gone trip's address replaced itself with Trips in place, which fixes one entry. The history still held every other entry of the trip, and each of them replaced itself the same way. The redirect did the same for a tab: it let the entry be made, then fixed it. Each screen decided for itself whether its trip was there, so the screens that didn't ask kept stopping Back. And "go to Trips" after a removal pushed an entry that Back could only reach as the same screen again.
- Lesson: an entry that can't be shown is passed over in the direction the user was going, not replaced one at a time, and that is decided once, in the shell, before anything draws, for every address that names the thing. Back must not show the screen it left again: a move that passes entries also passes those at the address it left. That needs the history's order, which the browser doesn't give a page, so the shell numbers its entries and keeps the number through its own replaces; entries other code pushes carry a number too. Forward needs one number more (the newest entry known): `history.forward()` on the newest entry does nothing and leaves the screen blank. A link that would be redirected at once goes straight to where it ends. Leaving a screen for good (Remove) goes the way Back goes, not forward to a new entry.

## 2026-11-06 A chat is a trip with a draft flag
- Changed: the scope record gains `draft: true` on one standing draft (`src/core/chats.js`), a trip scope made at start and after each one is used, and never removed. `createTrip`'s options gain `draft`, which gives the draft already there instead of a second one, in the same transaction. `listTrips` returns `{ active, archived, draft }`: the draft is in neither list. Exports and `changedSince` leave it out, and live mode never makes it the trip in play, so no location point goes into it. Its first use (the user's first line, typed or heard and not dismissed, or a file handed in) clears the flag, sets `created` to that moment, and names it as `system` from the line ("Photo · 12:10" or "Voice · 12:10" when there are no words) while it still has the draft's name, `New conversation`.
- Why we had to change: the + opens a conversation with no naming step, and has to call `open` and `tap` before its first `await` so the microphone's start stays inside the tap (an iPhone rule). The agent, a voice session and the camera all need a real scope before the first word, so one must be ready before the tap.
- Original design was wrong because: a trip was always made by naming it, so every scope was a conversation someone meant to have. A scope made ahead of need isn't one until it's used, and the lists, exports and live mode must tell the two apart from the record, not from whether it has lines.
- Lesson: a thing made ahead of need carries its own mark, written by the one that makes it and cleared by the one that spends it, and every list, export and automatic follower that reads those records leaves it out. An older page doesn't know the mark: it shows the draft as an ordinary trip called New conversation, which it can open, use or remove like any other.

## 2026-11-06 start_trip refuses in an undated conversation
- Changed: `start_trip` called with a conversation open whose trip.json has no `start` (a chat) refuses, saying to patch `/trip/trip.json` with the title and dates if the chat is becoming a trip; in a dated trip it works as before. An undated conversation's system text gains "# This conversation has no dates", taken from the snapshot so it doesn't change from turn to turn; it calls the name a first guess only while annai's own naming is trip.json's last write (2026-11-09: a name the user gave is not hers to change). The conversation shows "Shiori set the dates" at that write, a line that stays there whatever the user says after and through a reload (stage 5), and whatever happens to the dates after (2026-11-10); its Undo shows while the dates are still the ones her patch that first dated it set, read from that write's log entry. Undo takes back only the dates (the version before's `start` and `end`, written as the user's update), so a name given since stays, and the line then says "Undone".
- Why we had to change: chats are trip scopes. In one, `start_trip` made a second trip without opening it, so "plan our weekend in Testville" in a fresh chat left the user in an empty chat beside a trip they never saw.
- Original design was wrong because: it took any open conversation for a trip that a booking might not fit, so starting another one beside it was the only way. A chat is the trip it may become: nothing to start, only dates to give.
- Lesson: when a thing can become another by gaining a field, the tool that made the other kind refuses where the field is missing, and its refusal names the field and the file to set. A change made for the user by the model shows where they look, from the record that already holds the version before, with Undo.

## 2026-11-09 annai: an archive's times stay within a date's range
- Changed: the archive's `TIME` (every time in its manifest and every `ts` in its history log) is a number within what a Date holds, ±8.64e15 ms (ECMA-262), not any number. A file with one outside it is refused as damaged, like any other malformed file. No annai wrote such a time, so no file annai made is refused.
- Why we had to change: since stage 4 home places each chat by its record's `created`, or by the user's last line, and past a date's range `Intl` throws "Invalid time value". A hand-made `.annai.zip` with a line at `ts: 1e300`, or a trip record with `created: 1e300`, imported ("Imported."), and then home said "This screen couldn't open" at every draw, with no way to the trips but their addresses.
- Original design was wrong because: it took "a number" for a time, but a time here is something code makes a date of, so the format has to promise what a date holds. The first reader to make a date of it (home, stage 4) was the first to meet a value only a hand-made file can carry, and by then it was stored.
- Lesson: a field that is read as a date states a date's range in the format's schema, and a file with a value outside it is refused when it is read in, not guarded in each screen that draws it.

## 2026-11-09 annai: a file's moves are undone as one chain, and Undo's count is her writes alone
- Changed: Undo of a hand-over took back each move of a file from both of its ends, one trip after another. It now follows each moved file's whole chain once: its moves paired exactly (the same one in order of the moves between the same two places by the same hand), back to where the hand-over found it, then on to where the file is now, and puts it back there once. The `handover.undo` mark keeps its shape, but its `undone` (what she is told as "N files put back as they were") now counts only her writes it put back in that conversation. A moved file is said by `back` and `waiting`, as before. Old pages read the mark the same way.
- Why we had to change: in a file moved back and forth more than once in one hand-over, the undo of a repeated move matched the later arrival, and the file ended one move short, in a trip the user never put it in. Three patches in a row had each fixed one more case: stage 4's entry, a check for a move already taken back, and a condition for this Undo's own moves. A trip removed on the way left the file in the last trip. And a moved file was counted in `undone` by whichever end Undo reached first, so one conversation was told "2 files put back" for her booking and a file that had left it.
- Original design was wrong because: it treated a file's moves as separate operations, each reversible from what it found at its ends, when only their order says where the file belongs. And it let a count mean whatever its side happened to do, so its value depended on the order Undo visited the trips.
- Lesson: reverse a sequence of operations on one thing as one sequence, read in order from the records both ends keep, never step by step from guesses about the present. A count in a record counts one kind of thing, whichever side writes it, and what another field already says isn't counted again.

## 2026-11-09 annai: twoStep's optional parts in one options object
- Changed: `twoStep(label, confirmLabel, action, onArm)` became `twoStep(label, confirmLabel, action, { onArm, kept })` when home needed an armed Delete kept across its redraws (`kept`). The three callers that passed `onArm` (Plugins' Remove, a provider's Remove key and Remove provider) pass it by name.
- Why we had to change: a second optional part had nowhere to go but a fifth position, so a caller wanting only `kept` would pass `undefined` for `onArm`.
- Original design was wrong because: it gave its one optional argument its own position, as `PluginError` once did (2026-10-27): that lesson was written for errors and read as being about errors.
- Lesson: the 2026-10-27 rule holds for any function shared between modules, not only errors: after the required arguments, anything optional goes in one options object, even when there is only one so far.

## 2026-11-09 Reading another trip is granted by annai, not by her
- Changed: Kokoro gains `read_trip({ trip, path?, query? })` (trip-tools.js), read-only: another trip's name, dates, text files and last lines, one of its text or JSON files, or its conversation's lines with some words; photos and PDFs aren't opened. It is never Koe's, and never `task`'s helper's. It reads only once allowed: a `trip.grant` entry in this conversation's log (`{ trip, how: 'tap' | 'voice', always?: true }`, actor `user`) written after its last `import` entry, else the new app setting `readMyTrips` (Always) for a trip of the user's own, else annai's own card, logged as `out.ask` with `own: true`, annai's options (Allow, Always for my trips, Not now; no Always for an imported trip) and `tap`, annai's own random id for that card, which is what a tap on it answers (its `id` stays the call's, for the screens and the transcript; an older page's tap on it is an ordinary line). The loop gains `Agent.confirm`, which logs that card, says the question with voice on as a reminder is, and waits: a tap on its option, or a heard line that annai's word list (`spokenYes`, spoken-yes.js) takes for a yes in the 20 s after the question ends, dismissed or not. Both voice modes keep one settle per card (`#settleAsk`): each new or revised kept line starts its 3 s again (`STALL_MIN_MS`, the figure a live turn ends on), chatter set aside doesn't, a line not judged yet holds it, and then the newest kept line decides, else a yes set aside; the window counts from when a line was heard. In a Koe session Halves hands each heard line to `kokoro.confirmHeard`, and words still to come hold it. In classic voice each segment joins it once written down and past the gate, a segment being written down or gated holds it, and so does speech going on (the window's own hold); the window still closes the microphone at its end. A no carries every kept segment to her result, in order; kept segments an answer doesn't carry (before a yes, or when a tap, typing or a Stop came first) go on as heard lines, as one after the card would. In both voice modes a line set aside as chatter leaves it listening, and typing, a late tap on another card, or any other heard line kept as the user's ends it unanswered at any time once it has settled as the newest (classic voice hears only in the card's 20 s), its words following her result; so does a line typed, or in classic voice heard while she thought, before the card went up (fix pass 2). A tap before a heard line is judged answers it. Each read is a `trip.read` entry, annai's. A trip from a trip's file gets `imported: true` on its scope record (archive.js); an everything restore keeps what the record holds. `list_trips` gives the dated trips and the 30 newest chats, from the records as home's timeline reads them.
- Why we had to change: the user, about forgetting and the slow route of moving: "with user approval, shiori can just read another trip's files? ... so she just able to know what you are talking about without going to the other trip". A chat beside a happening trip had no way to know what was on tomorrow without the user going there.
- Original design was wrong because: trips were walled off completely (reach.js, `resolvePath`), and the only way across was moving. The wall is right for her own tools, so it stays; what was missing was a read that a person allows. `ask` can't be that approval: its question and options are hers, and a tap on "Allow" in a card a model wrote is that model's words coming back. `#waitFor` is private, and Kokoro's `ask` is gated into a note while Koe talks, so the card is annai's own method on the loop.
- Lesson: an approval comes only from something the app itself drew and checks, with its own words and options, never from text a model, a tool or a file can write, or from a plugin's message (`ui.shiori.send` is an ordinary line). A code plugin is another matter: it is trusted fully, with annai's own powers (docs/plugins.md), so a `trip.grant` it writes to the log counts, as its tap on the card would; a check of the entry's actor would guard nothing. What counts: a tap on the app's own card, or a heard line the app's own strict list matches (no substring, no model), judged once the user has stopped, on what they said last ("Yes." then "No, wait." is no). A late tap is a line like any other, and which card a tap answers is an id the app mints for that card, never one a provider wrote: a provider with no ids says `call_0` every turn, so a card left by a page that died would answer the next. A grant lives where it applies (this conversation's log), so a reload keeps it and nothing else inherits it. An approval brought in by an import is no approval: an archive's log can name this phone's ids (a Share names its trip's), so only one given after the log's last import counts. And what came from outside (a shared trip) is marked as it comes in, so a standing "always" can't reach it.

## 2026-11-10 Koe: a conversation with no dates has no plan to report, and a note ends Koe's own question
- Changed: Koe's view (`compactView`, the `<today>` block of Koe's chat tail and of a live session's start) says of a conversation with no dates that what is on, and where and when, is in the user's trips, which Kokoro reads, and leaves out "plan for …: nothing planned" and "bookings in the next 48 hours: none" there (rows it does hold are still listed). Koe's rule of what it may state adds that its `list` and `read` see only this conversation's files. A note from Kokoro now ends Koe's own `ask` waiting for a tap or typing, as it ends a `wait`: the result says so, and that the question is closed (the Conversation takes an ask's buttons once its call has a result), so Koe asks it again if it still matters. `tell_koe`'s description says Koe never sees `for_user`.
- Why we had to change: on the user's own setup, in a new chat, "What's on tomorrow?" got Koe's "Nothing planned for tomorrow, and no bookings" first in 3 of 3 runs, before Kokoro had read the trip happening now; 19 of 33 trip-reading steps began with a confident wrong statement, and in 3 the user ended wrong: Koe "corrected" Kokoro's right answer from its own empty folder, Kokoro's correction waited behind Koe's own "Want ideas for tomorrow?" until a tap that never came, and Kokoro told Koe to "give the summary in for_user", which Koe can't see.
- Original design was wrong because: the view was written for a trip, where an empty plan is a fact; a chat has no plan of its own, so "nothing planned" there was a claim about the user's other trips that nobody had checked. And a note waited only behind `wait`, though Koe's own question is a wait the user may never answer.
- Lesson: a summary block says what its source can't know, instead of an empty value that reads as "none". A wait on someone who may never answer yields to what must be said now.
- Extended (2026-11-11): with no Koe model set, a reminder that fell due while her turn waited on the user's answer (annai's card, "Read Kyoto?" or "Carry on in Kyoto?", or her own question with buttons, none of them timed) was queued behind that answer with its words: nothing showed or was said until they answered, and then she said it late. annai now takes the reminders held behind such a question, those queued for this conversation and those her own turn still owes, once only a tap or typing can answer it, and shows them and says them as any reminder of its own (`remind`: aloud with voice on); a wake that comes after that is taken as it arrives. The wait carries a one-way mark for it (`byHand`), set where no microphone listens for the answer: as her own question starts waiting for a tap (`#waitFor`), and on annai's card when its 20 s of listening shut, or straight after the microphone attempt when none opened (voice off, or it couldn't open). With voice off that is at once; with voice on, when the listening ends. An answer inside the listening takes the wake into her turn with its words, as before. A cue (a place, the road, an arrival, a stop off the plan) stays on its wake: it is hers to let pass. The queue for her next step took it that the step comes soon; a step that waits on the user may never come. The lesson above holds: what must be said now doesn't wait behind someone who may never answer. How it got there (the reviews of that change and a design check): the first build showed the reminder silently while a microphone listened for the answer or was about to, aloud otherwise, deciding from the microphone's state at whatever moment something called it (the wake's arrival, then every setting of the pending question, through a private accessor). Each round found a moment where that reading was wrong: a wake queued before the wait, the reminder her own turn owed, the moment before the microphone opened, and with voice on every one of them silent for good, since she was told the user had it; and it took cues as reminders. A mark that only goes one way, set where the listening ends, has no such moment, and no silent case is left. The real-model run found that the words she reads mattered too: with Opus as Shiori, 3 of 3 runs said the reminder again after the answer, though annai had shown it while the card waited. Her request held two lines about it: the reminder's own line, which gave the reason for a model that couldn't be reached ("since you couldn't be reached"), false here, and nothing about the user having it; and the wake's line, which still read as a watch that fired, with "Its reminder" and its words. The reminder's record now says it came due while a question to the user was waiting (`waiting`), and its line says why, that it was shown (and said aloud, when it was), that the user has it and there is nothing more to tell them about it; the wake carries `shown`, and its line, in the log and in the now block, ends "(annai has already shown the user its reminder.)". The cut turn's line ("since your turn ended without telling the user"), which she had not repeated in 0 of 3 runs, was the model for it. The wake's words themselves are not edited: what annai says about its own showing is a field and a sentence it adds. Lesson, extended: decide from a mark set once where a state ends, not from reading that state at the moment a caller happens to ask.

## 2026-11-10 list_trips gives each row when it was last talked in
- Changed: `list_trips`' TSV gains a last column, `last talked`: when the user last talked in that trip or chat, else when it was made, as ISO 8601 in the device's zone. It comes from the scope record through one helper (`talkedAt`, timeline.js), the value home places a chat by and annai's card names one by ("Read Hi, last talked Nov 6, 07:40 AM?"); the undated snapshot's latest conversations sort by it too. Its description lists the column, and `read_trip`'s says each trip read costs the user a tap: the likeliest first, another only if it lacks the answer, ask which when she can't tell.
- Why we had to change: "what did I say about ramen the other day?", with two chats named "Hi" talked in 4 and 9 days ago: Opus asked to read all five other trips and chats at once (five cards, 3 of 3), the local model the same over three replies. The cards told the two "Hi" apart, but only once she had asked for both; the list she chose from gave a chat its id and name alone.
- Original design was wrong because: the list gave her what a chat's row shows by name and left out what the screens use to tell two of one name apart, though the record already held it (2026-11-06's lesson was applied to the record, not to the list read from it).
- Lesson: a list a model chooses from carries what a person's screen tells its rows apart by, the same value from the same source, not only each row's name; and a tool whose every call costs the user an action says so where she reads it.

## 2026-11-10 a sleep beside a failed call doesn't end her turn, and a note to Koe after one isn't sent
- Changed: when a call in her reply failed (anything but `sleep` itself, and the notes between halves, `tell_koe` and `tell_kokoro`, whose failures have their own rules), the `sleep` in that reply doesn't run: its result says "Not asleep: a call in this reply failed (its error is above), so your turn goes on", and she has another step. Twice a turn at most: a reply that fails a third time ends it on its sleep. The sleep tool's description is unchanged. A `tell_koe` that comes after such a failed call in the same reply isn't sent either, while her turn goes on past that reply: its result is an error that says why ("Not sent to Koe: a call before it in this reply failed …"), so Kokoro still owes Koe its note, and tells it what holds in its next step. Past the bound her sleep ends the turn, and the note beside it is sent as written: a held note with no step after it reached no one, and Koe was told "Kokoro has nothing to add" (the review's probe: two failed writes in one turn).
- Why we had to change: Haiku sent a malformed `show` ("show needs a card") and `sleep` in one reply; the error came back to nobody, the turn ended, and the answer that was in the failed call never reached the screen. And in the released-app check, and again in this pass's rerun (2 of 2 all-Haiku hand-overs), Haiku wrote a booking and told Koe "Filed …" in one reply; the write failed, Koe said "filed" (once "confirmed"), and a correction followed a few seconds later. Rewording Kokoro's rule ("tell it again once a tool's result says it's done") didn't move Haiku.
- Original design was wrong because: `sleep` was a decision written before the reply's results were known, and the loop took it as final even when a result said the reply hadn't done what it was for; a note written in the same reply as a call is the same kind of plan, and Koe says it as fact. A first version without the once-a-turn bound ran the test process out of memory: a scripted reply of [failing show, speak, sleep], repeated, never made three identical calls in a row, which is all the stuck check looks for.
- Lesson: an end-of-work signal sent in the same batch as the work is a plan, not a result: when a step of the batch fails, the end waits for the one who sent it to see the failure. Bound every retry a failure buys, by a count, not by spotting repeats.

## 2026-11-10 annai: a provider's rule for the sequence it is sent is enforced where the request is built, over whatever is stored
- Changed: anthropic-messages 0.5.1 (`settle`, index.js) builds each request so that a reply which called a server tool (Anthropic's web search or fetch) beside one of annai's tools is sent only in a shape the API takes: when the user message after it holds the tool results alone, the request runs the search and has no tail, and that message goes the same way in every later request; when it holds anything else (the user's line or Koe's, a photo, a heard line), the call is left out of its reply and the message goes as stored, so the model reads it all at once and searches again if it still wants to (one search either way: the call left out never ran). A call that no reply after it answers is left out of its reply. Nothing waits or moves: a first version that held the other blocks back one reply left a line typed in that step unanswered, and after a stopped or failed step it sent the user's next question nowhere in its turn. No stored format changed; the same function serves a conversation the released version had stuck. provider-anthropic 1.2.2: Haiku 5.5 gets its `max` level, which the API takes.
- Why we had to change: the real check (2026-11-09) found that every such reply left the conversation stuck. The API runs the server tool when the next request holds only the client tools' results, and the reply after begins with its result; annai put the now block and other text in that message, the API refused it ("`web_search` tool use ... was found without a corresponding `web_search_tool_result` block"), annai read the 400 as refused reasoning and asked again without it, and every later turn failed on the same message. What the API accepts, asked with real requests (Opus, Sonnet and Haiku 5.5, web search and web fetch alike): only tool results after the reply, at the end of the history and in the middle of it, though the next reply carries the result; text inside the tool result is taken but is the wrong role for the user's own words; a reply after it that holds no result (another model's, Koe's) is refused whatever the user message holds; the same history without the call is taken, with the reply's thinking kept.
- Original design was wrong because: the 2026-10-30 lesson kept the provider's reply as it came and made requests append-only, but took the rest of the sequence for free: annai's own text goes wherever the transcript puts it. A provider that runs a tool itself checks the sequence round the call as well as the reply.
- Lesson: when a provider defers a call it makes itself, the rule for what may follow is part of the contract; keep it in the one place that builds the request, working from whatever is stored, so a fix reaches the conversations already stuck and no stored format changes. Ask the real API for every shape (the end of the history, the middle, and something other than the answering reply after it) before choosing, and give the stand-in the same refusals.

## 2026-11-10 annai: the card hears words where they first exist, and no model's verdict decides it
- Changed: annai's card for `read_trip` judges a heard line when its words are written down, by its own lists only. With Koe on a live model, live.js hands the card each line's words as the session's transcript of it arrives (`LiveHost.heard`: the line's id, its words, when it began, whether it began over her voice), through Halves to `kokoro.confirmHeard`; the line's `heard` entry, written when Koe's turn completes, now brings only Koe's verdict, under the same id. In classic voice the card judges a line when `writeDown` answers. A yes (`spokenYes`) begun inside the 20 s window, or a no (`spokenNo`, new: plain refusals and take-backs in the same languages) at any time, arms its option; the newest decides `SETTLE_S` after its words (3 s, now its own number with its reason, no longer `STALL_MIN_MS`): a yes grants Allow, a no declines as Not now (`how: 'voice'`). Any other line drops an armed yes at once, whatever a model later makes of it. A line begun over her voice arms nothing (`LiveInput.overHer`: half-duplex's own rule, now noted on every route; classic voice closes the microphone while the question is said, as for her own spoken ask). Only ending the card unanswered still reads the verdict: a kept line that arms nothing ends it SETTLE_S after its verdict, unless a listed word is armed. Gone: the settle's choice of "the newest kept line, else a yes set aside", "a line not judged yet holds it", and speech going on holding the settle.
- Why we had to change: the real-voice check (2026-11-10). Gemini's transcript of a line is whole at the socket 0.9 s after the user stops (315 of 315 lines, one chunk, never revised), but annai wrote the `heard` entry only at Koe's turn completion: median 0.8 s later, p90 5.7 s, and for 5.4% of lines (17% of short replies like "Yes.") only at the session's close. The card waited for the entry: a spoken yes was lost; "Yes." then "No, wait." was granted 3 of 3, the settle firing before the second entry existed; the 20 s window was judged at the entry. The kept/chatter verdict was late and wrong for exactly these lines: "No, wait." and "No thanks." set aside as chatter (the trip read after a take-back; the card and Shiori hung 45 s), a bare "Yes." set aside so an earlier kept "No." outvoted it.
- Original design was wrong because: the card was fed from the log, which is where the words are *recorded*, not where they first *exist*; the record's timing is set by Koe's turn, which has nothing to do with the user's words. And it let a model's verdict choose between lines (a kept line outranked one set aside), so a judgment made for another purpose (whether Koe should answer) decided a grant, late and for the wrong reason.
- Lesson: a rule that reads words must be fed where the words first exist, not where some other step writes them down later; if two copies of the same words exist, find the shared identity (here the line's id) and pair them by it rather than making a second one. A model's verdict is not an input to a gate: a gate decides from what it can check itself (its own lists, its own clock), and a model's opinion may at most decide what happens when the gate's own check finds nothing (here: end the wait unanswered), never cause a grant or block one.
- Extended the same day (the final review of the merged branch): the rebuild applied its own lesson to two paths of three, and took out one hold too many. (1) Koe on a chat model hears through its own microphone, and Halves still stamped each of its lines "begun now" when Koe's entry was written, behind a quick-decision gate of 3 to 15 s: an "Okay" said to Koe before annai asked granted the read. Koe's microphone now tells the card when each segment's speech began (`Half.speech`), and the card has the segment written down as it is heard (a Koe model that takes the sound, Gemini's, writes nothing down until its turn ends: the real-speech check saw a spoken "Sure." go to Koe's `wait` as sound and never reach the card), classic voice judges a segment by its own begin time rather than when it is routed behind another line's gate call, and an entry alone carries no time on any path. (2) Speech going on is evidence too: words arrive 0.3 to 1 s after the speaker stops, so a take-back longer than what was left of the settle came too late and the trip was read while the user was still taking it back. A yes now waits while anyone speaks (`Agent.confirmSpeech`, fed by both microphones and a live session's turn edges) and has `SETTLE_S` again once they stop; a no never waits. With them: listed words said to the card are written with its answer, in the order said, never queued to her as a newer line; where annai hears while she speaks, a run begun within 360 ms after her voice is hers, quiet or not (her voice comes back late there), and a line marked hers doesn't end the card.
- Lesson, extended: feed the rule where the words first exist **on every path that carries them**: list the paths (here three: a live session's transcript, classic voice's write-down, a chat Koe's microphone) and check each, because a path left on the old feed keeps the old bug. "Where they first exist" includes when they began: a time stamped where the words are recorded is as late as the record. And a rule that acts on words waits while more words are being said, since they may undo it; only the side that is safe to act on (here a no) may skip the wait.
- Extended again (the re-review of that fix, the same day): the chat Koe's line now had two copies of its words, Kokoro's write-down and Koe's own at its turn's end, and the card judged whichever arrived, in arrival order, and judged a line again when its words changed. A yes written down after its take-back, or Koe's later "Yes!" for Kokoro's "Yes.", armed after "No, wait." and granted the trip the user took back. The newest line by when it was heard now decides; a line's late or rewritten words change nothing once a newer line has words. Two more from the same review: the write-down went to Kokoro's model, which may take no sound (Claude), so Koe · Chat's model was made to write Koe's line down (taken back out the same day: see below); and a tap ending her tail at once let her echo, still on its way back, count as the user's, so a tap ends it only once her voice has played long enough (690 ms) for any echo to be back already (taken out after the last review: see below).
- Lesson, extended again: order events by when they happened, never by when their record arrived. When the same thing can be reported twice, or late, or in other words, give it its time where it happens (here the line's place among the lines heard) and let a late or second report fill in, never reorder. And a fix that adds a second source of the same facts adds a second arrival order: check the rule against both.
- Taken back out (the third round, the same day): with Koe · Chat's model writing the card's line down, real speech granted "Sure." 9.5 s after it was said instead of 4.1 s, since "Send audio to the model" comes first in the list and so a full turn of Koe's chat model wrote the line down instead of the transcription model listed after it; Koe's own turn, finding no transcript kept, wrote it down again; and once that turn hit Koe's 8 s first-output limit, which rested Koe's only model, so Koe went quiet and nothing was granted. The setup it was for (no transcription model at all, Kokoro on a model that takes no sound) turned out narrow: adding Gemini lists two transcription models, and with none Kokoro can't hear a spoken question anyway; there the yes was heard late, when Koe's turn ended, not never.
- Lesson: a fix for a rare setup that changes the path the common setup takes is measured on the common setup too, with real speech, before it ships; the rare case gets a fallback behind the common path, not a new first step in front of it.
- Extended once more (the last review, the same day): a yes settled 3 s after its words whatever was still coming, so a take-back said after it whose words came later was read over: a model asked to write a line down took 0.9, 5.7 and 7.0 s in real speech, and a live session's words for a turn can come seconds after it ends. The yes now waits for the words of every line heard after it, and a live session hands the card a turn's line as it begins (`LiveHost.speech(on, line)`, and `confirmHeard({ …line, live: true })`, which starts no write-down; both are additions). The same review showed the re-review's tap exception giving her words a way back in: after a tap 0.69 s into her voice, her last word before it, coming back as a run of its own, was taken for the user's and could answer the card. It came out: a run begun in her tail is hers, tap or no tap, and an answer begun within 360 ms of a tap on headphones or in a car arms nothing.
