- Original design was wrong because: `ask` kept its own list of the answers it knew, written before a microphone could answer a question that waits by hand; the wait tool's fix for the same tap (a3b4836e) added the heard answer there and not here.
- Lesson: a tool that waits for the user reads every kind of answer through one reader, so a new way to answer is added once, not to each tool in turn.

## 2026-11-12 A tap's listening has an end
- Changed: a tap's listening for a question that waits by hand (her `ask`, with voice off) gets the silence limit a listening has, `LISTEN_S` (8 s), held while someone speaks (`#limitTap`, through the pending's `hold` and `arm`, as `#listenFor`'s): at its end the microphone closes, the dock says the question waits for a tap or typing again, and Kokoro's note for the user (`#noted`) is no longer held behind it. A second tap listens again. No stored format changed.
- Why we had to change: found by reading the code and reproduced in unit tests; not seen on a phone. With voice off and Koe asking with buttons, a tap on Shiori marked the question as listening, with no time of its own: her turn is busy, so the tap's quiet timer never ran, and `#noted` leaves a listening question alone. Kokoro's note waited until the user answered, which may never happen, and the microphone stayed open with nothing said.
- Original design was wrong because: `#tapListen` marked a pending as listening without giving that listening an end, where every other listening (`#listenFor`, annai's card) brings its own.
- Lesson: whatever marks a wait as listening gives that listening its own end, since other rules (a note ends a wait, the quiet timer) read "listening" as "an answer is coming soon".
- Changed again, after the review of this change (2026-11-12), which found three ways the limit still failed: the limit runs from the tap, whatever opened the microphone; a noise neither ends it nor restarts it; and whatever ends a listening lets `#noted` look again. (1) a tap that cut her off while she said her question aloud opened a microphone owned by 'tap', and `#askAloud`, when its own 8 s ended, closed only a 'turn' microphone and left that one open while the question waited by hand: it now closes the microphone whatever opened it. (2) `#limitTap`'s 8 s started in full again on every noise that came and went (a click, phone dictation's speech with no words), so in a noisy room the microphone stayed open and Kokoro's note stayed held: each tap now has a fixed deadline (`by` on the pending), `arm` waits only for what is left of it, and `hold` still stops the clock while someone speaks; her question aloud (`#listenFor`) keeps its own deadline. (3) voice going off during a tap's listening (the chip, or the earbuds taken out) closed the microphone and ended the listening but never let `#noted` look again, so a note that came meanwhile stayed held: `#voiceChanged` now calls `#noted()` after it. All three found by the review's probes on the test stand-in, not seen on a phone. The idle tap window (`#armQuiet`) still restarts on noise, and a live session taking the microphone mid-speech can leave a listening marked: both are in `docs/limitations.md` as deferred.
- Lesson (added): a limit counts from the moment its listening was asked for, not from whatever last happened on the microphone and not from what happened to open it; and anything that ends a listening hands the rules that read "listening" a chance to look again, since they were waiting on it.
- Changed a third time, after the second opinion on the last build (2026-11-12), which found two more places the fixes did not reach. (1) annai's card: its `shut` (the end of its 20 s) closed only a 'turn' microphone, so a tap that cut the card's question, whose microphone the card then listened with, left it open while the dock said "Waiting for your OK"; and a tap while the card already waited by hand (its window over) opened a microphone with no limit at all, which silence kept open until a button, typing, voice off or another conversation. `shut` now closes whichever microphone is open, and `#tapListen` runs `#limitTap` for the card once its own window is over (`byHand`): the card's `arm` waits for the later of its window's end and the tap's 8 s (`by`, 0 until a tap), so a spoken yes still counts only inside its window (`askSaid`, `inside`). (2) Koe's listening after it spoke: `#noted` took a note into a wait only while the wait was `listening`, and voice going off clears that flag, so a note that came after the chip went off waited for the listening's silence limit (20 s by default) while the dock said "Your turn": the rule is now `kind === 'wait'`, and no other wait pending ever has `listening` off. Both reproduced on the test stand-in (unit tests that failed before), not seen on a phone. Not changed: the wait tool's fallback after a refused microphone. A tap's microphone there closes with the tap window's own rule (the turn's end, then 8 s of quiet, measured 8 s after the wait's end), as a microphone that listened for a wait does; a wait's time is not its end.
- Lesson (added again): when a close or a rule is keyed on who opened a resource (the owner label) or on a flag one path clears (`listening`), a path that reaches the same state another way slips past it; key it on what the thing is now (is a microphone open; is this a wait), and look for each place that ends or reads the state, not only the one the last report named.

## 2026-11-12 Heard whole is all made and all played
- Changed: the realtime contract (docs/plugins.md, "Realtime voice protocols") gains an event, `{ type: 'made' }`, that a protocol emits once the turn being made is all made (its sound, words and tool calls all sent), and a field of the type, `made: true`, that says it emits it (a live session's `made`). Gemini Live 0.5.0 emits it on `generationComplete`, which annai ignored before; OpenAI Realtime 0.6.0 on a `response.done` that wasn't cancelled. Live Koe decides "heard whole" in one place (`LiveKoe.#whole`), for a tap, a close, a drop and the user's turn beginning: all of it played (past her last loud sample, see the last change below), its sound begun, and, with a type that says, all of it made (the event, or the turn's end). Before, all played was enough at a tap, a close and a drop, and the user's turn beginning showed every note not yet passed on. A turn whose tool call was answered silently ends once it is all made, its sound playing out, where it waited for its turnComplete or 2 s past her sound (`#endLater`): that turnComplete never came for 19 of 121 real `tell_kokoro` turns, and until then the dock said she was speaking and Kokoro's note waited. A type without `made: true` keeps those rules. An annai from before ignores the event and the field (`#on` and `openLive` pass over what they don't know), so the plugins keep `api` 1.
- Why we had to change: her turn counted as said only at the provider's turnComplete, which on real Gemini came 0.19 to 0.63 s after her last audible sound (2026-11-12, 113 notes; 0.06 to 0.65 s in the runs before; about when annai's player queue empties, her sound ending in a quiet tail of 0.2 to 0.6 s): a user answering in the window between her sound's having all played and that end got the note again as a silent card ("Shiori's answer"), though they had heard it whole. A user answering in the quiet tail still did, as her sound was still queued then; the last change below closes that. And all played alone, at a tap or a close, counted a note as heard while the provider was still making it (a pause in her sound: 7 of 204 real replies, up to 1.5 s), so its rest was neither heard nor shown (since 2026-11-12 at a close too).
- Original design was wrong because: annai read one provider signal, the turn's end, for two facts, "all of it is made" and "the user has heard all of it", and where the end hadn't come it stood in the player's empty queue, which proves neither: a pause still being made empties it too. The provider already sent the first fact on its own (235 of 235 turns), never inside a pause (0 of 107), and annai dropped it in the plugin.
- Lesson: keep a provider's "made" and the player's "played" apart, and need both for "heard whole"; neither is the turn's end, and a user's cut can come after "made" (3 of 5). Read what the protocol already sends before inferring it from timing. A new signal in a contract is declared by the type that sends it, so a host reads a type that doesn't declare as "never sends", not "hasn't yet"; an event an older host doesn't know is ignored there, so adding one needs no newer `api`.
- Changed again, after the review of this change (2026-11-12), which found these in it (found by reading the code and by probes on the test stand-in; not seen on a phone):
  - A turn that goes on after a tool call annai answers without `silent` (a read such as `watch_list`) counted as all made at the provider's `made`. Gemini sends generationComplete right after the call (0 to 17 ms in the measured silent ones), before annai's answer, so it can't know yet that more comes. A user speaking while she waited for the tool's result got no card for the note she had begun to say, and it was neither shown nor said; before this change the card came. `#whole` now counts `made` only for a turn that doesn't go on (`goesOn`); until the provider ends it, the user's turn beginning or a tap is a cut, and the note shows. The contract's wording (`LiveEvent`'s `made`, docs/plugins.md "All made", spec §10.8) says that after such a call more of the turn comes.
  - A note that went in before the provider's turnComplete for a turn annai had ended at `made` makes Gemini end that turn with `interrupted` and then its turnComplete 20 ms later (19 of 19 real turns). `#on` took the `interrupted` for the answer to the note: the note's 8 s wait ended, so a session that then said nothing was never asked again, and the cut stub it left as the trail dropped the first words of a reply that came before its sound. An early-ended turn's own late end, either one, now answers nothing when no turn of hers is going on. The stand-in sends that `interrupted` and turnComplete when a text turn meets a turn with no end yet (it sent nothing before, so no test ran this path), and a turn can send its first words before its first sound (`saidFirst`).
  - A line counted heard whole was written by its share of her sound's length, so one whose sound is not a whole number of ms (nearly every real reply: the player's heard ms is rounded, and a byte a chunk ends on is dropped) lost its last word, with no ✂. `#said` now writes a turn heard whole (`whole`, with a stream) whole; a turn that waited and was never heard keeps its share, so the user going on still lets it go unwritten.
  - A tap stopped only the turn that sounded, and `#said` then played the turn queued behind it, so she went on talking after the user stopped her (also before this branch). `interrupt()` now takes the turns waiting behind (`#unwait`, as `#cut` does at a close) and writes them as her answer, silently, after the line of the one it stopped.
  - Docs and a test said the duplicate "Shiori's answer" card was gone, and the Deferred entry was deleted. It is gone only for an answer after her sound has all played and before the provider's end: real Gemini's sound ends in a quiet tail (0.2 to 0.6 s) that the player still holds, and the 0.19 to 0.63 s was measured from the audible end. The entry is back, rewritten (the window is the tail); the spec, docs/plugins.md and the "Why we had to change" above say so; the test that passed with a stand-in whose sound had no tail now says what it tests, and a test with the real shape pins today's behaviour (until the next change).
  - The real Gemini run (2026-11-12, 222 note replies, headphones) measured the quiet tail: 50 of 50 answers begun in it, as her last word ended, got the note again as a silent card and a ✂ line; the tail is a median 300 ms (p95 about 360) of her sound's end, after the last 20 ms frame at -40 dBFS rms or above. Heard whole is now all made and played past that frame (`src/core/koe/loud.js`; `#whole`): the end of the last loud frame is kept per turn as the chunks arrive (a frame of 20 ms per chunk, as measured; chunks are almost always a multiple of 480 samples), and compared with how much was played once her stream is stopped (`#whole` stops it: each caller cuts her next). Any level from -50 to -34 dBFS gave the same result on the 93 answers and 40 cuts (54 duplicate cards removed; none of the 28 real cuts and none of the 35 noise cuts counted whole; -30 begins to count real cuts whole), so -40 was picked for the last soft syllable: it keeps 4 of the 5 quiet final consonant bursts measured (a released final d at -35 to -39 dBFS) as sound, where -34 would count them past her last loud sample, so a doubt errs toward the card, not toward a lost word. The analysis' own recipe (the level 6 dB over the tail's peak) chose -34; the frames at -41 to -46 dBFS in the tail are her own fades and bursts, not noise, since the audio is the model's, not the room's. Not covered, and the cost of any level: a last syllable under -40 dBFS that the user begins in counts as heard (they heard all but its fade). The Deferred entry for the duplicate card is removed. Those figures were computed from the earlier build's records; the rule as built then ran on real Gemini (2026-11-12, 36 answers aimed at a note's end): 0 duplicate cards in the 24 begun after her last loud frame, ✂ and the card in all 12 begun with loud sound left, and no line written whole with a loud last word cut.
  - Changed again, after the second review (2026-11-12): a turn that goes on after a call she speaks after (`goesOn`) was counted heard whole once the provider ended it and her sound had played past its last loud sample, which `#whole` says and which writes her line whole; but its three note-passing callers (the user's start, a tap, a close) took that as the note passed on, while `#said` already asked `!t.goesOn`. A start, a tap or a close in its quiet tail, after the provider's end, passed the note with no card, and when the user's question dropped the continuation, the note was lost (a probe on the stand-in; about 1 to 2% of starts aimed at the end of such words on real timings). The four sites now ask one method, `#passes(t, whole)`: heard whole and no call she speaks after. `#whole` is unchanged, so her line is still written whole.
  - Also after the second review: a turn with no loud frame at all (`end` stayed 0) was heard whole as soon as it was all made, wherever her sound was. With no last loud sample to play past, the end is now the whole length of her sound (`loudMs`), so it is heard whole once played through. None of the 222 real replies had no loud frame (her level ran -25 to -11 dBFS); it is a guard for a reply of near-silence.
  - Lesson (added): a stand-in that leaves out what the real provider does at an edge (here, ending the turn a note meets) leaves that path to the first real run: give it the measured behaviour for every input it can get. A provider's "all made" is made only as far as the provider knows: where annai owes an answer (a call it answers aloud), more follows.

## 2026-11-12 One input at a time
- Changed: live Koe (`src/core/koe/live.js`) has one input in flight. `#idle` sends nothing new while a note for the user that went in is not passed on, or while an answer is due: to a text annai or the user put in (a note, a trigger, a recap, the ask once more, a typed line), to the user's spoken turn until she answers it (a tool call included), or to a call she speaks after. When it is free, one note goes in, not all that wait. A call she speaks after is an input: annai's answer starts the same 8-second wait (`#due.more`), and with nothing back she is asked once more, which has its own 8 seconds before annai shows the note and says it. All made ends a turn whose calls are answered in the same tick (`#endNow`), so her continuation is a turn of its own by construction, not by a 0 ms timer. An `interrupted` never answers anything. Cutting in is one method (`#cutIn`) for the user's spoken start, every line they send and annai's urgent reminder; Kokoro's own immediate note replaces the note in flight. A tap keeps her stopped until the next input (`#stopped`): what she makes meanwhile is written, never spoken. The realtime contract's words in docs/plugins.md ("All made") follow; no plugin changes, no stored format changes. The user's spoken turn lost its exception: a note at their turn's end used to go in at once (spec §7.1), and now waits for her answer to them.
- Why we had to change: on real Gemini (2026-11-12, 60 questions with a `list` call and a second note 0.2 to 1.2 s after it), the second note went in before her continuation for the first, and her one whole line passed both on: a note was neither said nor shown in 17 of 60. The wait for a continuation belonged to nobody (2 of 124 real calls never got one, and the dock said "speaking" until the user spoke), a stray `interrupted` ended the wait for her reply, and the three rounds of fixes before this all landed in that state.
- Original design was wrong because: annai can't tell which note a line of hers says, and Gemini gives turns no ids, yet several inputs were in flight under one "passed on" mark, so the first whole line passed whatever had gone in. "Between turns" counted as free, though an answer was still owed, and a continuation was a turn of its own only by timing.
- Lesson: when replies carry no id and you can't read which request they answer, keep one request in flight: the first whole reply after it is its answer by exclusion, and every wait is that one input's, bounded by its measured time (8 s: 6.4 s was the slowest of 123 real continuations). An answer a model owes after a tool call is an input like any other. Take cutting-in paths through one method, so a new way to cut in can't skip the rule.

## 2026-11-12 A model's word that it did a thing settles nothing alone
- Changed (fix round 1 of the one-input-at-a-time build, after the review and the real Gemini run): `already_told` no longer settles a note by itself: live Koe shows the note on a silent card and passes it on (the user's pick "Show it silently"; the `#resent` mark that guarded only notes sent again is gone). A whole line of hers that is exactly one of annai's own words to her (the tools she calls without speaking, and `silent`, a tool answer's scheduling) counts as no answer: she is asked once more, then annai shows and says the note (the user's pick "Count it as no answer"). Kokoro's immediate note drops only Kokoro's own notes in flight, and only once it goes in (`#cutIn(…, replaces)`); annai's reminder in flight is cut in on as by any cut-in, a silent card unless she said it whole. A tap that stops her gives the user the floor first (`#holdForUser`, as after a tap that opens a session), and while she is stopped annai asks nothing aloud (`#silent`).
- Why we had to change: the real run (2026-11-12, 21 sessions of three notes at once) lost 2 of 63 notes with no card and no error. After saying note 1, she called `already_told`, which counted note 2 as passed on; in the other, her whole line for note 2 was the word "silent", the scheduling word annai sends beside a silent tool answer, which took it as her answer. The review found a third: `note()` emptied the whole list of notes in flight for Kokoro's immediate note, annai's own reminder with them, before it knew the note could go in.
- Original design was wrong because: it took what she did (a call, a line of words) as proof that the user had the note, and a model's reply decided what annai owed, which no rule allows; and the drop for Kokoro's immediate note was written as "the in-flight list is cleared", not as "Kokoro's own notes are dropped", so it took with it what it was never meant to.
- Lesson: a model's report that it did something is a claim. Let it settle what the program owes the user only where a wrong claim costs a duplicate (a card for something already heard), never a loss; and a rule that drops an item names what it drops, once the thing that replaces it is in, never empties a list that other kinds share.

## 2026-11-12 A reminder ends Koe's wait
- Changed: with Koe on a chat model, a wake that carries a reminder or a place alert (a fallback, a cue or not) ends Koe's `wait` as a note from Kokoro does, and one already queued when the wait begins, or while the model call that asks for it runs, ends it at once (`#cameDue`, src/core/agent/loop.js). The wait's result then reads, in new words Koe's model reads: "A reminder came due: say it (below). Kokoro's note is still to come.", or for an alert alone "An alert came (below): say it if it helps them now, or let it pass. Kokoro's note is still to come."; the wake follows it as before. Koe's speak listening and its own question, and the waits of one Shiori and of Kokoro, are as they were.
- Why we had to change: Koe says "One moment.", passes the user's line on and waits for Kokoro. With Kokoro's turn held by annai's card, a reminder due then was said only when Koe's wait ran out: 89 s late on the stand-in with a wait of 120 s, 569 s with 600, while Kokoro was told that Koe was saying it. In real runs Koe chose 30 s or 60 s for 54 of its 57 waits. Built once on 2026-11-12 (5f717b2d) and taken out (569e30c8), because a reminder said inside the card's 20 s drew an "Okay." that answered the card; it is back with the rule that her voice beginning anything else ends the card's listening (the user's pick, 2026-11-12, "Anything else ends it": "Reminders and alerts are said on time"), so a place alert ends the wait too.
- Original design was wrong because: the wait ended only for what it was waiting for (the note), not for what must not wait behind it. 2026-11-10's lesson, that a wait on someone who may never answer yields to what must be said now, was applied to Koe's question with buttons and not to its wait.
- Lesson: a wait ends for anything that must be said now, not only for what it waits for; its result says what came and what is still to come, so the model says it and can wait again.

## 2026-11-12 The card listens only while its question is the last thing put to the user
- Changed: annai's card ("Read <trip>?", "Carry on in <trip>?") ends its listening for a spoken answer the moment her voice begins anything else: a line said through the speaker (annai's own, a chat model's, Koe's), or a live session's turn (`Voice.onBegin`, told by `Speaker.begin` and by `LiveKoe`'s `#play`; `over` in `confirm`, src/core/agent/loop.js). The window's end moves to that moment and the listening shuts as at its 20 s, so a yes heard after it arms nothing and a kept line ends the card unanswered, with the words that tell her what to do if they meant yes. A question that ends while something is already queued behind it or a live turn still sounds (`LiveKoe.sounding`) opens no window. A yes armed before her voice began still settles; a line begun after it inside its 3 s drops it. The listener lives for the card's whole life, so a window a tap starts again is covered. With one Shiori (no Koe model, or Koe down) and voice on, what annai would say itself while the card listens on her own microphone (quiet hours, past the hourly limit on wakes) waits for that listening to end, then is said; on a Stop it is shown only. A cue (a place, the road, an arrival, a stop off the plan) behind annai's card is now shown, and said with voice on, once the card's listening is over, as a reminder; behind her own question it stays hers.
- Why we had to change: the card took any yes-word heard in its 20 s as its answer, whoever the user meant it for. A reminder said in that time (live voice at once, about 50 ms after it came due on the stand-in; a chat Koe after a wait that ran out inside the window, or within a second once its turn had ended; annai itself in quiet hours) drew an "Okay." that read the trip, or opened it and moved their files. A hold on what is said was tried first and taken out (569e30c8): it reached two of the eight ways something gets said there and kept producing findings. The user picked "Anything else ends it": "Reminders and alerts are said on time; once anything else is said, a spoken yes no longer answers the card. It stays up for a tap, and a yes said anyway makes Shiori ask again." For one Shiori they kept the 2026-11-11 pick, "Hold it until the yes", since nothing else would ask again there.
- Original design was wrong because: the card's window was a span of time, when what makes a yes the card's answer is that the card's question is what it answers. Time was a stand-in for "nothing else was said since", and every new path that says something (a reminder, an alert, a live turn) broke it.
- Lesson: an answer heard by voice counts only while its question is the last thing said; end the listening at the one event every path shares (her voice beginning), rather than holding back each thing that might be said.
- Changed again, after the first review (2026-11-12): "said" was only what sounds, so what is only shown slipped through. (1) A voice-off tap reopened the card's listening after a reminder was said into it, or after Koe's own question was shown; a yes meant for that granted the read, or opened the trip and copied their line. The one signal is now `cardPassed` (`Agent`, with `Half.passed` carrying Koe's half to Kokoro's card): raised where either half writes a line or question for the user after the card (`#write`), and where a reminder is written (`remind`); her voice beginning anything (`Voice.onBegin`) raises the same. It ends the listening, and drops the card's `start`, so no tap starts it again. (2) With voice off the unsaid card opened its 20 s when it appeared, so a voice that came on within them, with a chat Koe's microphone open, granted a yes with no tap and no question: it opens at a tap only. (3) A question whose sound failed (blocked, no voice) was treated as said: it takes the unsaid path now. (4) One Shiori's hold: a reminder due in a tapped window (voice off) waits too (it was said at once and closed the microphone), it is let go at the window's own 20 s whatever speech seems to go on (it waited for ever when a speech start had no end, 125 s and 610 s on the stand-in), and after the card's answer is written, so the user's "Yes." comes first in the conversation.
- Lesson (added): a window for a spoken answer has two ends, one by time and one by what is put to the user after its question; the second must be one signal raised at every place something reaches the user (said or shown), not a list of the ways something gets said. A hold has the same bound as the window it holds for, set where the window is, not by an event that may never come (a speech end).
- Changed again, after the second review (2026-11-12): (1) something shown, not said, while the card's question was still queued or sounding was dropped: `over` found no window to end, and `confirm`'s `.then` then opened one by its own rule. `cardPassed(shown)` now also marks the card (`ask.passed`), and `.then` opens no window, and no tap start, for a marked card. The mark comes only from what is shown without voice: a voiced line's write isn't marked (its sound's beginning tells the card through `onBegin`, and a line whose sound began before the question, which queued behind it, would otherwise kill a card whose question really is the last thing said). It isn't set in `over`, since the card's own `say` fires `onBegin` synchronously. (2) A question cut by voice going off while it was said counted as said: `.then` takes the unsaid path when voice is off by then (not keyed on a cut, which a tap makes with voice on, and the user's pick keeps that tap answering it). (3) A notice that asks the user something (it has actions: "Agree", "Try again") raises the same signal in `#notice`; a status notice doesn't (a ruling of the main session, not yet put to the user). (4) The microphone the card opens for its listening is closed when that listening ended while it opened. (5) `Halves.tap`'s exclusion of a tap that cut something went: inert, since a cut only stops what was said after the card, which dropped its start already.
- Lesson (added): an event that ends a window must also be remembered for a window that has not opened yet, or the window opens by its own rule afterwards. Mark it where the event is raised, not where the window's own start fires.

## 2026-11-12 A tap starts the card's 20 s, and the dock shows it
- Changed: with annai's card's question unsaid (voice off when it appeared), a tap on Shiori that listens starts the card's 20 s at the tap, as her question's end does aloud, and each later such tap starts them again (`tapCard`, called from `Halves.tap` with the tap's moment, whichever half's microphone hears). With her two halves the microphone is the talking half's (a chat Koe's), and it listens until those 20 s end (`cardUntil`, through the half's hook), not only its own 8 s. While a tap's microphone is open for what her turn waits on (her wait, her question, annai's card past its own listening), the dock says "Listening", and when it closes it says "Your turn" or "Waiting for your OK" again (`#tapEnded`); a tapped wait with voice off gets the 8 s a tapped question has (`#limitTap`). A second tap while that microphone is open, or someone is speaking into it, closes it only: the wait, her question or the card stays, no Stop runs, and words being recorded are routed to it first (phone dictation's are lost: its close aborts them).
- Why we had to change: with voice off the card's 20 s counted from when it appeared, so a tap late in them and a yes after them got "Not read" although the microphone was open. The user picked "A tap starts the 20 s": "Your tap is the moment you chose to answer, as her question ending is with voice on; a yes after the tap counts." And a tap during a voice-off wait opened the microphone for 8 s while the dock kept saying "Your turn": the user picked "Show listening": "While that tap's microphone is open the dock says it's listening, as it does elsewhere, and goes back to 'Your turn' when the 8 s end; a second tap closes the microphone, like any tap while she listens." Read as written, a tap while she listens would Stop her turn, which here is the wait itself.
- Original design was wrong because: the window was tied to the question's end even when nothing said the question, and `#tapListen` returned before the dock's state whenever her turn was busy, which it always is while she waits on the user.
- Lesson: a window for an answer starts at the user's moment to answer (her question heard, or their tap when it wasn't), and a tap on a listening the user opened ends that listening, not what it listens for.
- Changed again, after the first review (2026-11-12): the tap starts the card's 20 s only while the card is the last thing put to the user (the user's pick, "Only while the card is last"): once a reminder was said or shown, or Koe asked its own question, `confirm`'s `over` drops `ask.start` and a tap opens nothing for the card; the card waits for a tap on Allow or a yes after she asks again. The card also opens no window when it appears: with its question unsaid it waits for a tap (it had opened one at once, which a chat Koe's microphone could use). A dictation result lost at a second tap: the recorder's close flushes the words being said and routes them to the card first, but phone dictation's close aborts them (the earlier text said both were routed).

## 2026-11-12 The card listens in the plain case only
- Changed: annai's card ("Read <trip>?", "Carry on in <trip>?") opens its listening for a spoken yes in one place: the end of its own question in `confirm` (src/core/agent/loop.js), only when voice was on, the question was said to its end (its sound neither failed nor was cut by a tap), voice is still on, and nothing else was said or shown since the question began. Nothing opens it again. Removed: `Agent.tapCard` and `Agent.cardUntil`, the `Half.cardUntil` hook and `Halves.tap`'s call to `tapCard` (a voice-off tap started the card's 20 s, and Koe's tapped microphone listened to them); the pending card's `start`, and its reads in `#limitTap` and `#armQuiet` (a tap's microphone is the ordinary 8 s again); one Shiori's hold (`ask.behind`, `#sayBehind`, `#cardListens`, the hold in `remind`, the `finally` in `confirm` and its Stop branch): a reminder annai says itself is said on time and ends the listening, as with Koe. Added: her voice beginning anything after the question's own start marks the card (`onBegin` registered after the question's `say`), so its listening never opens; `confirmHeard` takes `again` (`Halves.#koeLogged` sets it for Koe's `heard.text`), a later copy of a line's words that can take back a yes not yet settled and never give one; and a Stop while the card's microphone opens closes it (the condition `#listenFor` has). empty-heard (Koe's model writing the card's line down) is not merged.
- Why we had to change: the user's direction (2026-11-12): "if it is in a mainly works but maybe in some edge cases does not behave as expected, then always let it fail safe: aka stop trying to listen to voice yes and the card should always work", and "what I prefer is simple and robust solutions". The plain case worked in every real run (8 of 8 live, 10 of 10 classic); of the 16 unsafe rows the status review found, 9 were a listening that opened, or a yes that counted, outside it. The second re-review found one more (N1: a voiced line of Koe's queued behind the question, voice off mid-question, a tap, "Yes." to Koe granted), the third review running to find one in the tap path. The user picked "Plain case only (Recommended)", and "Known limitation (Recommended)" for cards with buttons from outside the conversation.
- Original design was wrong because: each part existed so the spoken yes would work in one more edge (a card nobody heard, a reminder due inside the 20 s, a setup with nothing to write speech down, a second copy of a line's words), and each made annai decide, later and from more state, whether a yes was still the card's answer. Every review of those parts found a wrong grant (F1 to F3, R1 and R2, N1; empty-heard's copy and late grant). The edge each served can take a tap on Allow, which can't be mistaken.
- Lesson: for an act that grants, keep the automatic path to the plain case, opened in one place under one condition, and let every other case fall back to the action that can't be mistaken; accept those cases as limitations at design time, before building for them. Evidence that arrives later can withdraw consent, never give it.

## 2026-11-13 A question a tap cut opens nothing
- Changed: annai's card's listening (`confirm`, src/core/agent/loop.js) opens only for a question said to its end: `spoken.cut` joins `spoken.failed` and voice off among the conditions that leave the card to its buttons. Before, a tap on Shiori that stopped the question, or stopped a line of hers with the question queued behind it, resolved the question's `say` unfailed and opened the listening at the tap. Six tests that asserted that changed (five now assert no grant, one asserts the tap's own 8 s), and a probe the review found (Q4) is three failing-first tests with their no-tap controls.
- Why we had to change: the review of the plain-yes build (2026-11-13) found a wrong grant through it. A reminder annai says, or a line of Koe's, is playing when the card comes; its question queues behind it; the user taps Shiori to stop the line and says "Okay." to the line; the tap cut the question, which never played, the listening opened at the tap, and "Okay." granted the trip. It was on 1d67a15a too (probed), and on the live build by reading its code (not probed there). The user picked "Remove the exception (Recommended)" (2026-11-13) over switching the spoken yes off: "One condition: only a question said to its end opens the listening. Removes a path, adds nothing." The cost: tapping Shiori mid-answer and saying "yes" to a card queued behind her needs a tap on Allow.
- Original design was wrong because: the exception ("a tap that cuts the question opens it at the cut") was the one piece of the rule beyond the user's words. The user's pick said the card listens after annai says its question aloud; the exception opened it for a question nobody heard, on the guess that a tap meant the user wanted to answer the card. A tap means "stop her", and annai cannot tell which of her sentences the yes that follows is for. Card-first tested it, the card-ends review kept it "on purpose", and the design check carried it into the plain rule as "already the user's pick", each trusting the step before it instead of reading the pick's words.
- Lesson: when a design check carries a rule forward as "already the user's pick", read the pick's own words, not the earlier agent's summary of them: a rule beyond the user's words is where the next review finds the wrong grant, and for an act that grants it is dropped, not guarded.

## 2026-11-13 The card is answered by tap only
- Changed: annai's card ("Read <trip>?", "Carry on in <trip>?") is answered by its buttons only. `read_trip` and `open_trip` pass `confirm` no yes list (src/core/agent/trip-tools.js), so annai's word lists can make a heard line a no and never a yes; and `confirm` (src/core/agent/loop.js) no longer opens the card's listening after its question: said aloud or not, the card waits for its buttons, as an unsaid one already did. A no a microphone hears (Koe's, or the one a tap on Shiori opens) still declines; a kept line ends the card unanswered, and the results now say only a tap answers it. The rest of the listening (the window, `over`, the settle, `confirmHeard`, `cardTakes`, the halves' hooks) stays in place, unused for a yes, until the user decides whether to remove it.
- Why we had to change: the user's stop rule (2026-11-12 and 11-13): "No more fix rounds on the spoken yes: turn it off (about 5 lines) and the card takes taps only."; "Stop rule stays: any further wrong grant switches the spoken yes off, no question asked." The review of the card's 20 s build (2026-11-13) then found one on the live code: one Shiori, "Yes." armed 18.0 s into the card's 20 s, annai says a reminder itself (quiet hours) at 18.3 s and closes the microphone, the "No, wait." after it is never heard, and the trip is granted by voice at 21.1 s, after the listening had ended (its probe C1).
- Original design was wrong because: a spoken yes was made to count by deciding, from more and more state, whether a heard line was still the card's answer: a window, anything said or shown since the question, a settle that waits for a take-back, copies of a line's words, which microphone heard it. The plain case worked in every real run (8 of 8 live, 10 of 10 classic), but each part built so one more edge worked drew a wrong grant in its next review (F1 to F3, R1 and R2, N1, Q4, C1). The last was a take-back missed on one more path: the window's early end closed the microphone while a yes still settled. A yes is safe only if every way to take it back is heard, and the set of paths that can stop the hearing kept growing.
- Lesson: when an automatic yes keeps drawing wrong grants at its edges, switch it off rather than guard one more path: the action that can't be mistaken (a tap) is the design, and the automatic path is an extra that must stay simple to earn its place. Agree the stop rule before the next review, so one more wrong grant settles it without another round.

## 2026-11-13 A result names the state it leaves
- Changed: read_trip's and open_trip's "not answered" results (`src/core/agent/trip-tools.js`) now say that the card is closed, that only a tap answers it, and to call the tool again for a new card ("Not read: only a tap on annai's card allows reading X, and this card is closed. If they meant yes, tell them only a tap answers it, and call read_trip again."; open_trip the same with "takes them into X; this card is closed, and nothing moved"). Koe's instruction (`src/core/koe/instructions.js`) says "[the user chose] is their answer to a question with buttons (a tap, or a no said to annai's card)": a yes is no longer in it. Before, read_trip's result said "If they meant yes, ask them to tap Allow", and open_trip's said only "call open_trip again".
- Why we had to change: the review of the yes-off build (2026-11-13, findings 1 and 2). A line Shiori keeps, a spoken "Yes." included, ends the card unanswered, and its Allow, Always and Not now go grey. read_trip's result then told Shiori to ask the user to tap Allow: the user would tap a grey button and nothing would happen, the "staring at the screen to see why it does not work" that the user's words rule out. Koe's instruction still described the spoken yes the build had switched off, so a model could offer "just say yes".
- Original design was wrong because: the words pointed at the card's Allow without checking what the card shows once the result arrives: its buttons go grey then (since 2026-10-16), so a typed line met a dead button from 2026-11-09 on, and the build that switched the spoken yes off moved the commonest spoken answer onto that path; no test runs a sentence's meaning, and a test pinned the old words as the right ones. The build report listed them as left alone ("a prompt change"), which left the one user-facing failure the stop rule was meant to remove.
- Lesson: when a change alters what an act leaves behind, re-read every sentence a model is given about that act. A result that tells it what to say to the user must describe the state as it now is (the card is closed) and the way out that exists (call again, for a new card), never a control that no longer works.

## 2026-11-13 A reminder ends the wait of the half that speaks
- Changed: a wake that carries a reminder or a place alert now ends the `wait` tool of whichever half speaks now (`#cameDue`, src/core/agent/loop.js: `this.#half.voice()` in place of `this.#half.name === 'koe'`): one Shiori's (no Koe model), Kokoro's while Koe is down, and a chat Koe's as before; Kokoro's wait while Koe works is as it was. The wait's result is the same words with what is still to come chosen by who waits: Koe's model reads "A reminder came due: say it (below). Kokoro's note is still to come." as before; one Shiori's and Kokoro's read, in new words, "A reminder came due: say it (below). The user hasn't answered yet." (an alert's: "An alert came (below): say it if it helps them now, or let it pass. The user hasn't answered yet.").
- Why we had to change: with one Shiori, or Kokoro while Koe is down, a reminder due while her wait waited for the user (voice on listening, voice off for typing) was said only when the wait ran out: 570 s late on the stand-in with a wait of 600 s. The backlog triage (2026-11-13) counted a reminder up to 10 minutes late as a wrong result, and the user approved its fix ("Approve all").
- Original design was wrong because: 2026-11-12's change ended the wait for what must be said now, but keyed it on one setup's name (Koe) and worded the result for that setup only ("Kokoro's note is still to come"), so the same wait in the other setups kept the old fault. The rule is about who says things to the user now, which the half already knows (`voice()`).
- Lesson: when a rule fixes a fault in one setup, key it on the property that causes the fault (here, the half that speaks), not on that setup's name, and word what the model reads for each waiter it now reaches: a result must not promise something (a note) that this waiter will never get.

## 2026-11-13 A queued message is never taken apart
- Changed: a wait, her question (`ask`, aloud or not) and annai's card (`confirm`) that find a message of the user's already queued (written while she worked) no longer take it off the queue as their answer. They end at once, and their result says it follows: "Not waiting for an answer: the user wrote while you were thinking; it follows." (`speak`'s listening and `wait`), "Your question is closed, its buttons too: what the user wrote follows, sent before it could take their answer. If it answers your question, take it as the answer; otherwise ask again if it still matters." (`ask`; fix round 1 reworded it, as a line typed while she still said the question is not one written before it), "What the user wrote follows." (annai's card, after its "Not read …" words). The turn then takes the whole message as it takes any wake: its photos, handed files and hand-over with it, as "[meanwhile, the user wrote] …". Removed: `typedGot` and its three sites (`#waitFor`, `#listenFor`'s two looks, `confirm`), and the wait tool's own copy of the typed-answer branch; one look, `#earlier`, asks "is a line of theirs queued" for `#waitFor` and both of `#listenFor`'s looks (src/core/agent/loop.js). Koe's owed mark (`#facts.wrote`) now lives once, in `#answer`: a line typed as the answer to Koe's listening, or one that follows it so, is owed with voice on as with voice off. A line typed once she is waiting still answers ("The user wrote: …"), as before. Her own question takes a queued tap (`chose`) as its answer (`#earlier('ask')` splices it, "The user chose: …"; a tap carries no photos or files), and annai's card never does.
- Why we had to change: on the live build, a message sent with photos or handed files while Shiori worked lost them whenever her next step waited, asked or showed annai's card: only its words were kept as the answer (small-voice re-review 2, Finding 1; the design check found annai's card as a third site). With voice on and a reply of speak + wait 600, a line typed while she worked was taken by speak's listening, and her wait then listened its whole 600 s before she could answer it (probed: 600.5 s; 1.0 s now). And with voice on, a line typed into Koe's wait was not owed (Finding 2), because the mark sat in only one of two copies of the typed branch.
- Fix round 1 (the review's Findings 1 and 2): the take-apart had a second job nobody listed, and removing it dropped it. With voice on, her question's buttons are written before her voice starts and its wait exists only after the question is said and the microphone opens (seconds), so a tap in that window is queued, not an answer, and her question closed with "ask it again" over a tap that had answered it. The three `ask` returns also skipped `#answer`, where Koe's owed mark lives, so a line written before Koe's own question was not owed (0 nudges, now 1).
- Original design was wrong because: the take-apart was a second, partial copy of the user's message (words, claim and tap, not photos or files), written by the first agent loop of 2026-10-16, 81 minutes before photos existed, and never updated for them. Nothing needed it: the turn's own join already delivers every queued wake whole after each step, and a heard line queued then has ended speak's and wait's listening with "it follows" since the same day. The copy only moved the same words from after the tool's result into it, at the cost of everything it left out.
- Lesson: a step that meets a queued item either takes it whole or leaves it for the one path that does: a second, partial copy of a message loses whatever is added to the message later. When two paths answer one question, keep the one that carries the whole thing and remove the other. Before removing a path, list every job it did, not only the one it was written for: here it also answered a tap that came before the question's wait existed.

## 2026-11-13 read_trip takes from
- Changed: read_trip takes an optional `from` (an integer, 0 or more), as `read` does. With `path`, a text file comes in pieces from that character: each is as much as fits the result's limit (`RESULT_MAX`, 40,000 characters) with its heading, which then says "from character N", and ends, while more is left, with "… N more characters; read_trip again with from: N". The piece leaves room for each line-leading `[` that `theirs()` escapes, so the escaped piece still fits. Its description says "long text comes in pieces, read again with from". The commit before made `read`'s piece the limit less `READ_ON` (100, the read-on line's room), which both now share (src/core/agent/tools.js).
- Why we had to change: Shiori read a long text file in another trip (a saved booking email, a long note) and got its first 40,000 characters, told how many more were cut, with no way to read on: an answer from the end of the file needed a move to that trip (the limitation "read_trip reads a long text file only up to its result's cut", from stage 5's review, finding 11; fixed by the backlog triage of 2026-11-13). Building it showed `read`'s own read-on line never reached her either: its piece was the whole limit, so the result's cut took the line, and she was told "… 53 more characters were cut." with 10,000 more in the file.
- Original design was wrong because: read_trip copied what `read` reaches (a trip's text files) but not how it reads on, leaving the result's general cut as the only bound, and a cut counts what it dropped without saying where to go on; and `read`'s piece was sized to the very limit it had to fit inside, so its own line was the part cut.
- Lesson: a tool that reads like another reads on like it too. A piece that pages leaves room inside the result's limit for its read-on line, counting whatever the result adds to the piece (a heading, escapes); a result's general cut is no way on.

## 2026-11-13 Anthropic's photo uploads start off
- Changed: the Anthropic Messages type's `uploads` tunable defaults to `false` (anthropic-messages 0.5.2), so a photo sent with an Anthropic model goes in the request, with no upload to `/v1/files` tried first, and the Test button tries none there either. The provider page's switch starts off; turned on (for a server that speaks the type and takes uploads), it uploads as before. A provider page saves only what differs from its type's tunables, so every Anthropic provider saved before takes the new default with the update.
- Why we had to change: Anthropic's `/v1/files` answers the browser's CORS preflight with 400 ("Disallowed CORS origin") and no `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`, and its answer to a POST has none either (checked 2026-11-13; `/v1/messages` answers its preflight 200 with `*`). Every photo run of the wait-takes real check began with a failed upload; annai then sent the photo in the request, and stopped uploading there until its next start.
- Original design was wrong because: uploads were on by default for every type with a files API, and nothing ever asked Anthropic's from a browser: the unit tests stub fetch, and the e2e stand-in answers every path's CORS check with `*`. Test's upload step failing doesn't fail Test (the photo still goes in the request), so nothing said it never worked.
- Lesson: ask the real API from where the code runs: a browser client's feature is on by default only where the real address answers the browser's own CORS check for that path, as one path of an API passing says nothing of another. Check with the preflight a browser sends (`OPTIONS` with `Origin` and the request's headers), not a request from a server; a stand-in that answers every path is no evidence of it.

## 2026-11-13 The now block tells the time to the second
- Changed: the now block's `time:` line carries seconds (`time: Thursday 2026-11-26 16:12:37, America/Los_Angeles (GMT-8)`; before, `16:12`), made in one place, `localTime` (src/core/agent/context.js). Every model that is told "now" reads it there: one Shiori, Kokoro, a chat Koe, and a live Koe's setup. Nothing else in the block changed.
- Why we had to change: Shiori set a reminder "in N minutes" from a time to the minute, so it could come up to 60 s early, and "remind me in 1 minute" was sometimes refused as already passed (seen in a real run). `watch_set` refuses a time that is not ahead of the real clock; from a minute's time she can set one that is a few seconds ahead of the true now, or already behind it by the time the call runs. The user picked "Time to the second (Recommended)" (2026-11-13): "Give her the time to the second in her context block: a few bytes, one test, a real-model check."
- Original design was wrong because: the line was sized for saying the time, where minutes are enough, but the model also counts from it to set times that annai keeps to the millisecond and checks against the real clock. Seconds cost the prompt cache nothing: the now block comes after the last cache marker, and a now block kept within a turn is kept as it was sent.
- Lesson: give a model a value at the precision of what it computes from it, not of what it says: when a tool checks the model's result against the true value, the model needs the true value.

## 2026-11-13 A live Koe hands reminders to Kokoro
- Changed: a live session's tools (`liveTools()`, src/core/agent/loop.js) leave out `watch_set`, as they leave out `speak` and `wait`, so a live Koe on Gemini Live or OpenAI Realtime is never offered it and a call to it finds no tool. A reminder asked of a live Koe takes the path every ask it has no tool for takes: "One moment.", and `tell_kokoro` with what the user said; Kokoro sets it from its own now block, which is written fresh for each of its requests, to the second. A live Koe keeps `watch_list` and `watch_cancel`, which read and cancel stored watches and work nothing out from the time. A chat Koe keeps `watch_set`: its now block is written fresh into every request. No instruction changed: Koe's live rules already send any request it can't do itself to Kokoro. The Koe and Kokoro design ("Tools: who has what") and the main spec (§10.8, Tools) say so.
- Why we had to change: a live Koe is told the time once, in its session's setup, and a resume sends that setup's context again (koe/live.js), so after a close for quiet the next session talks from the first one's clock for as long as Gemini keeps the handle, up to 2 hours; on OpenAI Realtime a session's clock can be up to about 55 minutes old. "Remind me in 5 minutes" said to it was then refused as "already passed", which only Koe read, or set minutes early, and Koe said the wrong clock time aloud (the time-second review, F1, from reading the code; a chat Koe on a real model was seen setting a reminder itself, 44 s early, from a now block a minute old). The user's pick, 2026-11-13: "Live Koe hands off".
- Original design was wrong because: it gave Koe `watch.*` as a quick tool for both of its ways of talking without asking whether each has what the tool's input is worked out from: a relative time needs the time now, which a chat Koe gets on every request and a live Koe gets once. The only guard was a line in its instructions ("<now> and <today> are from when this session opened: for the time or place now, ask Kokoro") set against a tool still offered, and a chat Koe under the same pass-on rule set reminders itself.
- Lesson: offer a model a tool only where it holds, fresh, what the tool's input is worked out from. A fact it is told once (the time a session opened, which a resume keeps) goes stale, and words telling it not to trust that fact lose to a tool still offered: take the tool away there, and let the part that has the fact do it.

## 2026-11-14 Her words come in the user's language, not given in English
- Changed: Koe's instructions give it no English words to say. Passing a line on, it speaks (live: says) "a word or two to tell them to hold on, in the language you answer in and in the voice your personality gives you" (it was `"One moment."`; the first rewording, "to ask them to wait", gave a Japanese user the shop phrase 「少々お待ちください。」 on all 5 pass-ons of the after run), and "If a word is needed, say okay in the language you answer in." (it was `"Okay."`); Kokoro's part names that word or two the same way. The Languages section (`languagesText`) always ends with one sentence, the same for every setting and worded by its own condition: "When you answer in a language other than English, say things the way a native speaker of that language would, and say what annai's English words mean instead of translating them word for word (a plan item marked fixed is one that can't be moved)." It sets no register: her personality does. A new install's profile has no `- Language: English` line; an older one that is still exactly the old default is rewritten to the new one at start (by annai, as an unedited personality follows an update); any other profile stays as it is, but for that line: when the languages she is told of (the user's pick, else the language the phone is set to) hold no English, a line that is exactly `- Language: English` (trailing spaces and a CRLF allowed) is removed at start, by annai, every other byte as it was (the user's pick, 2026-11-14: "Drop it when it contradicts (Recommended)"); with English among them it stays (a phone set to Japanese or Chinese, with nothing picked, loses it; an English phone keeps it). The recap, the one reader that took its language from that line ("their profile says which"), names its language in its request's first lines by compaction's rule (`summaryLanguage`: the fixed answer language, else the listed language the traveler last wrote in, else the first listed), over the traveler's words without annai's placeholder for audio it couldn't write down; with nothing chosen and none of their words, the screens' language.
- Why we had to change: the user, 2026-11-14: "the japanese words seems bit 不自然 feels like english translated to japanese not native japanese". The real run before (live main b3bc9066, Japanese, typed) found a chat Koe saying "One moment." in English on 4 of 6 lines (the other two 「少々お待ちください。」, call-centre keigo); annai's own English carried over word for word (a booking's `fixed` as 「時間固定」, "the train in the plan" as 「計画にある電車」) with written connectives in chat; and every request held `- Language: English` from the default profile beside "The user speaks Japanese", which made a new install's recap English for a Japanese speaker.
- Original design was wrong because: the instructions are English for every user (localization design, section 7) and quoted the exact words to say, and a small model says quoted words as they are; they said which language to answer in but nothing of how it should sound, so a model writing Japanese from English instructions and English data translates. The profile's template predates the Languages setting and kept its own answer to the same question, which one reader still used.
- Lesson: in instructions written in one language for answers in another, describe what to say and the language to say it in, never quote the words: a small model repeats quoted words as they are. Naming the answer's language is not enough: say it should sound as a native speaker would say it, and name no register (with "to a friend" in the sentence, Opus dropped です・ます in 7 of 8 texts; without it, 0 of 16). Say the app's own words are explained, not translated: one example of what such a word means (not a word to say) moved Opus (「時間を動かせない予定」) but not Sonnet or Haiku (「(固定)」 5 times in the final run). Describing an act as a request ("ask them to wait") gets the stock phrase of that act, and naming whose voice it is in did not change it (「少々お待ちください。」 on 9 of 11 pass-ons in the final run): settled, as the user accepted the phrase (2026-11-14, "Accept it (Recommended)": "Correct and natural; a personality can make it warmer later"). A template's default for what a setting now decides is a second answer: remove it (from an untouched old copy too, and from an edited copy where the languages she is told of hold no English, that line alone: an edited profile kept `- Language: English` beside "The user speaks Japanese"), and move its readers to the setting.

## 2026-11-14 A sleep ends her turn by what her calls gave back
- Changed: a `sleep` in a reply that also holds her question (`ask`) no longer ends the turn once the question has come back (src/core/agent/loop.js, `#asked`, `ANSWERED`). The `ask` tool marks that it put her question to the user and waited on it (not for a malformed ask, which fails first and keeps the failed-call rule, nor Kokoro's ask that annai's gate turned into a note to Koe, which never runs, nor a live Koe's, which is answered at once); the loop reads the mark after the reply's calls ran, and treats the sleep as annai treats one beside a failed call: it doesn't run, its result says why (`Not asleep: your question came back answered or closed (its result is above), so your turn goes on.`), and her next step reads the user's answer. Unlike the failed-call rule it has no count: each answer takes an act of the user or a note of the other half (which also closes a question), so a model repeating `ask` and `sleep` waits on something from outside her turn each time. The sleep that gave way is recorded: its `tool.result` carries `awake: true` (a stored-record field, new; an older page reading a newer log ignores it and behaves as before), and the stored log's reader (`transcript.js`) reads it next to `end` and `cut` as "the reply did not end the turn", so an answer given just before a reload, or before another conversation is opened, is taken up on return as it is for a question with no sleep beside it. No instruction changed (the prompt and the tool descriptions are as they were, so no provider cache restarts); only what the sleep's result can say is new. The spec says so (§6.2, §7.1).
- Why we had to change: in the answers-natural study's third round (real Sonnet, annai on b3bc9066) Sonnet put `ask` and `sleep` in one reply in 3 of its 9 replies with an `ask`; the user typed an answer over the card; annai ran the `ask` (waiting for the answer), took the typed line as its result, then ran the `sleep` and ended the turn. No model request followed, and the line was stored and never answered (the harness waited 45 s). A tap does the same by the code, and a spoken answer too (the reproduction, typed and tapped: the second request never came). A traveller's line that is never answered fails the app's whole point.
- Original design was wrong because: the end of the turn was decided from the reply's words (a `sleep` in it) before its calls had run, in the loop and again in the stored log's reader (the first fix changed only the loop, so a reload between the answer and her next reply still read the turn as ended: the final review, F1), and `sleep` always runs last by design, so a call that waits for a person was answered in a step nobody was going to read. The failed-call rule had found the same fault for errors (a sleep beside a failed call doesn't end the turn) and fixed it for that one cause only: the user's answer arriving in a result was the same shape and had no rule. Where Koe's chat half had a backstop for one corner of it (a line typed into its wait is owed an answer, and nudged), nothing covered a tap, speech, or the other halves.
- Lesson: a decision that ends a turn is made from what the step's calls gave back, not from what the reply said it would do: a call that waits for a person puts the person's words in a result, and a turn that ends on them has thrown them away. Fix it where the end is decided, once for every kind of answer (typed, tapped, spoken, a queued line), rather than in each call or half that can hold the answer; the model's tool result says "not asleep" and why, so what it reads matches the state. Every reader of that decision, the running loop and the stored log a reload reads, takes it from the same record: a sleep that gave way says so in a field of its result, never only in the words a model reads.

## 2026-11-14 An answer to her question is owed a reply
- Changed: the user's answer to her question card (`ask`: a tap, typing or speech) is a line of theirs that she owes a reply to, as a typed line that joins her turn is (src/core/agent/loop.js, `#answerAsk`, `#facts.answeredAt`, `unanswered()`): when her reply to the answer says nothing to them (plain text she never shows, a bare `sleep`, a `wait`), annai asks her once more in the same turn, and a second silence is the card "Shiori didn't answer". What makes the answer owed is the count of times she had reached the user when the answer came (`#reached`), compared with the count now: her question put something up, so "nothing has reached them since the turn began", which the typed line rests on, was already false. A tap now goes through `#answer` like typing and speech (two copies of its branch are gone). The same change in the other two places that read "did this reply end her turn": the stored log's reader gives a hand-over back when a result says a sleep gave way (`#settled`, transcript.js), so a turn taken up after an answered question, a failed call, or a cut carries its hand-over and Undo covers what she did after the answer; a restart's own results count too: the sleep it cut before it ran is stored as one that gave way, as the loop writes it; and the stored result of a sleep that gave way carries `awake: true` from the loop's own decision (`o.awake`), not from one branch of it, so a sleep whose input was malformed, or that was cut before it ran, is read as one that gave way after a reload, and a call cut by another conversation opening counts as the failed call, so a turn cut during work after she had spoken is taken up on return, with or without a `sleep` in its reply, until the turn has had two sleeps that gave way (past that a cut reply that had spoken and held a `sleep` reads as ended and is not taken up: deferred in docs/limitations.md; this ended one shape of an approved limitation, narrowed there). No instruction, tool description or stored field is new (the cue is the one annai already sends; `awake` is written in three more cases: those two, and a sleep a restart's repair answers).
- Why we had to change: the real-model run of the ask-sleep fix (2026-11-14, Sonnet 5.5, 85 runs) found that after an answered card 7 of 55 replies on the fixed branch and 1 of 22 on live main showed the user nothing (a full answer in plain text 3 times, `sleep` only 2, and a `wait` of 120 s twice, which the run's 40 s window closed on, so those two are not known to have ended silent), and no second request followed: 3 of the 7 came after an ordinary `ask`, so it was older than that fix. Two leftovers of the fix, both found by its reviews: a hand-over's Undo missed what a resumed ask-and-sleep turn wrote (`handover` null where the same turn with the `ask` alone wrote `h-1`), and a sleep that gave way but errored on its input was not taken up after a reload.
- Original design was wrong because: what the loop owed the user was settled when she reached them and re-opened only by a line that joined her turn as a wake. An answer to her own question comes in a tool's result, never as a wake, so the one thing it needs, "this line is still unanswered", had no owner: Koe's half marked a line typed into its wait, for itself only, and nothing marked a tap or a spoken answer or the other halves. The stored log's reader repeated the earlier fault of deciding from a reply's words: it settled the hand-over when the reply held a sleep, before the results said whether the sleep ran.
- Lesson: when what a person answers to the program's own question comes back inside a call's result, it needs its own mark for "owed a reply", set where the answer is taken and counted against what reached the person since, for every kind of answer to the question card (a tap, typing, speech) in the halves that ask again (not Kokoro behind Koe); an answer into a wait is not one, for it may be no one's; the mark the next message would have set (a wake) never comes. Compare counts of what reached them, never "nothing since the turn began": the question itself broke that. And every reader of "did this reply end her turn" reads the results, the hand-over's too: what a reply settled by its words is given back when a result of its calls says otherwise, and a copy of it undone meanwhile is not. This was the fifth correction to that reader in as many reviews: a reader that infers an end from words keeps needing one, and the loop, which knows when her turn ends, is the place to write it down. Two rules added in the second fix round were taken out again (the user's pick, 2026-11-14, after the design check): a give-back at any wake that joins a turn gave an older hand-over back to a later turn that was never hers (the recap of a move into that trip), and treating a cut question as owed gave an older page's restart log its hand-over back for good. A rule that gives state back across a turn boundary must be fenced to the turn it belongs to, or not exist; the next change writes the turn's end into the log, so the reader needs neither (docs/limitations.md, deferred).

## 2026-11-14 A plan item's fixed is shown as what it means
- Changed: wherever annai shows a model a plan item beside its data, `fixed` reads as what it means, from one helper (`fixedWords` in src/core/plan.js): "can't be moved" for a fixed item, and nothing for a flexible one. Kokoro's now block (`next in the plan`) and Koe's today view had a column `fixed` of `yes` / `no`; it is `constraint` now (an empty cell reads as no constraint, where an empty `timing`, the first rename, could read as no time set), and so is the recap's plan table (`kind`, `fixed` / `flexible`). The plan watches' wake texts said `"Lunch" (fixed, 12:00)` and `(flexible, 09:45)`; they say `(can't be moved, 12:00)` and `(09:45)`, and the off-plan wake (flexible items only) no mark. Koe's view listed each booking's `status` word (`unverified`, `confirmed`); its column `check` now says what the screens mark (`checkWords` in src/core/bookings.js, from `checkOf`): "not sure it was read right" when they mark the booking whole or a field of it, and nothing otherwise, a confirmed booking included. The stored fields, the files she reads and writes, the format check and every API keep their names and values; nothing stored changed. The Languages section's example ("a plan item marked fixed is one that can't be moved") stays: she still meets the field's name in the files she reads and the skills that explain it.
- Why we had to change: the user, 2026-11-14: "the japanese words seems bit 不自然 feels like english translated to japanese not native japanese". After the Languages sentence and its example, Sonnet and Koe (Haiku) still wrote 「(固定)」 beside a plan item in the r3 real run (5 times; Opus 0), and Kokoro's notes to Koe carried it, which Koe repeated; Koe said 「予約はまだ未確認です」 of bookings (r3: 2 and 3 times). The user's pick: "Fix at the source (Recommended)". The check (kotei-r1, real Sonnet 5.5 and Haiku 5.5, Japanese, the r3 lines): main ef833e39 2 「固定」 in Sonnet's words and 4 in Kokoro's notes to Koe over one run each; "can't be moved" / "can be moved", 0 in 6 runs, but she then told the user lunch could be moved, unasked, in 5 of the 6 first answers (main: 0 of 2). The user's pick on that: "Only say what can't move (Recommended)": "Flexible items get no words in what she's shown, so she mentions a constraint only where there is one, as a person would." The status word, worded "not checked by the user yet" (one round, 3 runs), took 「未確認」 to 0 but became 「ホテルの予約はまだ確認されていません」 (2 of 3), which reads as the hotel not having confirmed it, about bookings the screens mark nothing on (Shiori writes every booking `unverified`, and since bookings list the fields she is unsure of, the screens mark only those). With nothing for a flexible item and the screens' mark for a booking (9 runs: Sonnet alone, the two halves, and the two halves with the hotel confirmed and the train's seat unsure): 「固定」 0 and 「未確認」 0 in what the user read, a flexible item said movable 0, a blank read as no time set 0, a confirmed booking said checked 0; Koe said the unsure train once, 「電車の予約は読み取りに自信がないので、後で確認させてください」.
- Original design was wrong because: a column's or a tag's name is data's label for a program, but a model reading it beside an item treats it as a word for that item, and answering in another language it translates the word: the instruction to explain annai's English words moved Opus only, and the data repeated the name on every request. Both values of a flag were shown as words of equal weight, so the model passed on the one that is no news. The booking's `status` no longer answered "what should the user check": the screens' `checkOf` does, and the view showed the older field.
- Lesson: data a model is shown beside an item says what a field means, in plain words, not the field's name: the name is said, translated. Show only the value that is a constraint, and nothing for the other: a phrase for the ordinary value is said too, unasked. Make the words in one helper at the source, used by every place that renders the field for a model, keep the stored name, and where the screens already answer the question (what to check), show the model their answer, not the field they used to read. Name a column so an empty cell reads as "nothing to say", not "unknown".

## 2026-11-14 A draft carried from changed English blocks the check
- Changed: `node scripts/strings.mjs --check` fails on a translation carried over from changed English (a `draft` whose note says "English was: …"), naming the key, the old English and the new, until the row is rewritten, set `ok` and its note cleared. A draft with no such note still ships. A plugin manifest's changed English follows the same rule as a code message's (before, a manifest row whose English changed kept shipping its old translation as `ok`), and `--review` shows each draft's note beside it.
- Why we had to change: a review of the strings tool (2026-11-14) found that a changed plugin description kept its old Japanese and Chinese, and that a carried code message did the same as a draft: in ja.tsv's history, 11 of 90 carried drafts kept the old text in the commit that carried them, some wrong in meaning (a live-session line lost "when she's asked to sleep"; a timeout line kept "of quiet"). The user's pick: "Block until rewritten (Recommended)".
- Original design was wrong because: "drafts ship" (the localization design's decision 5) treats every draft as a translation of the current English waiting only for a reader's polish. A carried draft is a translation of other English: it says what the old message said, so shipping it shows a reader on a Japanese or Chinese screen a meaning the app no longer has, and nothing on screen tells them.
- Lesson: a value kept across a change of what it was made from is a stale copy, not a draft of the new one: let it help the person who rewrites it, never let it ship as the new one's meaning. Mark it where it is kept (the note), and fail the check on that mark, not on a reading of the text.

## 2026-11-14 The loop logs when her turn ends
- Changed: the loop writes a `turn.end` event (`actor: 'system'`, data `{ wake, handover }`, plus the half's mark) once a turn, in `#steps` (src/core/agent/loop.js), where the turn ends by its own rule: a reply in words the nudge doesn't ask again, asleep (or back to sleep after a line), Koe after its cue with only Kokoro's notes left for annai's backstop to show, or once annai has taken Koe's owed notes to show them (a switch, Start fresh). A turn cut at its work (a call of its last reply other than `sleep` came back cut or never ran, or the cut came between steps or in the model call) gets none, and neither does a turn that ends on "Shiori didn't answer" (its `silent` error ends it); a turn can get both `turn.end` and an error record (a Stop that lands on the reply's `sleep`, Start fresh), and the reader ends it at whichever comes first. Every reply's `model.turn` record carries `endLogged: true`: "the loop logs this turn's end", a name chosen so it can't be read as "this reply ended the turn" (the trial's `ends`, which sat on replies that end nothing). The reader (src/core/agent/transcript.js) keeps one field for it, `#underway`: a reply with the key starts it, a `turn.end` or an error of hers with a wake ends it, and an unfinished turn with nothing after it is owed. The hand-over is answered by a `turn.end` that names it, by an Undo, or by a reply from before this change, at no other time. Gone from the reader: `#ends`, `#reached`, `#settled`, the settle at a reply (a sleep among its calls, or no calls), and the result rules (`end`, `awake`, `cut` with its table of tool names, the Stop's words on a result without `cut`, the give-back). Kept one version: `end` and `awake` on results, and the `awake` that `repair()` stamps, for a page one version older (deferred, docs/limitations.md). A reply without the key, written before this change, is read as its turn's end: one stated rule for old logs, approved as a known limitation.
- Why we had to change: nine findings in one night (among them the re-review's R1 and R2, F2 with N1, L1, L2, and the silent-card shape), each the reader learning again a behaviour of the loop (a sleep that gives way, a nudge after an answer, a call cut by an open, a restart's repair), and each fix closing its shape while the next got around it or the rule reached too far.
- Original design was wrong because: the end of a turn was the one fact the loop knew and didn't write, so the reader guessed it from her words (a `sleep` among the calls, or no calls) and corrected the guess with fields on results and rules by tool name. Every new way the loop ends or doesn't end a turn needed a second rule in the reader.
- Lesson: the code that ends a step writes the end as a record; each record of the step carries a key named for "its end is logged", never for "it ended"; readers read the record, never the actor's words; older records are read by one stated rule.

## 2026-11-14 nearby waits for Overpass, and VK's server has its own switch
- Changed: `nearby` gives Overpass a limit of its own, `OVERPASS_MS` (55 s, one limit for all its servers together, src/plugins/places/index.js; the offline pack gives each request its own `ANSWER_MS`, 120 s), while Wikipedia, Nominatim, Open-Meteo and Wikivoyage keep `ASK_MS`'s 25 s; `nearby` still answers once, when both are in. When every Overpass server asked refuses with a 429 or a 5xx, its result says "OpenStreetMap is busy" as the last one refuses; "didn't answer" stays for the limit running out and for any other failure. overpass.private.coffee is no longer asked: the one list, in src/core/pack/osm.js, is overpass-api.de, z.overpass-api.de and lz4.overpass-api.de, asked in turn (the next only after the one before refuses or fails, for `nearby` and the offline pack alike, so an answer costs one request), and VK's maps.mail.ru joins it, for `nearby` at once beside that list and for the offline pack after it, while a new app setting is on: `places.vkOverpass`, `true` only when ticked in Settings → Tools; anything else is off. The credits, the spec ("A tool hangs", §11.7, the credits) and the limitations entry say so.
- Why we had to change: online, `nearby` rarely got OpenStreetMap's places: the phone's Trace had overpass-api.de answering at 24.6 to 24.8 s, cut by the 25 s limit, and private.coffee never answering. A day of light probes from one server (the user's pick, 2026-11-13: "Allow light probes"), 24 hourly runs of nearby's default query (sights and food, 1000 m, at four landmarks) to each server at once, had overpass-api.de answering 13 of 24 (and refusing 11 in half a second, in stretches of hours), z. 15, lz4. 18, one of those three answering in 23 of 24, all in 25 to 51 s; private.coffee and kumi.systems never answering; and maps.mail.ru answering 10 in 14 to 22 s.
