The 50 s first chosen was the 95% of those 46 answers asked at once (47.7 s, rounded up to the next 5 s); the code asks in turn, so the count that sizes the wait is a call's, replayed over each run's own times (overpass-api.de first, the next only after a refusal, one limit): 22 of 24 calls get places at 50 s and 23 of 24 at 55 s, because overpass-api.de answered once at 51.3 s and, at 50 s, the call ended before its backends were asked. So 55 s: the slowest first answer, rounded up to the next 5 s. The user's pick (2026-11-14): "Fall back to its backends (Recommended)": "Ask overpass-api.de first; on a quick refusal ask z., then lz4. Same operator, same usage rules, no extra load when it answers. VK stays behind its switch, off." For VK the user had picked "maps.mail.ru only as an extra server behind a setting, off by default", since asking it sends the search point, to about 11 m, to maps.mail.ru, which VK runs.
- Original design was wrong because: the 25 s came from the query's own `[timeout:20]` plus 5 s to connect, the limit the request declares, not from when answers come. Overpass holds a request until a slot is free before its clock starts, so a busy server answers well past the declared time, and every answer was cut. And the first build (one server, a longer wait) rested on the probe's first 8 hours, which had overpass-api.de answering 7 of 8; the full day had it answering 13 of 24, so that build would have put places in about half the calls and "busy" in the rest.
- Lesson: size a wait from the answer times measured where it runs, never from the limit the request declares: a server can queue a request before its own clock starts. Measure a shared server over a full cycle of its load (a day), not its first hours: 7 of 8 became 13 of 24, and the design built on the first hours reached half the calls. Size it from the code's own rule replayed over the runs (a call's count), not from the pooled answer times of servers asked another way: asked in turn, one slow first answer ended a call that the pooled 95% called safe. A switch that sends data to a party names that party, not its role: a key named for the role would carry the consent to whoever fills the role next.

## 2026-11-14 annai's own plugins' words are read field by field
- Changed: on the phone, a words file of annai's own plugin (`catalog/l10n/<tag>/<id>.json`, kept with the plugin) is no longer left out whole when one manifest field fails its check: a field this annai doesn't list as translatable (`TRANSLATED`), one the installed manifest hasn't, or a text past its limit or not text is skipped alone and shows its English, and `wordsOf` lays over the manifest only the fields that pass. One check of a field (`fieldWrong` in src/core/packages.js) serves both `checkTranslation` and `wordsOf`. A third party's file, and `catalog.mjs`'s check of annai's own, still fail whole; the file's own checks and its messages still leave annai's own out whole.
- Why we had to change: the strings-stale branch (2026-11-14) made a provider tool's `label` and `note` translatable. Its re-review (R1) found that a phone still on the app before it refuses the whole new Gemini words file ("isn't a field a translation may give"), so on a Japanese or Chinese phone all of Gemini's words show in English from the first open after that release until the app restarts into it. The user accepted that window for that release ("Release as is (Recommended)"); this change ships a release before any further translatable field is added (the next is "Google Search" in the traveller's language).
- Original design was wrong because: one words file serves every installed version of annai's own plugin and every annai still running on phones, but the reader took a field it didn't know for a broken file, as it does in a third party's package, whose manifest and words come together. The skew rule written for plugin-l10n-fetch (a field the installed manifest hasn't is skipped) covered the plugin's version and not annai's: the list of translatable fields belongs to the reader, and it grows with annai.
- Lesson: a reader of words that a newer catalog writes skips the fields it doesn't know, never the whole file, and applies only the fields it knows, so a field it treats as data is never replaced. Ship that reader a release before the first new field. The writer's own check stays strict: what ships is right for the version it ships with.

## 2026-11-14 Google Search is named in the user's language
- Changed: a provider tool's `name` (a model's `providerTools`, and `modelDefaults`') is a field a translation may give (`TRANSLATED`, src/core/packages.js, up to 80 characters as an ability's name), and Settings → Tools shows it from the plugin's words as the screens show them, by the tool's id (`toolWords` in src/ui/screens/provider.js, which the provider page's switches use too), from the plugin's running words, else its stored ones, so neither Settings → Tools nor the Trace (whose Tool switches are the same list) waits for a plugin to start, and one that hangs starting holds neither (`ownTools` in src/ui/screens/tools.js). annai's own four providers' tools have words in ja, zh-Hans and zh-Hant: Google 検索 / Google 搜索 / Google 搜尋, as Google writes them; Anthropic's search as its Japanese and Chinese docs name it, and its fetch with annai's own word for fetching a page (ページ取得 / 抓取), as the screen beside it says; OpenAI's and OpenRouter's, whose docs are English only, in annai's own words. The grounded card, its line read aloud and the Trace's grounded rows name the search with `sourceOf` (src/ui/screens/talk.js): an entry whose `source` is "Google Search" (what the Gemini plugin writes) or empty gets annai's own translation of "Google Search"; another source shows as it came. Nothing stored changed; the attribution event and the `grounded` entry are as they were. One Shiori's instructions no longer quote the tool's `shows` ("under "From Google Search""): she is told a reply in which she searches is shown to the user as she writes it, with its sources. `shows` still marks the tool whose replies are shown so; its words are no longer read.
- Why we had to change: the user, 2026-11-14: "the japanese words seems bit 不自然 feels like english translated to japanese not native japanese from feedback", then of the Chinese, "they also show similar translated vibes". The strings-stale re-review's lead L1 and its second opinion's leads 3 and 4: on Japanese and Chinese screens Settings → Tools listed "Google Search", "Anthropic web search", "Anthropic web fetch", "OpenAI web search" and "OpenRouter web search" under a translated heading, and the card read "Google Searchから" / "来自 Google Search", read aloud "Google Searchより。", while the glossary has Google writing its name Google 検索 / Google 搜索 / Google 搜尋. Step 1 (2026-11-14, annai's own plugins' words read field by field) shipped first, so a phone a release behind skips the new field alone.
- Original design was wrong because: a provider tool's name was left English because models are told it, but a model reads the manifest itself and the screens a translated copy of it (`wordsOf`), so translating the screens' copy never reaches a model. And the card's English title was quoted to her, so once the card is in the user's language her words could name a card the user doesn't see.
- Lesson: a name a model and the screens both use is translated in the screens' copy only, and a model is told what the user sees, never the words it is shown under: quoted words are said as they are (the 2026-11-14 language runs; not run again for the card's title, whose search needs a paid key).

## 2026-11-14 `me` takes no more turns; the draft is open when nothing else is
- Changed: `me`, the conversation outside any trip, is never opened again and takes no turns (timeline stage 7). Its old lines are read at a new address, `#/talk/me` (src/ui/screens/talk.js), read-only: no button on an old line talks or changes anything (her questions' options, a card's choices, Try again, "This was for Shiori", a tuning card's Undo; a plugin's kind of card is drawn as annai draws its own, with its title and text; photos, "Show what she said" and links still open), no ⋯, no dates or Undo line, and the dock rests as on home, so talking there is the +, a new chat. Home shows it as an undated row, "Shiori, no trip", placed by the user's last line there, read once a page load, and again after an import that replaced `me` (`lastTalked`, now exported from src/core/scopes.js; `forgetMeTalked`, src/ui/screens/trips.js), and only when there is such a line. With nothing else open, the standing draft is: at start with no `activeTrip` (or one whose record is gone, main.js) and after the open trip is removed (`removeTripNow`, when nothing was opened meanwhile), as a tap on + opens it (`openDraft`, src/ui/plus.js). `#/talk` and `#/plan` go to the open conversation's address, the draft's too, and home only when nothing is open. The dock with nothing open (the gap in a removal before its draft opens, or no draft could be made) opens the draft first; if it can't, the + card shows and the words and files stay in the box (a camera photo is dropped, and its card says to take it again). Whatever the scope drawn is, `me`'s, is read-only, a trip address whose open failed included. The loop's one guard (`#drain`, src/core/agent/loop.js): with nothing open (the scope is `me`), no turn runs; queued wakes are written into `me`'s log and their reminders said, as for any conversation that isn't open, and an open of nothing owes nothing, so no restart wake is queued there. Old time watches in `me` still fire: annai says the reminder and logs the wake (live.js `#looked` looks at a `time` watch in any conversation). Its place, activity and "every" watches are never looked at again, as only the open conversation's were (a known limitation, docs/limitations.md).
- Why we had to change: the user's picks for the redesign: "Read-only row (Recommended)", "You can read it; talking starts a new chat." (2026-11-05); "Open the new conversation (Recommended)" for what is open when nothing is, and "Put it back in the type box (Recommended)" for a line typed when no conversation can be made (2026-11-14). The spec check found that `me` was still written by six paths (her `remind`, catch-ups, `/me/` writes, the box with nothing open, old watches, owed restarts), and that the re-plan's way of making a chat at every user act (`openChat` in `send`, `tap`, the box and the camera, with its "no draft yet" ordering) needed state in Halves for a case the standing draft already covers.
- Original design was wrong because: "nothing open" was a real state with its own conversation (`me`), its own screens and its own file space, so every user act had a second destination. Once every conversation belongs to a trip, the state that needs no guard is the one that can't be reached: with the draft open whenever nothing else is, `open(null)` is left only for a store that fails, and one guard at the place turns start covers it.
- Lesson: when a mode is being removed, make its entry point unreachable in the common case (open what the user would get anyway) and keep one guard at the one door for the rare case, rather than redirecting each act that could reach it. A read-only view of the old data is the old view with its actions left out, not a new view.

## 2026-11-14 The no-trip paths and openWhenDone go
- Changed (timeline stage 7, task 4): Shiori's `/waiting/` root goes (`resolvePath` takes `/trip/` and `/me/` only; a `/waiting/` path is refused as any other root is), with the files plugin's waiting branches (her list of the root, `delete` dropping a pasted text for good, `/waiting/names.json` the only file she could write there), the side-task read of a waiting photo or PDF (`waitingMedia`), `me` reaching waiting files' content by hash (reach.js), the no-trip words of her instructions, her snapshot ("# No trip is open"), Koe's today block, `handOverText` and the trip tools (`start_trip`'s "with no trip open, it opens once you are done", `open_trip`'s "with no trip open", its Not now's "Start no trip for the files", the `/waiting/files/` in their inputs' descriptions), and the planning of a waiting calendar file filed by them. `start_trip` stays for a dated trip, where what it starts doesn't open. Halves' `openWhenDone` goes, so `went` comes only from the user's choice on annai's card (`carryOn`). The screens: Trips' Waiting with Shiori panel, the waiting file's screen and its route `#/waiting/file?path=…` (an old address now goes home, in place of its history entry), `waitingHref`, the waiting space's `shownPath`, and the composer's and `askAbout`'s no-trip branches. The waiting space (`sys:waiting`) stays for data: an older annai's files and an old backup's are moved into a "Waiting with Shiori" chat (task 3), and Undo still sends files there when they have nowhere else to go. `dropWaiting` and names.js's `sys:waiting` clause go with their callers. `READ_ON` shrinks by 3 (the longest root is `/trip` now).
- Why we had to change: the user's pick, "Read-only row (Recommended)" (2026-11-05), makes the no-trip conversation read-only, and with the draft open whenever nothing else is (2026-11-14, "Open the new conversation (Recommended)") no turn runs with no trip open: every one of these paths served only that conversation.
- Original design was wrong because: the waiting space was built as a second home for what the user hands over, with its own root in her view, its own rules for what she may write and delete, its own way of reading files, its own screens and a tool that opened a trip by itself once both halves were done. Each was right for the no-trip conversation, and each had to be told apart from a trip at every reader. A space a conversation can't be in needs none of them.
- Lesson: when a mode goes, remove every path that only served it in the same change, its view, tools' words and screens included, and keep only what old data needs to be read or moved (here: the scope, its archive folder, and Undo's way back into it), emptied by one rule at the moments new data can arrive.

## 2026-11-15 A booking's check names the part
- Changed: Koe's view's `check` cell (`checkWords` in src/core/bookings.js) said "not sure it was read right" for a booking with any field marked. It now names each marked field by what it is, as the screens mark it beside its value: "not sure the check-out time was read right", "not sure the departure time or the seat was read right" (a booking's times by its kind; a few keys whose own words mislead, such as `from` and `party`, by what they are; any other key as the words it is made of). A booking marked whole (one from before, not confirmed) keeps the one phrase. A plan row whose shown time is the same moment as its booking's start or end, where the screens mark that time or the booking whole (`timeUnsure` in src/core/plan.js; the times the view shows: Koe's plan shows start and end, the other views the start alone, so a sure check-in is not marked for an unsure check-out: fix round 1 of the review, F1), says "can't be moved; not sure the time was read right"; `fixedWords` takes `{ unsure }`, required, so every view that shows a constraint decides it: Koe's plan rows, Kokoro's now block (`nextPlanItems` reads the booking an item stands for, and takes `{ fallbackTz }`), the recap's plan (the journey's plan lines carry `unsure`), and the plan watches' wake texts. Nothing stored, and no screen, changed.
- Why we had to change: the 固定 re-review's second opinion (L2). A booking with `unsure: ["end"]` was marked on its check-out on the booking screen, but Koe's view said only "not sure it was read right" of the whole booking, and the check-out row of its plan said "can't be moved" at the unsure time. The user's picks (2026-11-14): "Fix at the source (Recommended)", for bookings "Yes, same fix (Recommended)", and the words "how people will actually say". The real check (booking-check, Koe and Kokoro on Haiku 5.5, "What time is check-out?" and 「チェックアウトは何時？」, 6 runs each, before and after): every answer gave 11:00 on both sides; before, 5 of 12 runs said a field word to the user (unverified, end, 未確認) and 4 of 12 called the time fixed (「予定にも固定で入っています」, "It's a fixed plan item", 「時間は動かせません」 with no word to check it); after, 1 of 12 said one ("unsure", relayed from Kokoro, who reads the stored file), none called it fixed, and all 12 said to check it. After, Koe's first words were more often that she would check (8 of 12; before 2 of 12), Kokoro read the booking, and the time came about 6 s later.
- Original design was wrong because: the 固定 fix moved Koe's view from the `status` word to the screens' answer (`checkOf`), but folded `checkOf`'s fields into one phrase for the record: the uncertainty kept per field in the data, and shown per field on the screen, reached the model per record, so she couldn't say which part to check. A plan row worked out from the booking's time copied the time without its mark, and read as settled.
- Lesson: keep uncertainty at the level the user acts on in every view, a model's too, and carry it onto whatever is worked out from the uncertain field: a phrase for the whole record hides which part to check, and a copy of the value without its mark reads as settled.

## 2026-11-14 OpenRouter's uploads are deleted from its own list
- Changed: the endpoint-type contract gains three optional hooks, `deleteUploadRequest`, `listUploadsRequest` and `readUploads`. Chat Completions gets upload hooks and the `file` part, its `uploads` tunable off by default and on in OpenRouter's description. Spec §4.8's "annai deletes no upload" now holds for OpenAI, Anthropic and Gemini only: at OpenRouter annai deletes its own uploads 48 hours after each, from OpenRouter's own list, by the name only annai writes (`annai-<16 hex>.<ext>`), at start, on coming online, once a day while open, and all of them as the key, the address or the provider goes, at Erase and when the switch goes off. Spec §4.9 says these deletions don't wait on the device as kept turns do.
- Why we had to change: the user asked for OpenRouter's files API (2026-10-16), and a real test (2026-11-14) showed OpenRouter's Chat Completions takes an uploaded photo by id. OpenRouter's files never expire and its upload takes no expiry, so without deleting, travellers' photos would stay in their workspace for good.
- Original design was wrong because: the type contract assumed every files API expires uploads by itself, which held only for the three providers checked then, so it had no way to delete, and §4.8's promise that nothing annai uploads outlives its use rested on the provider, not on annai.
- Lesson: where what annai leaves on a provider's side never expires, the provider's own list, read by a mark only annai writes, is the record of what to delete. A record annai keeps would be lost with the device, and miss what another device, a failed deletion or a forgotten id left. Sweep at moments that come anyway (start, coming online, the daily check an open page already makes, the key going), not on a timer after the act; and a switch for such a type shows only where annai knows the list.

## 2026-11-15 OpenRouter's uploads: a mark holds one fact
- Changed: the in-memory "uploads off" mark of `Uploads` (src/core/llm/uploads.js) is two. Key marks (`off`: a call's upload failed, or Test's own upload (any failure, as a call's); a sweep's request, or Test's read of the list, was refused; or Test's delete of its own photo got a definite no, a refusal or a 404. A sweep's delete that fails any other way sets nothing) are cleared by a new key or address, or a passing Test. The list mark (the list answered and isn't one annai can read: a 404 or any other 4xx but a 401, 403, 408 or 429, or a body it can't read) is cleared by no key or address change, only by a restart or by a Test whose own read found the list. One method, `Uploads.listed(endpointId, result, { test })`, applies the rule for the sweeps and Test, and `isOff` reads both marks and the provider going. It replaces `listFailed`, which service.js and check.js imported. The list read at every key save and the "no upload without a saved key" gate went, and the service's release (a key or address change, a removal, Erase) is as before.
- Why we had to change: the final review and two re-reviews of OpenRouter's uploads each found something in that one mark, and each fix to it made the next finding: a dead key at start left uploads off after the key was fixed, so a clearer was added at a key change; the clearer also cleared a list annai couldn't read, so the list was read again inside every key save; that re-read raced with an old key's late answer, a Test under the old key cleared what the new key's read had set, and a gate added for the keyless case disagreed with what Test and `uploading()` said. A fresh check of the design (2026-11-15) found the cause, and the user picked this change.
- Original design was wrong because: one set held two facts with different owners. "This key was refused" is the key's, and true until the key changes. "This server's list can't be read" is the server's, and a key change says nothing about it, so clearing it there turned uploads on where annai could not delete what it made, and each part added to make that safe (the re-read, the gate) was a new setter or clearer on the same shared state.
- Lesson: a mark holds one fact, set by what learned it and cleared only by what can know better. When a fix adds a clearer to shared state, list everything else that sets that state and what each of them could have learned; a clearer that needs a re-read or a gate to stay honest is a sign the mark is two marks. One question has one rule in one place (here `listed`), for the background sweeps and the Test button alike.

## 2026-11-15 A booking's read result carries its check
- Changed: `read` of a booking's file (the files plugin's, Kokoro's and Koe's) and `read_trip` of another trip's booking file end the file's text, on a line of their own after the JSON, with the check the screens mark, in words: "(annai marks this booking for the user to check: not sure it was read right.)" for an old unverified booking, "...not sure the check-out time was read right." for `unsure: ["end"]`; nothing for an old confirmed booking, `unsure: []`, or any file that is no booking's record. One helper, `checkNote` in src/core/bookings.js, built on `checkWords`, so the two reads say the same; each piece leaves it room in the result's limit. Kokoro's CORE line (src/core/agent/instructions.js) lost its old-shape clause and says what the user is asked to check of a booking is what annai marks, the note `read` puts after it, each said by what it is and never by a field's name, with no note meaning nothing marked, and never the status; `tell_koe`'s description says the same, shorter. Nothing stored, no screen and no Koe line changed.
- Why we had to change: fix round 1's wordings. b466a4b1's "a booking with no unsure at all, and not confirmed, is one they check whole" was read to cover `unsure: []` too (line 23 of the same instructions calls a booking she is sure of "a booking without unsure"): Pe, Pj and Ne, the bookings the screens mark nothing of, were each told to have the user check the whole booking (3 of 3), and its pass on an old booking was seen only after Koe raised the doubt herself. 9f8f5067's "a booking without unsure, if not confirmed, is checked whole", the tightened one, was line 23's phrase word for word, and an old booking was told as settled in both languages (Oe, Oj: 2 of 2). Each wording moved the failure to the other shape. The re-review (booking-say-f1-review.md, Q3) named the cause: the program already computes the answer once (`checkOf`, `checkWords`) and the screens, Koe's view and Kokoro's next-plan block show it; only `read` of the file showed raw fields, so she worked it out again from a missing key against an empty list. After (booking-say-f2 evidence, one run each, Kokoro Opus 5.5, Koe Sonnet 5.5): Oe and Oj, the old booking, were asked to check the whole booking with Koe having passed on first (no hedge to widen), Ne had nothing asked, Pe and Pj answered "can't confirm it with the hotel" and pointed to the hotel's own email, Qe asked only the check-out time; no field word or status in the six, and no "can't be moved" in any.
- Original design was wrong because: it told a model which of two shapes of a record it held (a key missing, a list empty) in words, when the program knew the answer and shows it on every other view; two shapes a model reads alike can't be told apart by any wording of the instructions that still names them, and the second place the answer was worked out disagreed with the first.
- Lesson: a model is never told in words to work out what the program has already decided: put the program's answer in the tool result that shows it the record, beside the raw fields and apart from them (so a write-back can't carry it), and have the instructions point at that answer, not at the shapes it comes from; nothing when there is nothing to say. Where a missing key and an empty value mean different things to the user, a model reads them alike.
- Amended in booking-say's fix 3 (the second opinion's leads 1 and 2 on fix 2, probed): the note's parts for a key of `fields` annai had no table entry for were made of the key's own text ("the meal plan"), and the note sits outside `theirs()` as annai's voice: a key with a line break (a parsed JSON key can hold one), one with brackets and one of 40,000 characters each put the booking's text into "annai's words" in the result, the last pushing the note out of the limit. Now a key annai has no words for is "some other detail", once, so no word of the booking's reaches the note; the source is `partWords` under `checkWords`, so Koe's view (which only escaped it) says the same. Its tables grew by the names annai files into `fields` itself (EXTRAS) and a booking's own `place` and `cost`. CORE line 24's "With no note, nothing is marked" was untrue after a write, a patch or a search (their results show no file, so no note), and in the file hand-over she writes a booking with `unsure` and reads none: it now says it of a booking she read, and line 23's "ask the user about only those" stands for the one she wrote. Lesson: what a program says in its own voice is built only from words of its own: a record's key, like its value, is outside text, however plain it looks; and "no note" is only a statement about the tools that carry one.

## 2026-11-16 A row's fixed is its plan item's
- Changed: `planRows` (src/core/plan.js) makes rows of its own where no plan item stands for something, and set `fixed: true` on every one of them: a booking's own rows (its start, its end, a stay's nights) and every set's end row and quiet line, a flexible item's too. Now a row's `fixed` is only what a plan item stored: `true` or `false` as Shiori wrote it, and `null` ("nobody said") on a booking's own rows. A set's end row and quiet lines keep their start's `fixed`; a booking's check-out, return or arrival takes the `fixed` of a plan item linked to that booking whose own end is at the booking's end (what FILE_BOOKING has her write: a stay or flight item with both times), else `null`. Every reader of a row's `fixed` follows from the one source: Koe's view says "can't be moved" only for `true` (`fixedWords`, so `null` and `false` say nothing); the Itinerary and Now draw ● ("Fixed:" to a screen reader) for `true`, ○ ("Flexible:") for `false` and no mark and no word for anything else (`constraintOf`, src/ui/format.js); the `plan-row` component's `row.fixed` stays a boolean, false on a booking's row (docs/plugins.md says so). Kokoro's next in the plan, the wakes and the recap read the stored plan items and never had these rows. Nothing stored changed, and what Koe may move was and is decided from the stored item (`lightChange` in src/core/koe/tools.js), never from this column.
- Why we had to change: asked "What time is check-out?" about a hotel booking with nothing marked and no plan item, Koe (Sonnet 5.5) said its time can't be moved (「時間は動かせません」, 2 of 2 runs before and after booking-say), and Kokoro corrected her about 8 s later (it isn't in the booking; ask the hotel about a late check-out). The user's answer (2026-11-15): "Only what was said fixed (Recommended)": Koe says "can't be moved" only for a plan item Shiori stored as fixed.
- Original design was wrong because: a row the program made for a record carried a constraint the record never stated: `fixed: true` there was a result nobody computed. While `fixed` only drew a mark, it read as "booked"; once a view put it to a model as "can't be moved" (the 固定 fix, 2026-11-14), the filled-in value became a claim she said aloud, and the half that read the stored items corrected her.
- Lesson: a row the program makes from a record carries only what that record stored; where it has nothing to go on for a field, it takes the value that claims nothing. A value filled in for rows nobody marked is a claim nobody made, and a model shown it says it.
- Amended in fix 1 (the branch's final review and its second opinions, probed): the first version made the value for "nobody said" `false`, and `false` is a claim too: the Itinerary and Now read it as "flexible" and drew ○ and "Flexible:" on a booking's own rows (a timed ticket, a car's return, a flight's arrival), where they drew ● before and the user had only spoken about Koe; and a fixed stay item with both times, linked to its booking, lost its "can't be moved" on the check-out (the item's end is taken off its start row, and the booking's end row, built from the booking's view, said `false`), which was the user's own "a plan item Shiori wrote as fixed". Now "nobody said" is its own value, `null`, which every reader keeps apart from `false`; and the end row asks the stored item that ends there. Lesson added: the value that claims nothing is a value of its own, not the nearest stated one: `false` ("flexible") and `null` ("nobody said") are two answers, and a reader that only tests for the first one turns the second into it; and when a view's rows are made from two records (a plan item and its booking), the fact belongs to the one that stored it, wherever the row is drawn from.

## 2026-11-16 An old booking is read as one with every part marked
- Changed: `checkOf` (src/core/bookings.js) returns `{ fields }` only: its `whole` is gone. A booking with no `unsure` key that is not confirmed (one from before 2026-10-29) is read as if `unsure` named every part it has: its own fields that `unsure` may name (title, start, end, place, confirmation, cost), then the keys of `fields`, through the same `has` rule as a new booking (a value that is there: not null, no text empty after trim, so a blank name or extra is no part); an old confirmed one, one with nothing to check and `unsure: []` mark nothing. Reading only: nothing is stored, and the file keeps no `unsure` until she writes one. What followed from it: `checkWords` lost its whole-booking phrase ("not sure it was read right"), so Koe's view, `read` and `read_trip` name the parts of an old booking; `timeUnsure` lost `marks.whole ||`; the plan rows (src/core/plan.js) lost `check` and keep `checkFields`; the Plan's "Check this" chip and the booking details' banner (and its style and the two interface strings, in every language) are gone: an old booking says "Check N details" (5 for the test hotel: its name, check-in, check-out, place and confirmation number) and each detail has its own check, N being the checks the details draw (`checkCount`, src/ui/format.js: a flight's from and to are one Route line, one check), so the chip and the details agree; CORE line 23 of Shiori's instructions lost "(a booking without unsure: write unsure: [])" and puts nothing in its place. In the files plugin's guard (`shioriBooking`), a patch that adds to `unsure` with `unsure+` on a booking with no `unsure` key starts from the parts marked now, so adding a mark keeps the others (appended to nothing, it stored only the added names and every other part read as right).
- Why we had to change: two failures of booking-say that four rounds of wording had each moved to another shape. Cj (the old-edit run): told "the hotel says check-out at 11 is right" about an old booking, Kokoro followed that parenthetical to the letter, wrote `unsure: []`, which cleared the mark on the whole booking after one detail was checked, and said nothing was left to check. And f3 review's F1 (Oe run 1): an old booking asked "What time is check-out?" was answered as settled, with no word that annai marks it. The user's answer (2026-11-16, to the chief session): "Build the route as one round (Recommended)", after a design check that found the state could go.
- Original design was wrong because: "a booking with no `unsure` key" was a third meaning beside "these fields" and "none", with its own answer type (`whole`), its own phrase for a model, its own mark on two screens and its own sentence in her instructions, and every reader carried a branch for it. The model had to be told in words which shape it held ("a booking without unsure"), and that vocabulary collided with the rest of the same lines (rounds 1 and 2: `unsure: []` read as the old shape). What the whole mark said ("nobody has checked this part by part") is fully said in the per-field language: every part it has is marked, and which part to check is exactly what a phrase for the whole record hid (the 2026-11-15 lesson).
- Lesson: when an old record shape needs its own answer type, phrase, mark and instruction in every reader, read it at the one place every reader takes as the new shape's answer with the uncertain thing spelled out (here every part marked), writing nothing back, and delete the branches; after that there is one shape to test and nothing to tell a model about the old one. A stored rewrite would be a second part on top, since a restore, an Undo or an import brings the old shape back. Measured (one run a cell, Kokoro Opus 5.5, Koe Sonnet 5.5): old booking asked its check-out, the time given and the check-out said to need checking (English and Japanese); told one detail is right, the stored file kept the other four parts marked in both (`unsure` of the four, and no "nothing left" in what she said); marked check-out, nothing-marked and the confirmed-or-not question as before.
## 2026-11-16 The dock says Thinking while Kokoro works

- What changed: the dock's state (`Halves.state`: what the dock's label and avatar, `ui.shiori.state()` and the `'state'` event give) was the talking half's alone, Koe's. While the sign says Kokoro is working (`kokoroWorking`, the one answer to that: Kokoro's turn running, from gathering the user's lines to its card waiting for them) behind a Koe that rests (`sleeping`) or waits for the user's turn (its `wait` tool), it is now `{ name: 'thinking', label: 'Thinking' }`, without "tap to stop" (a tap there talks to Koe; the sign's Stop stops Kokoro). Koe's own thinking, talking, listening and its question waiting for the user's OK show as before, and so does "Your turn" while Kokoro's own card waits for the user and Koe waits; with Koe asleep, the card's own words show ("Waiting for your OK") instead of "Tap to talk". The state carries `over`, Koe's own state's name beneath it, which home reads (`dock-busy`): Kokoro's work behind a Koe at rest shows on home as the sign alone, as before. The new state is told when Kokoro's change alone makes it (`#restate`, beside the sign), which also replaced the live session's own last-name check. A half's waiting state now says what it waits for (`wait: 'ask' | 'wait'`), so Halves tells Koe's wait from its question by a field, not by its words.
- Why we had to change: the user, 2026-11-16: "when kokoro is thinking and koe is ready, it currently says your turn. maybe that is confusing? maybe put thinking maybe? (then we have to think about what to put when koe is also buzy thinking)". Koe busy thinking already said "Thinking · tap to stop".
- Original design was wrong because: the dock showed one half's state for a Shiori the user sees as one. Koe, waiting for the user's next words while Kokoro worked on what they had asked, said "Your turn", and the sign beside it said "Kokoro is working": two answers to "is she still on it?", the label's the wrong one.
- Lesson: what a person sees as one actor shows the state of all its parts, composed in the one place every reader takes, and told again whenever any part's change changes it, not only when the part that talks changes. One part's "your turn" while another part still works says the opposite of what is true.
- Amended in fix 1 (the branch's final review): the first version asked Kokoro's own state name (`thinking` or `away`), while the sign asks `kokoroWorking`, and Kokoro behind Koe doesn't start its turn until 1.5 s after the user's input (it is busy but still `sleeping` meanwhile): "Your turn" showed beside "Kokoro is working" for up to 1.5 s after each typed line, 0.8 to 0.9 s measured. The label now asks the sign's own answer. Lesson sharpened: two readers of one fact must ask the same question, not two questions that agree most of the time: the dock and the sign each had their own idea of "Kokoro is working", and the gap between them was the bug.

## 2026-11-16 The dock's box takes several lines

- What changed: the dock's text box (`#dock-text`) is a `textarea`, not a one-line `input` with `enterkeyhint="send"`. One line at rest, as tall as before, it grows with what is typed to five lines (three on a screen under 600 px tall; a phone on its side keeps one), then scrolls inside. On a touch screen (a coarse pointer) Enter, the phone's return key, adds a line; with a mouse or trackpad (a fine pointer) Enter sends as before and Shift+Enter adds a line; Ctrl or ⌘ with Enter sends on both. The send arrow, now on the box's last line, sends; a box that scrolls shows no scroll bar of its own (`scrollbar-width: none`), which a desktop's classic one would have put under the arrow. An IME's Enter still neither sends nor adds a line. A long paste put back as text keeps its lines, on a line of its own after any words typed. The e2e specs that sent with Enter press Ctrl+Enter.
- Why we had to change: the user, 2026-11-16: "for the text box, currently it is a one line box? can be not what we want.... can you make it multiline? auto expanding text box would be ideal. also the keyboard button is send not change line, maybe should do that? as we already have a send button near the text box".
- Original design was wrong because: the one-line box spent the keyboard's key on sending, which the send arrow inside the box already did, so a message could have no lines, and a confirmation pasted and put back lost its line breaks (they became spaces, "because the box is one line").
- Lesson: a text box with its own send button leaves the return key its own meaning, a new line: a key that sends repeats the button and costs the box its lines.
- Amended in fix 1 (the branch's final review and its second opinions): the first version made Enter add a line everywhere. The user spoke of the phone's key; on a desktop Enter sending is the habit of every chat box, and nothing there said Ctrl or ⌘ sends. Now the pointer decides (`matchMedia('(pointer: fine)')`, read at each key, as dock-hides.js reads coarse). Put back as text also dropped a blank line when the typed words ended in a line break (`trimEnd`). Lesson sharpened: which key adds a line depends on the keyboard in the hand, and the pointer tells touch from desktop; one rule for both costs one of them its habit.

## 2026-11-16 The Undo line follows the turns that carry its hand-over

- What changed: each turn's `wake` record names the hand-over the turn carries from its start (`handover`, `null` for none; loop.js `#steps`, through `#logWakes`, whose optional parts are now one options object). The Conversation's Undo line follows her lines while the newest turn of the half that files (one Shiori's, or Kokoro's) carries its hand-over, and stays where it is once a turn of that half starts that carries none; the user's own words stop it only once that turn has ended (its `turn.end`, which the screen now reads, drawing no line for it), so an answer to her question, a line typed while she works, or one said to Koe while Kokoro files no longer does. A wake without the key (written before this change, or for a turn not run yet: a kept or a stopped wake) leaves the old rule in force, the user's words stopping it, until the next turn's wake.
- Why we had to change: the scope audit's row "After a hand-over, the Undo line doesn't show when her first line is a question card and she files only after the answer" (2026-11-14), against the user's pick for how she files, "She asks, I tap or say yes (Recommended)" (2026-11-11). The line stopped moving at the user's answer, so it stayed hidden under her question and showed only after a reload, there; and after a turn that failed or was let go, the user's next line stopped it before the turn that carried the hand-over filed anything, so the line never showed, even after a reload.
- Original design was wrong because: the screen guessed when her work on a hand-over was over from what it could see (the user writes or speaks again), while the loop decides it, by which turns carry the hand-over: her question and the user's answer are inside her turn, and a Try again or the user's next line after a failed turn takes the hand-over up again. Two answers to one question, and the screen's was the wrong one in every shape where the user speaks before she is done.
- Lesson: a screen that places something by when an actor's work ends reads it from a record the deciding code writes where the screen already reads (here a key on the turn's own wake), never from a guess at what it sees. The key goes on every turn's wake, `null` included, so a wake without it reads by the old rule (a kept or a stopped wake starts no turn and has none).
- Amended in fix 1 (the branch's final review and its second opinions): the line was looked at only when one of her lines moved it, so it stayed hidden when her last line came before her last write (she says she is filing it, then files it), and when her turn ended silent ("Her turn ended") or failed ("Shiori can't reach her model") after a write with no line of hers after it, or was stopped. Now the line is also looked at, and put in the list if no line of hers put it there, where her turn ends (`turn.end` naming the hand-over: one that took it up partway too, as a reminder's turn does when the user types while it runs and an older failed hand-over is owed) and where an error ends a turn that logs no `turn.end` (the card of a failed or silent turn; the quiet record of a Stop or a let-go); an error after her turn ended (a tidy-up that failed) is not hers. `turn.end` stays out of the 400 records the screen draws, read apart from them, so a long conversation draws as many lines as before. Lesson sharpened: placing by "where her work ends" means looking at the record that ends it, not at the lines it wrote; and a check that is cheap and idempotent (is there something to undo) runs at every record that could have changed its answer, not at the ones the placing rule happens to use.
- Amended in fix 2 (the re-review of fix 1 and its second opinions): every finding of both rounds landed in one part, the line moved by a stream of records under flags (`placing`, `named`, `ended`), so records that weren't her turn's moved it: another job's error card (a watch that can't be checked, the halves' quiet records), and a turn's end, which put it at the list's end, under the user's words when they had spoken during her turn; and an error that ended her turn didn't count as its end, so after a Stop the line went on to Koe's answer to the user's next words. Now a record of her turn is told by its own `wake` id: the line sits right under the hand-over's line, then under each line of hers while she tells of the work, and under the card of her turn that carries it; a turn's end moves it only for a turn that took the hand-over up partway (under that turn's last line), and the error that ends her turn ends it for the user's words too. One flag stays, whether she tells of the work, because Koe's lines carry no link to the Kokoro turn they tell of (a live voice has one id for its whole session), and a wake from before 2026-11-16 names no hand-over. Lesson sharpened: a screen that places something by an actor's work tells that work's records apart by the id they already carry, never by which records happened to come while a flag was set: another writer's records come in the same stream.
- Amended in undo-f1 (2026-11-17, the second opinion on fix 2): the two records her move tools write (`handover.moved` from start_trip, open_trip and move_file; `conversation.moved` from open_trip) carried no `wake`, so they were not told as her turn's records. A hand-over that joined her running turn, then her line, then a move and the turn's end ("Let me look at those.", "Shiori took 1 file to Kyoto.") put the line back under her line at the turn's end, above the move. Now both carry the call's `wake` (trip-tools.js), as her lines do; the readers of `wake` (transcript.js, budgets.js) read it only on other record types. Lesson sharpened: a key that readers tell an actor's records by goes on every record the actor's work writes, those its tools write through their own helpers too, not only the ones the main loop writes.
- Amended in undo-next (2026-11-17, the design check of the Undo line's placement, its option 1): every round of undo-card and undo-f1 landed in the Undo line's partway end. The loop decided which hand-over a turn carries at two places and wrote its answer at one: at the turn's start (its wake), but not in `join`, where a hand-over joins a running turn or the user's words take an owed one up into a turn that carries none; that answer came only with the turn's `turn.end`. A turn that ends without one (it fails, ends silent, is stopped, is let go) never gave it, so the line stayed hidden, in two shapes after a reload too; the design check found that a file handed over while any turn of hers runs is enough, then a Stop, a failure or a silent end. Where the `turn.end` came, the screen worked backwards with state of its own (`turn.node`, its reset, the partway branch), which ended the span at the user's words while Koe talked (R1) or put the line back above what was drawn since (F1). Now `join` writes one more `wake` record of the turn where it changes the hand-over the turn carries, right after the lines that joined (Kokoro's, in two halves, in her turn before what she files for it; `meanwhile: true`, no reasons, the hand-over, and `half` while she is two halves); the screen's wake rule reads it (a wake record of her newest turn that names a hand-over sets what the turn carries from there), and `turn.node`, its reset, the `ours && hers` line and the partway branch are gone. Nothing of it reaches the model: its readers pass over a wake with no reasons, and every request in four shapes, both halves', is identical with and without it but for the clock and the random ids, which differ run to run on main too. A log from before it still has the turn's `turn.end` naming the hand-over: the line is looked at there, where the span left it (under an earlier turn's card, or above the user's later lines and hers). This reverses "no new record shape is worth adding for a case this rare" (the Deferred entry, and undo-card's fix round 1). Lesson sharpened: the code that decides writes its answer wherever the answer changes, not only where it first gives it: a reader that sees only the first answer has to work backwards from the end, which a run cut short never writes, and the state it keeps to do that is where the findings land.

## 2026-11-16 The top bar goes: every screen it reached has its own way back

- What changed (timeline stage 8): the top bar, its six tabs, plugins' tabs and the experimental swipe between tabs went, and Now with them. The addresses and plugin APIs that named them:
  - `#/now` (an old bookmark, a history entry, a plugin's `ui.go('#/now')`) opens today's Itinerary of the trip in play (`#/trip/<play>/plan`), else home, by the shell's replace; Back never lands on it again. `redirectOf(route, target, { play })` takes the trip in play in an options object.
  - An address opened `?from=#/now` names no screen now (`now` left file.js's BACK), so each screen's own fallback crumb answers: a file's goes to its trip's Details, a booking's to the trip (by its name), an item's to the trip's Itinerary.
  - A plugin's `ui:screen` with `tab: true` is a link in a quiet row under the head of Trips (was a top-bar tab); a plugin's screen crumbs to Trips (was Now). `homeFor`'s take-over is unchanged, from home, the conversation and the Itinerary.
  - `now-card` and `now-section` are drawn at the top of the Itinerary's today only, with the same arguments; `plan-row` draws the Itinerary's timetable only.
  - The class `no-swipe` is no longer needed: annai takes no sideways swipes of its own; harmless where a plugin still sets it.
  - `experimental.swipeTabs` is no longer read or offered; its stored key is left where it is, read by nothing, as `annai.resume` was.
  - Settings and Trace, which only the bar reached, get a crumb to Trips, and home's head holds Trace and the gear; Driving's ← with nowhere it came from goes to Trips.
- What `#/now` costs, and why it is allowed: the Itinerary at a trip's address opens that trip's conversation (open-trip.js), so a `#/now` while another conversation is open stops that conversation's turn and closes a live session. It happens for a plugin's `ui.go('#/now')` and for Back onto a `#/now` entry from before the update. It is allowed: the design chose "the trip in play" for `#/now`, and a screen may open what it shows when the user moved there (2026-11-06, "the conversation has its own address"), which following an address is. At a cold start, before live mode has its rows, it may land on the standing draft's empty Itinerary, as `#/plan` does with the draft open; no guard, since live mode's `#play()` already answers what an unused draft is.
- Why we had to change: the user, 2026-11-04: "a bit messy screens and tabs here and there"; "everything come back to the conversation screen"; and, after the phone look, 2026-11-16: "yes timeline looks fine", the gate for the tabs going; of Now, the same evening: "yes it is fine removing the now I specifically told it to do that when designing".
- Original design was wrong because: the bar was the only way out of Settings and Trace in an installed iPhone app, which has no Back button, and Now drew again what today's Itinerary, Driving and home's happening card show. Removing the bar alone would have left two dead ends and an address that drew nothing.
- Lesson: when a way between screens goes, every screen it reached gets its own way back, and every address it had is sent to its new meaning at the one door every address passes (the shell's redirect, by replace, so Back never lands on it): old bookmarks, history entries and plugins' calls keep working. A plugin slot that drew on the gone screen keeps its arguments where its content went.

## 2026-11-17 A capped answer says it was cut
- Changed: nearby's answer (`src/plugins/places/index.js`) now ends with a line when OpenStreetMap's answer held 400 elements, the cap the query gives Overpass (`OSM_LIMIT`, one constant the query and the check both read; counted as Overpass sent them, not as the rows kept). It comes after the line saying the offline pack filled in for Wikipedia, and says: "OpenStreetMap's answer stopped at 400 places, not chosen by distance, so a nearer place may be missing. Ask again with a radius of N m or less for the nearest places within it." N is half the circle that was cut: the first of the query's circles that holds 40 of the rows that came, by the distance each carries, or half the radius asked where none does. In the same round, a Wikipedia that doesn't answer where no pack reaches the point is said too ("Wikipedia didn't answer; these are OpenStreetMap places only."), where only the pack-filled and Wikipedia-only cases had said so.
- Why we had to change: the lighter nearest-first query (2026-11-14, "Plan the lighter query (Recommended)") caps Overpass's answer at 400, ten times the 40 rows kept, and Overpass cuts in id order, every node before any outline. It shipped in two releases (ad9e22b4, b6db88d4) saying nothing: where places thicken sharply outward a nearer place can be missing while a farther one is listed, and Shiori read the 40 rows as the nearest. Chief's decision, 2026-11-16: "Accept, and nearby says when its answer was cut (Recommended)". The first form of the line said "half the radius or less", and the final review (2026-11-17) found it right in only one of the three cases: the query stops at the first circle holding 40 or more, so when an inner circle is cut, a query for half the radius is the query that already ran, and the same cut answer comes back after another wait of about 35 s (27 to 52 s). Its test for "elements, not rows" also passed with rows counted, since an untagged node still makes a row.
- Original design was wrong because: the cap was chosen to bound the download, and nothing marked its answer as partial, so a limit that is a fact about the answer's meaning was kept as a constant inside the query. The first advice to the reader was the general rule (half the radius), not what happened in this answer (which circle was cut), and a retry here costs the traveller a wait.
- Lesson: an answer that is capped says so, in the answer, from the version that adds the cap; the count is read from what the server sent (rows kept after filtering would miss it), and the test removes the thing the count must not read. When the line tells its reader what to ask next, it names that from what the answer showed, because each retry costs a wait, and it promises only what that retry gives (the nearest places within the circle named, few or none; what lies between it and the first rows stays unknown), and a retry that is cut again says so again.

## 2026-11-17 A cold `#/now` waits for live mode's read of the trips

- What changed (screens-next, stage 8's Deferred entries built): `Live` has `tripsRead`, a promise that settles once `start()` has tried its first read of the trips (right after it, or when `start()` fails before it). The shell waits for it before it decides where `#/now` goes, so a cold load there (an old bookmark, a plugin's link opened as annai starts) lands on today's Itinerary of the trip in play, not on the Itinerary of the conversation left open, the draft's, or Trips; a move made during the wait is the user's and is drawn by its own render.
- Why we had to change: the final review of stage 8 (a Haiku slice) read the race, and the e2e that loads `#/now` cold failed 8 of 8 on main in both engines: main.js starts live mode and then the shell, whose first draw came before live mode had read a trip.
- Original design was wrong because: `live.play` answers with a stand-in (the open conversation) until live mode has its rows, and stage 8's entry for `#/now` accepted that stand-in for the draft's case ("no guard, since live mode's `#play()` already answers what an unused draft is"), which holds only once live mode has read the trips; before that it is not the trip in play at all. The redirect is a replace, which nothing takes back, so a wrong first answer stays.
- Lesson: a decision that can't be taken back (an address replaced) reads a value only once what it is worked out from has been read; where a stand-in answers until then, the reader waits for the read's end, which the loader exposes as a promise settling on every path, a failed start included, so the wait can't hang.

## 2026-11-17 A plan item's own kind decides, read by one table of words

- What changed (leave-next, the leave design check's option 1, part 3): a plan item's kind, free text, is read through one table of words in `src/core/live/plan-watches.js` (`KINDS`), in any case and spacing: the booking format's kinds (`flight`, `lodging`, `car`, `rail`, `event`, `restaurant`, `other`), their everyday words (`plane`, `hotel`, `stay`, `train`), the rides `ferry`, `bus` and `coach`, and `meal`, the plan-a-trip skill's own example. An item's own word decides when the table has it; a word it lacks, or none, takes the booking's kind. Before, an item was of a kind when its own word or its booking's was that exact word (`isKind`), either one. Ferries, buses and coaches are carriers now, as flights and trains are (what comes after one doesn't count from where it leaves, and a fixed one is waited for). Shiori's instructions and the plan-a-trip skill say which kind a flight, a train, a ride and a stay's check-in or check-out carry. The stored format is unchanged: the kind stays free text, with no enum, so no plan written before is refused.
- Why we had to change: the design check of the leave part (2026-11-17), after four fix rounds in it: a car pick-up or a check-out on a flight's booking was a flight leg, so the flight home's leave reminder came 1 h 44 min late with no position near, and the dinner after a car pick-up 1 h 44 min late; a "hotel" or a "train" linked to no booking wasn't recognised; a ferry or a coach was no carrier, so the next item counted a drive from where the ride began.
- Original design was wrong because: "its own kind or its booking's" let the linked record override what the item said of itself, and each kind was one exact word of a free-text field. The item's own kind couldn't simply decide first: a flight written with a word of its own ("transport") would then lose its 2-hour margin (fix round 2's Call 3). A table of the words that name each kind is what tells "the item says it is something else" from "the item says nothing the reader knows".
- Lesson: a record that can inherit a field from a linked record decides by its own value when the reader knows that value, and inherits only when its own says nothing the reader knows; a free-text field that rules read is read through one table of the words its writers use, in any case, and the writers are told the words.

## 2026-11-17 A summary's language is one function's, which picks the lines it reads

- What changed (langs-next, the deferred entry "Summaries pick their language two different ways when no words decide it"): `summaryLanguage` (compaction.js) is given the user's lines as the log keeps them and itself leaves out annai's own lines for no words (`NO_WORDS`: "(files)", "(a photo)", a heard line it couldn't write down, an empty one) and counts each of the rest of 3 words or more that names a language once, for the language it is placed as (`units`, a Chinese, Japanese or Korean character a word: the size a stray short reply has; with a list chosen, a line in none of the listed languages doesn't count): the language with the most lines is named, a tie the one written in more recently; with no line counted, the first listed, or with nothing chosen the phone's answer, never a quoted line. The first version of the rule read the last three of the rest (`LAST_FEW`), which gave a Japanese trip the wrong language when its last three lines were "OK", an emoji or a place's name (the final review's fix round 1; main's recap read the whole trip); the second read the newest line that names a language, and an English trip's last lines "Where should we eat tonight?", "新宿", "ok" were Chinese and a Japanese trip ending "thanks" English (the re-review of round 1: a line of one or two words is a reply, a thanks or a name, not a change of language, and decides only when no longer line names one); the third, merged, read the newest line of 3 words or more that names a language, else all the lines together, and was replaced the same day by the count (summary-count-next, after a design check of its three rounds): a Japanese trip that ended "Thank you so much!" was English, three Japanese lines and a Spanish one with [en, ja] listed English (the first listed), and "Big Sur", 「はい」 French, read together; with nothing chosen, a script several languages share (Han alone, Cyrillic, Arabic) is placed with the phone's language as a listed one is (a Japanese phone's 「了解」 is Japanese, not Chinese). Compaction (loop.js) passes every line since the last summary instead of keeping its own last three, and the recap passes the trip's lines with no filter of its own. The recap's own branch for "nothing chosen, no words" (the screens' language) is gone: `writeRecap` takes the phone's language (`locale`, navigator.language from the Recap screen), and `summaryLanguage` answers "the language of the user's phone", as it does for compaction and as the Languages section does for her first answer. `PHOTO_ONLY` and `NO_WORDS` moved from loop.js to handover.js, beside `FILES_ONLY`, so compaction.js can read them without a cycle.
- Why we had to change: two callers fed one rule different input. Compaction left out "(a photo)" and the not-transcribed line but not "(files)", so three hand-overs with no words pushed the user's Japanese out of its last three lines and the summary was asked for in English (or "the language the user wrote in last: \"(files)\""); the recap left out all of them. And with nothing chosen and no words, the recap took the screens' language while Shiori's answers and compaction took the phone's.
- Original design was wrong because: the function answered "which language" from whatever lines it was handed, so which lines count was decided at each caller, twice, differently; the 2026-11-14 fix added the recap's filter and its fallback at the caller (the brief ruled out touching compaction's function), which made a third place. And each version picked one line (or the last three) to stand for a whole conversation or trip, so its answer was wrong whenever that one line was: a line of the traveller's may be a sentence in their language, a short reply, a name, a pasted text, a tap on a card or a model's transcript of their voice, and no threshold tells these apart; every finding of the three rounds was that. Counted, a misread line is one among many; the cost is a late switch (five English lines then two Japanese are English), which compaction's next window catches up.
- Lesson: when callers share a rule, the rule picks its own input (which records count) and its own fallback; a filter or a fallback left at a caller is a second answer to the same question. A rule that reads lines to answer for a whole answers from all of them, not from the one it trusts most: neither a window of "the last few" lines nor the newest line that says something has a reason of its own, and each lost the whole to one line. A line counts by a size that already exists for the same question (a reply of fewer than 3 words is no change of language), not by its naming a language at all, which "thanks" and a place's name do.

## 2026-11-17 The recap's highlights heading is described, not quoted

- What changed (langs-next, the deferred entry "Shiori's written recap gets no native-speaker sentence"): the recap's request (`RECAP_SYSTEM`) carries the Languages section's native-speaker sentence (`NATIVE`, now exported from languages.js), and asks for 'a last "## " section, its heading the trip's highlights in the recap's language' where it said 'then "## Highlights"'.
- Why we had to change: a real Japanese recap (Gemini 3.5 Flash Lite, Gemini's recommended utility model, a made-up Japanese traveller's day) read translated in places: "# Big Sur", "## 2026-11-27" and "## Highlights" in English in 4 of 4, and turns such as 「〜であることの案内がありました」 and 「〜を見ることができたこと」. With the sentence, the title and the days came in Japanese (3 of 3) but "## Highlights" stayed English (3 of 3); described, the heading came in Japanese too (「この旅のハイライト」, 「ハイライト」: 3 of 3).
- Original design was wrong because: the format instruction quoted the heading's English word, and a model repeats a quoted word as it is, even when it writes in another language (the 2026-11-14 lesson on her words); a heading in a format instruction is words to say like any other.
- Lesson: a format a model fills in names its parts by what they are and the language they are written in, never by an English word to copy; and a request that writes for the user in their language carries the same native-speaker sentence her answers do.

## 2026-11-17 A plugin update's rename takes a free name

- What changed (words-next, the Deferred entry "OpenAI's and Groq's speech models name their provider twice, and two same-named providers look alike"): OpenAI's speech models (provider-openai 1.6.4) and Groq's (provider-groq 1.2.2) no longer begin with "OpenAI: " and "Groq: ", since every screen names a model after its provider ("OpenAI / GPT Transcribe", 2026-11-16). `syncProviders` (src/core/llm/providers.js) gives a model its description renames the first free name, "name (2)" and on (`renamed`), as an add (`named`) and a user's save (`checkModel`) already did; a "(2)" annai gave it for its old name follows to the new one, and a name the user gave stays. With names unique at every writer, `modelNames` (config.js) lost its guard for a name two providers' models had. A second provider of one plugin, added with no name of its own, is "<plugin's name> (2)" (`addProvider`), as its models are. Roles hold model ids, so a phone that saved the old names keeps every pick; a line or a Trace entry that kept an old name shows it bare, as any name no model has now.
- Why we had to change: the provider-name rule made the hand-made prefix a second copy ("OpenAI / OpenAI: GPT Transcribe"), and dropping it renames stored models through the update path, which checked no name: an update could give two models one name, and the screens, which find a model's provider by its name, could then not tell which (the guard showed no provider). Two keys of one plugin were "OpenRouter" both times, their models told apart only by "(2)".
- Original design was wrong because: the provider was written into a field that names the model, before the screens showed the provider beside it; and the rule that model names stay unique was kept by two writers (an add, a user's save) and not the third (an update), so a reader carried a guard for the state the third could leave.
- Lesson: a rule a set of names keeps (unique) holds at every writer, an update's rename too, so no reader guards against its break; a suffix only annai adds follows the name it was made from. A name holds what it names: another field's value written into it becomes a second copy once that field is shown beside it.

## 2026-11-17 Tool results stop carrying `end` and `awake`

- What changed (turn-records-next, the Deferred entry "Tool results still carry `end` and `awake`, which this version doesn't read"): `#runCall` (src/core/agent/loop.js) no longer writes `end: true` on a result whose listening ended her turn (her line's window passed in silence) nor `awake: true` on a sleep that gave way (a failed call beside it, or her question came back), and `repair()` (src/core/agent/transcript.js) no longer stamps `awake` on a sleep it answers after a restart. The result's words still say why a sleep gave way, for the model. A log that has the keys (every release from before this one) reads exactly as one without them: no reader looks at them.
- Why we had to change: since the turn-end record (released 2026-11-15) the loop writes where her turn ends (`turn.end`) and every reader takes it from there; the two keys were still written in every release since (six of them, 78006090 of 2026-11-15 to dd8c41ab of 2026-11-17) only so a page one version older (a rollback, a tab not yet updated) would still read a newer log right. That page reads `turn.end` now, so nothing reads them.
- Original design was wrong because: her turn's end was stored as hints on the results of the calls that ended it, and readers worked the end out from them (and from her words); the record replaced that, and the hints stayed on as compatibility for an older page, through six releases, until they were planned out.
- Lesson: a field written only for an older reader goes in the release after the one whose readers stopped reading it, as planned work, not left to linger; a test pins that a log which has it reads exactly as one without it.

## 2026-11-17 A restart carries the hand-over of what it takes up

- What changed (turn-records-next, the Deferred entry "The restart of a turn annai started carries a hand-over still owed by an older failed turn"): the reader's `handover` (src/core/agent/transcript.js), which `#open` puts on the restart wake, answers from the records of what the restart takes up, kept in one field (`#carried`). A turn of hers under way carries what its own `wake` records name: its first (`handover`, `null` included) and a take-up record (a later `wake` of the turn with the key), from its first record on, so a turn cut before its first reply reads right too. A wake written while the conversation was closed (no key) carries what it brought (a file wake's hand-over), else what a cut turn before it carried, and takes up an older hand-over still owed only when one of its reasons is the user's (their line, a choice, a heard line, a cue: Try again is one), as `join()` and their own turn do, and keeps what it took up when a wake of annai's is written after it. A user's line that joins a turn carrying none takes the owed one up in the reader too, as `join()` does, so a page killed between the line and the take-up record (or a log from before the record) carries it. After a turn's end (`turn.end`, an error with its wake, a reply from before the turn-end record, a carry-on in another trip) nothing is said, so the newest owed hand-over answers, as before; an Undo of the hand-over a cut turn carried leaves it none. `owedHandover` (Try again, the user's next line) is unchanged, no stored shape changes but a move's wake kind (below), and `#open` is untouched. A log from before the turn's `wake` named its hand-over (2026-11-16) is read by its wakes' kinds, the closed conversation's rule: a turn of annai's own carries none, one of the user's the newest owed, as before. A log from before the take-up record (2026-11-17; not released when this was written, so every log on a phone then) reads the same way, from a typed or tapped line of the user's; a spoken one isn't marked as said in her line's window, which takes nothing up, so it reads as none. The no-file line of a move (trip-tools.js) is its own wake kind, `move`, where it was a `cue` (Try again's kind, which the reader takes as the user's): a closed conversation that took it read the older hand-over as carried. A log the version before wrote still has `cue` there and reads as before.
- Why we had to change: a hand-over whose turn failed ("Shiori can't reach her model"), left alone, was carried by the restart of a later turn of annai's own (a watch or a reminder firing, a plugin's wake) cut by a reload or a kill, and by a wake of annai's written while the conversation was closed: what she filed for the watch counted as the hand-over's, and its Undo took that back too (the turn-end record's final review, F2, 2026-11-14; run on both versions then, and again here on main).
- Original design was wrong because: the restart took the newest hand-over still owed in the conversation, a state kept for the user's next line (it answers whoever owes it), whoever's turn was cut; the records that say what the cut turn carried (its `wake` since 2026-11-16, the take-up since 2026-11-17) were written for the screen and not read for the restart.
- Lesson: what takes up cut work carries what that work carried, read from the work's own records from its first one (work cut before its first result too), never from the newest matching item of a state kept for another reader; a record written outside the work (a wake for a closed conversation) is read by whose it is, as the code it would have joined decides. A kind says whose a line is: a reader that takes a kind for the user's act can't share it with a line of annai's. A move's line was a cue, Try again's kind, so a closed conversation read it as theirs, and it has its own kind now; six other lines of annai's (Kokoro's cues among them) are still cues, and the reader takes a kept one as the user's.

## 2026-11-17 A keep that stops partway gives back what it kept

- What changed (share-retry-next, the Deferred entry "A partly added share's retry keeps the files kept before the failure twice"): `addFiles` (src/ui/add-files.js) no longer throws when a file can't be kept (storage full, say): it stops there and returns the paths it kept, one per file in the files' order, with what each was stored as (`types`, so nothing reads the folder again after the keep, not the wake's, not the type box's hand-over: a read that failed there once left the kept files in the queue to be kept twice, or left the type box's files kept and Shiori never told) and `failed`, the error; `keepFiles` and `bringIn` (src/ui/bring-in.js) read the calendars and wake Shiori for what was kept all the same, and pass `failed` on. Every caller looks at it: the dock's camera and the type box's hand-over throw it as before, the trip's Add photos or files says the kept part's cards and its toast, and then shows the error (its list of files stays as it was, since drawing the screen again would lose the error), and the share screen hands it to `addLeft` (src/ui/screens/import.js), which takes what was kept out of the screen's list and out of annai's share inbox once its calendars are read and Shiori is told. The inbox is the record of what is left: an add again, from the share screen (whose list and title are drawn again), its failure card, Back to the share's address or a reload, takes only the rest. The failed add says what it kept (a toast, and its photos' and calendars' cards); the failure card names the rest, which its button adds.
- Why we had to change: the inbox was emptied only once the whole add had worked, and a throw lost which files were kept, so every way back to the share added them again ("one (2).pdf"), and a calendar kept before the failure was read by no add (the retry read its copy). Fix round 1 of screens-next kept the count on the screen: it reached that screen only, and dropped the kept files from the calendar import and the wake, which work over one call's paths.
- Original design was wrong because: a step over many items that could stop partway reported only "it failed", so the caller could neither finish the part that was done nor take it off the queue; the screen's memory was then the only record, which no other path reads.
- Lesson: a step over many items that can stop partway returns what it finished with why it stopped, never only a throw, and every follow-up (the calendar import, the wake, the queue's removal) runs over what it finished; the queue itself is the record of what is left, an item leaving it only after its follow-ups, so every way back reads the same answer.

## 2026-11-17 Compaction and the journey read the user's lines from conversationLines

- What changed (lines-next, the Deferred entry "A live spoken line logged in two parts counts twice for the summary's language, and its late words are missing from the recap"): compaction's lines for the summary's language (`compact`, src/core/agent/loop.js) and the trip's journey (`readJourney`, src/core/after/journey.js, which the recap and the replay read) take the user's lines from `conversationLines` (src/core/agent/blocks.js), the reading the gate, a live session's start and read_trip already took: typed or heard and not set aside, a heard line once, with the last words written down for it (`heard.text`), so the recap and the replay show a live line with all its words. The types they pass it are one list beside it (`USER_LINES`). Compaction's `aside` set and the journey's `waiting` map are gone, and so is the journey's `kept` filter after its loop (a line with no words is still left out where the journey adds it). Compaction reads those lines after it has written down what was heard in the part it summarizes, so the words it writes at the cut count for the summary that holds them; words written down after a summary for a line it already counted are no new line for the next. Card taps still count (a tap is a line of the user's, as `summaryLanguage` says), and annai's own lines for no words are still left out by `summaryLanguage` alone; the recap and the replay show them as before. Shiori's `recall` (src/core/agent/self-tools.js), which joins the two entries itself as well, labelling a set-aside line, put a heard line's first words before the later ones, so it gave and searched "Is it" for "Is it far?": it takes the later ones first now, and a range (`from`, `to`) bounds the lines it shows, not the words written down for one after the range's end.
- Why we had to change: a live session logs a spoken line as a `heard` entry with its first words, and the rest after Koe's turn as a `heard.text` with all of them (src/core/koe/live.js). Compaction counted both entries, so the line counted twice for the summary's language; the journey kept a `heard.text` only for a `heard` that had no words, so it counted the line once, by its first words, and the recap read "Is it" for "Is it far?". With [en, ja], two typed English lines and one Japanese line logged this way gave a Japanese summary and an English recap of one trip.
- Original design was wrong because: each caller joined the two entries itself, by the meaning `heard.text` had when the caller was written: the journey (2026-10-16) read it as the words of a heard line that had none, the meaning it had before live sessions (2026-10-19) also wrote it for a line that had its first words; compaction's count (2026-10-23) read it as a line of its own. `conversationLines` (2026-11-09) joined them once for three readers, and these two kept their own.
- Lesson: a record kept in two parts (a line, its words written down later) is joined by one reader that every caller takes; a caller that joins them itself keeps the meaning of the day it was written, and a writer that later fills both parts is counted twice by one caller and cut short by another.

## 2026-11-17 sentences() ends the words it joins

- What changed (sentences-next, the Deferred entry "A plugin's or a provider's own words can still run into the next sentence, and a blank error reads \"Starting failed:.\""): `sentences()` (src/core/intl.js), the join every card and line uses and plugins get as `annai.l10n.format.sentences`, now ends every part but the last with `asSentence` (a full stop where the words have no end of their own: 。 on Japanese and Chinese screens), and keeps a space between a Latin end ("20s.", "Found.", "wait…") and a Latin start ("Haiku", "Rock pools") in every language.
