The callers' own `asSentence` before a join went (plugin-cards.js, plugin-install.js, check.js), and so did the hand-made stops (trip.js and offline-pack.js's `replace(/[.!?]?$/, '.')`, mic.js's test); the watch line (talk.js) and its Trace English (live.js) end the trigger's words with `asSentence` in place of stripping one stop and adding "." in the row. `asSentence` drops a colon left at the end before it ends the words, so a frame made with blank words ("Starting failed: ", "The watch for \"Tea\" can't be checked: ", "Voice A couldn't speak: ", the restore's lines, a photo that wasn't sent) reads as a sentence of its own in every language with no row for it (the final review's Call 4; the first build had given two frames new English and rows, "Starting failed." and the registry's, which went again); a failed update keeps its own sentence, since a name alone says nothing there ("Tide pools failed with an unknown error."). A last part is left as it is: a status line has no stop, and a line that may be last (a failed update's, the "Roles updated" toast's and the restore's failed lines, a model's reason in failedText) ends itself with `asSentence`. The announcer ends her words and a card's words before the choices or the links that follow.
- Why we had to change: each caller had to remember to end outside words before the next sentence, and rounds 1 and 2 of fixes-next and both its re-reads each found another caller that hadn't (the "Use the recommended models" toast, the restore's failed lines, "Backups to X stopped", the voice test), while the five hand-made stops each ended words differently ("Really?" became "Really.", 。 gained a ".", "wait…" a second stop). On Japanese and Chinese screens, words that ended in their own Latin stop were glued to the next name ("20s.Haiku").
- Original design was wrong because: `sentences()` read whether a part ended and, where it didn't, only kept a space after it, so a plugin's words with no stop ran on ("boom You can turn it off") and the duty to end them sat at every caller, the next one included; and it treated every ended part alike, so a Latin stop lost the space the language keeps between two Latin words.
- Lesson: a helper that joins outside words ends them itself (every part but the last), since a duty left at each caller is the one each review round finds missing at the next caller; the last part stays as given, as only its caller knows whether it is a sentence (a status line has no stop), and the same door drops a colon left at the end, so a frame made with blank words reads as a sentence of its own in every language with no row for it; a line that may be last wraps its own frame in it. On Japanese and Chinese screens a Latin end keeps its space before a Latin start (a closer or a "%" may sit between the letter and the stop, as in OpenAI's "…and 'high'."), as ja-style.md spaces two Latin words and Chinese typesetting does too.

## 2026-11-17 The Plan reads an item's kind by the reminders' table

- What changed (kind-next, the Deferred entry "The Plan screen reads a booking's kind first, where the reminders read the item's own word"): `KINDS`, the one table of kind words, and `kindOf` (an item's own known word, else its booking's, else '') moved from `src/core/live/plan-watches.js` to `src/core/plan.js`, with the word lookup `kindWord` exported beside them. `planRows` reads a set's kind by `kindOf` (it read `booking?.kind ?? item.kind`), and a booking's own rows by `kindOf(null, booking)`, so the Plan's sets and Kokoro's view of today, which reads the Plan's rows, answer as the reminders do: a car rental of kind `car` over several days on a flight's booking is "Pick up" and "Return · Car rental" at its place, not "Arrive · Car rental" with none, and a "Hotel" over two nights with no booking is a stay's set (Check in, Check out, nights), not "Ends". Fix round 1 (the review's F1 and F2): a plan item that is another thing than the booking it links (both a flight, a train, a car or a stay, and not the same; `THINGS`, `anotherThing`) draws its own rows (its set when it spans days), keeps its own end and stands for none of the booking's rows, which the booking draws itself: a car rental on a timed flight's booking was one plain row, "Car rental" at 10:00, with no end and no return, which stood for the flight's row and hid it, and is now its Pick up, days and Return beside the flight's row (a stay's Check in, a flight's departure and an overnight flight's Arrive come back the same way). A ferry on an "other" booking, a "meal" on its restaurant's and an event on an "other" one are the booking's thing as before. And `CARRIERS` (flight, rail, ferry, bus, coach: "does this kind end somewhere else than it starts"), which stayed in the reminders' module, moved to `src/core/plan.js` beside `KINDS`: the Plan's end row (`planRows`), `rowName` and Kokoro's `ENDS` read it where each asked `kind === 'flight' || kind === 'rail'`, so an overnight bus or ferry ends with "Arrive" and no place, the Plan draws no leg from where it left and Driving's strip, which lists places, leaves that end out. A ride booked as `other` (the booking format has no kind for a ferry, a bus or a coach) takes the ride word of a plan item linked to it (fix round 2, the re-review's R1), so its end is the same "Arrive" with no place; with no ride item on it, it still ends "Ends" at its place. A plan item that is another thing than its booking carries none of that booking's facts on the Plan's rows and in the reminders' marks (fix round 2, the second opinions' findings): its rows have no booking, so a tap opens the item and no "Check N details" chip counts the flight's marks (its link stays in the file), and `anotherThing` is exported so the reminders' `unsure` asks the same question. The item screen's `kindName` reads a word through `kindWord`, and `BOOKING_KINDS` names only the kinds the table gives: its copies of the everyday words ("plane", "hotel", "stay", "train"), kept equal to the table by a test, are gone. The stored format is unchanged.
- Why we had to change: two readers answered "what kind is this item" from two sources; the Deferred entry from leave-next named the shape (words only: the Plan's rows and Kokoro's view; no reminder or time changed).
- Original design was wrong because: leave-next put the table and `kindOf` in the reminders' module, the reader that needed them first, though the question is about a plan item and every reader of plan items already imports `plan.js`. Left there, the Plan kept its own older answer, and the item screen kept a second list of the same words with a test to hold the two equal.
- Lesson: when a change gives one reader of a shared record a new answer to a shared question, the answer goes in the module every reader of that record already imports, and the other readers take it in the same change; a copy kept equal by a test is a second answer the test holds together, so read the one table instead. The review of kind-next found the same on the next question over: `CARRIERS` stayed in the reminders' module while the Plan asked `kind === 'flight' || kind === 'rail'` in three places, and an overnight bus ended at the terminal it left; move every table the readers share in one change, not the one the work was about.

## 2026-11-17 A flight leg's times are drawn as written, with no zone looked up

- What changed (small-next, round 3, the design check of the booking details): a flight's legs in `fields.flights` (a model's list of objects, kept as written by `unalias`) are one line each on the details screen, and a leg's departure and arrival are drawn as the text itself says: the day and the 24-hour clock from its own digits (`written`, src/core/bookings.js, the reading `timeOf` already accepted a write by, moved and exported; the details call it in place of a second one), one day and a later clock as one range ("Nov 20 08:00–11:30", the dash held to the clock by U+2060), anything else each in full, and a UTC time, a word or text that is no date as the text. No zone note and no zone lookup: round 2 had drawn each time through the booking's own formatter with a zone guessed from the leg's `tz`, else the booking's start or end, else the trip's, and read a time written with a space through `Date.parse`, in the phone's zone. Every object, alone or in a list, is one line through the same function: a leg's number, route and times, then every other value with its label (annai's word where it has one, else the key as written); a field named like one of the booking's own parts that drew its line is no second line, and one whose part drew nothing is that part's line, as written, under the part's label (the skip is by what drew, for the five parts that have a line). `zoned` and the facts' `zone` field are round 1's again. The stored format is unchanged.
- Why we had to change: round 2's re-review and second opinion found, in the same part, a wrong day and hour for a leg written with a space on a phone in another zone, a connection's middle times named with a zone they are not in, one `tz` zoning both ends of a leg that crosses zones, several zone notes in one fact (so a bracket rule that let any bracketed URL widen the page), a lone leg object drawn as a comma list, and a field named like an own part drawn by a second set of rules gated by "there by value" where the screen means "drew a line" (so a part Shiori was told of and a field that could have been it both went undrawn).
- Original design was wrong because: it gave a leg's time a zone the record never stated, to make it look like the booking's own time, so every rule after it (the guess's order, a bracket rule, a second drawing for the fields that stood for parts) was there to keep a claim nobody made, and each round found another place the claim was wrong. An itinerary prints each airport's local time and no zone; the booking's own Departs and Arrives lines keep the zones that `unalias` checks.
- Lesson: draw a time the record doesn't give a zone for as the record wrote it, never in the reader's zone and never in the nearest known one: with no zone claimed, no guess can be wrong and no rule has to keep the guess whole. Where a screen shows what another reader checked (a write's acceptance, a chip's count), the screen reads the text by that same function, and a skip or a fill is keyed on what was drawn, not on what the record had.

## 2026-11-17 A line counts for one of the traveller's own languages by what its writing shows

- What changed (detector-next, the design check of 2026-11-17, option 1): `languages.js` exports `writing(text)` (what a line is written in by its writing alone: kana, Hangul, Han with a character only Chinese writes, Han that says neither, a script of annai's languages, Latin, or nothing) and `placed(text, settings)` (the one of the traveller's languages that writing leaves, `EITHER` for Han that says neither where Japanese and Chinese may both be meant, or null). `summaryLanguage`, `speechLocale` and `tagOf` ask `placed`; `summaryLanguage` joins `EITHER` lines to whichever of Japanese and Chinese has the most lines of its own. `listedFor` lost its `text` argument (only the stray note asks it now), and compaction.js no longer re-exports `languageOf` (`languageOf` stays for the stray note, the realtime check and `tagOf`'s Latin branch). 点 left the Simplified list. The summary of a Chinese line with nothing chosen names its row ("Chinese (Simplified)", never "Chinese"). In its own commit, the voice's second step: `speechLocale(s, text, { before })` and the speaker's `browserOptions(text, { before })` carry her reply's sentences so far, whose language a sentence that shows none of its own takes.
- Why we had to change: five Deferred entries and a question for the user, each one line read wrongly: 「Los Angelesまで何時間？」 as Spanish, "Los Angeles hotel" as Spanish, her Japanese sentence with English names in the English voice, a Japanese trip full of kanji place names summarised in Chinese, a Russian line placed as Ukrainian with Ukrainian listed first, Cantonese read as the first listed Chinese row, a Chinese trip on a Japanese phone in Japanese, 20 listed languages never named. On made-up trips under 24 settings the summary was asked for in the wrong language in 378 of 1,165 cells; each entry's own patch, all together, made it 388.
- Original design was wrong because: each reader asked `languageOf` which of the world's languages a line looks like (a letters-against-characters scale, one hit on a small-word list), then mapped that guess onto the list, with the list's order taking any script several share and a Chinese row taking any Han alone. The readers never needed the world's languages: only which of the traveller's own a line is, and for English, Chinese and Japanese that is a matter of script. Every fix moved the guess, so each round's findings landed in the same part.
- Lesson: when a reader must choose among a known set, ask which members the evidence leaves, never which of everything the evidence resembles, and let a case that shows none count for none; an order or a default decides only the last fallback, where its owner said order means preference. A guess standing in for missing evidence is a state every patch moves rather than removes (the entries' own patches together: 388 wrong cells against 378; without the guess: 97).

## 2026-11-17 A reader that must always answer takes "no evidence" from its own last fallback, and a reply's language is its first sentence that shows one

- What changed (detector-next, fix round 1 after the final review and four second opinions): `speechLocale(s, text, { before })` takes `before` as the array of her utterance's earlier sentences (the speaker hands the sentences over, no longer joined with spaces), and her reply's language is the first earlier sentence that shows one (for Han that says neither, the first written in Han). With nothing chosen, a sentence in the script the phone's language is written in is read in the phone's voice; a sentence in a script two listed languages share is read in the first listed language written in it, before the last fallback. `hanRow(text)` says a Simplified or Traditional row only for text that is Chinese by its writing (`writing(text) === 'zh'`), so 東京駅 and 時間 say none and `hanTag`, `speechLocale` and the summary ask one answer; `firstHan(s)` is exported, and `summaryLanguage` uses it for Han that says neither where no other line counts. `writing` takes a line with Chinese, Japanese or Korean characters and Latin words as Latin only when its Latin words name a language, and `placed` names a language from one small word when every listed Latin-script language has a word list. The Vietnamese marks `placed` reads are the ones only Vietnamese writes (`vietnameseMarks` stays for the stray note).
- Why we had to change: the final review found the voice read wrongly where `placed` had no answer ("no vote" suits a count, but a voice must always pick one): an English reply that names Japanese places (one hiragana in one name made the whole joined reply Japanese), a Dutch or Ukrainian phone's own-script sentence read in another language's voice, `[en, ru, bg]` Russian in the English voice, `[en, es]` Spanish in the English voice. The second opinions found English lines with kanji names counted for nothing or Japanese, French lines read as Vietnamese from â ê ô đ, and Japanese kanji 東 時 間 giving a Traditional Chinese voice.
- Original design was wrong because: the reply so far was read by joining its sentences and asking the whole question again, so a long part or a name's characters outweighed the rest; and the count's "a line that shows none counts for none" was carried into the voice, whose last fallback was the first listed language of any script.
- Lesson: when a reader must always answer (a voice picks one language; a count may pick none), its last fallback is its own, never the count's "no vote"; and the context a sequence gives its parts is read from the parts in order (the first that shows it), never from the parts joined, where a long part or a name's characters outweigh the rest.

## 2026-11-17 A ride booked is a kind of the format's own

- What changed (booking-next): the booking format's `kind` takes `ferry`, `bus` and `coach` beside `flight`, `lodging`, `car`, `rail`, `event`, `restaurant` and `other` (`BOOKING_SCHEMA`, Shiori's instructions' list of kinds, the design spec). A booking of one of them is read as a train's: its times are never taken in the trip's only zone (`unalias` asks `CARRIERS`, where it had a list of its own, `FAR`, of flight and rail), the details say Departs and Arrives (a train's said Starts and Ends), Shiori is told the departure and the arrival time, the Files row counts "2 ferries", and the Plan and the view of today end it with Arrive and no place, with no ride item linked. `RIDES`, the rule by which a booking of `other` takes a ride's kind from an item linked to it, stays for the bookings already stored that way.
- Why we had to change: the readers already told a ferry, a bus and a coach from everything else (`KINDS`, `CARRIERS`, the kind words in four languages, since leave-next and kind-next), but the writer's list didn't have them, so Shiori wrote a ferry ticket as `other`: with no ride item linked, it ended "Ends · Ferry ticket" at the port it left, with a leg from there; its alias times took the trip's only zone; and a rule had to borrow the kind from a linked item, which only a booking with such an item had.
- Original design was wrong because: the format's list of kinds was fixed before the readers learned the rides, and widening the readers' table (leave-next) left the writer behind; the borrowing rule answered the missing kind from another record instead of from the record's own.
- Lesson: a format's list of kinds holds every kind its readers tell apart; when a reader learns a kind, the writer's list takes it in the same change, or the data takes the nearest kind and a rule borrows the missing one from another record, which only some records have. A rule that serves only records written before keeps reading them, and writes nothing back.

## 2026-11-17 A zone written beside two times is the first one's, and a time's other name is read by what is booked

- What changed (booking-next, fix round 1): in a write or a patch to a booking, the time zone written beside the times (a top-level `tz`, a flight leg's `tz`) is the departure's only for a flight, a train, a ferry, a bus or a coach: the arrival takes its own `{ at, tz }`, or is refused in words that ask for the arrival's zone (`timeOf` in `src/core/bookings.js`). The names `arrival`/`arrive` and `departure`/`depart` are the check-in and the check-out for a stay (they were the end and the start for every kind) and stay the end and the start for every other kind. A booking's own `start` and `end`, written as stored, get the check the aliases get (a zone annai knows, an offset the zone has then), when a write changes them (`shioriBooking`). The key `arrival` in `fields` is labelled "Arrival" (it was "To") and told as "the arrival", the word for what a model writes there, a time or a place; the place of a ride's or a flight's arrival that annai files itself goes under `to` ("where it arrives", 到着地), so a ride's arrival time and its place are never drawn under one word. Fix round 2 (same change): a patch's `flights+` leg whose arrival has a time and no zone of its own is refused as a whole `flights` write is, naming the appended leg (`flights+[0].arrival`; it left the booking's end at the old arrival and said "Patched"), while a leg with no arrival, or an arrival that is an airport as an object (`{ "iataCode": "HND" }`), still appends and leaves the end as it was (the last appended leg's arrival, the one that moves the end, written as text that can't be placed, "HND" or "08:00", is refused; the legs before it are kept as written); a write that changes a booking's start or end and leaves the end before the start is refused in words (a round trip's arrival and departure written as one car or other booking were stored Nov 25 to Nov 20); two times of one write are the same when they are the same instant in the zone they are written in (`instantAt`), where an offsetless one was read in the phone's; a stay's check-out written with the hotel's own address is no drop-off elsewhere.
- Why we had to change: a flight written SFO to NRT with the leg's `tz` the departure's stored the arrival 17 hours out, a Stockholm to Helsinki ferry with one `tz` an hour out, and a hotel's "Arrival" and "Departure" were stored as check-out and check-in, so a stay came out with its end before its start: all wrong results, silent. A start written as `{ at: "…+09:00", tz: "America/Los_Angeles" }` was drawn as another wall clock than the one written. A time kept in `arrival` ("15:00–16:00") was labelled "To".
- Original design was wrong because: one table (`TIMES`) answered a name's field for every kind, and one zone beside the times answered for all of them, as if a booking were one place; a flight, a train and a ride are two places. The check was in the alias path, not on the field it stands for.
- Lesson: a zone written beside two times serves the first only when the times are in two places, and the second says its own or is refused; a name a person writes for a time is read by what is booked (arriving is the start of a stay and the end of a trip); and a check on a field belongs on the field, not on the other names it can be written under, so every way of writing it gets it. One word on a screen holds one kind of thing: a place and a time drawn under the same word read as a repeat, so annai files each under the word that fits. A refusal is for what can't be placed, whichever way the write comes (a whole write or a patch's appended leg), and a leg with nothing to place is still taken.

## 2026-11-17 A flight, a train or a ride is its departure and its Arrive, whatever the day

- What changed (carrier-rows-next, the Deferred entries "A same-day carrier booking keeps its departure place" and "The Driving strip keeps a flight already left as the next stop"): in `planRows` (`src/core/plan.js`) a booked flight, train, ferry, bus or coach (`CARRIERS`) with a good start and end is a set whatever the day (`setOf(b, sameDay)`: a good span that ends on a later day, or, for a carrier booking, one that ends the same day; one that ends on an earlier local day, an eastbound flight over the date line, stays one row), so a same-day booking is its departure at its place and an "Arrive" with no place, as an overnight one already was; the departure item Shiori writes for a timed booking, which main drew alone with the arrival nowhere, now has the booking's Arrive beside it. A same-day carrier's Arrive is stood for by a linked item only when the item is on the departure's day too, in the departure's zone (`standsFor`'s `also`): across zones, an item can be on the arrival's day in the arrival's zone without being on the departure's day in the departure's, in both directions (an item written the evening before the departure is already the arrival's day there, Honolulu 00:30 to Los Angeles 08:30 with an item at 23:00 Honolulu time; one after landing is already the next day at the departure's clock, Tokyo 21:30 to Los Angeles 19:30 on the same date with an item after landing), and the booked arrival, which main showed on the booking's one row, was shown nowhere. Plan items are main's rows: an item is a set only when it spans days, as before, so a same-day item is one row with its end, a carrier's too, and it never draws a booking's set (the first build and fix round 1 split same-day carrier items too: they gave up their ends, took Arrives other items stood for and drew a booking's set, and the booking's own rows went; fix round 2 took that out, with `drawn`, the look-ahead in `rest` and the item-side `setOf` kind). Driving's strip (`routeStrip`, `src/core/now.js`) leaves out a row of a carrier (`carries`, beside `CARRIERS`) from its start. Kokoro's view, which reads the Plan's rows, gains the Arrive of a same-day booking. The reminders read plan items, not these rows, and are unchanged. The stored format is unchanged.
- Why we had to change: a same-day bus booking from Port A then breakfast 393 km on drew a leg from the port; a ferry booked 08:00 to 10:00 was listed on Driving's strip mid-crossing ("Osaka port 56 km, 73 min") until 10:00; an overnight one's port stayed on the strip for the first half hour aboard; a flight booking with a departure item showed no arrival on the Plan or in Kokoro's view.
- Original design was wrong because: the drive-test follow-ups split a row by the calendar (an end on a later day is a row of its own) where what mattered was the kind (where the end is): one row held both the departure place and the arrival time, a state every reader of a row's place read as "the traveller is here until the end".
- Lesson: a row that shows a place and a span claims the traveller is at that place for the whole span, unless an Arrive with no place follows it (that is what makes a departure that keeps its span safe); a booked flight, train, ferry, bus or coach is two rows, split by what it is, whatever the day it ends (one that lands on an earlier local day, over the date line, stays one row; a stay is always its check-in and check-out, and any other booking is a set only over several days, as before). One rule in the module that makes the rows serves every reader (the Plan's legs, Kokoro's view, the strip, the Next line) where a skip in each reader would answer the question again in each. Split a same-day carrier only in the rows annai draws from a booking (Shiori writes the bookings too, but their rows are annai's), and leave a plan item to its own rule, a set only over several days, as it was: a plan item that was split had to give up its end, or keep it, depending on which other item stood for the Arrive, and could become the drawer of a booking's set, so every pair of items on one booking was a new case, and the first build and fix round 1 each lost times main showed there (a round trip, a connection, a start-only booking beside a next-day item, a stay's check-out). A split never loses a time: a time a row showed stays on that row, or on the Arrive drawn for it. The test for such a change runs the shapes that put two items on one booking, which the first build's tests did not, and a differential probe against main over random shapes.

## 2026-11-18 The silence is each server's, counted in answer.js

- What changed (addr-limit-next, the Deferred entry "Installing a plugin from an address has no limit on a server that goes dead"): the limit on silence moved out of `src/core/llm/official.js` into `src/core/answer.js`, the module for what is wrong with reaching a server: `QUIET_MS` is exported from there (no longer from official.js), and `quietly(get, url, read, init)` asks and reads one request, ending it once nothing has come from its server for `QUIET_MS`, and then throws a fault with the new `AnswerSaid` code `quiet` ("… stopped answering. Check the connection and try again."), whatever the request or the read threw. The clock is one per server, by origin (a Map of Sets, where official.js had one Set for annai's site): annai's site keeps its one clock (its relative addresses are one origin), and each address's site has its own. official.js's `fetchWhole` and packages.js's `fetchPackage` both take it. `fetchPackage` no longer passes an `AbortError` through as it is: the only signal on its requests is the limit's own, whose end says what happened.
- Why we had to change: an address whose server took the request and then sent nothing held Add's "Get it" at "Getting it…" until the screen was left, and its request, an Update from it (and every update after it in the list) and the update checks that ask it (to the check's own 45 s) until annai was restarted. The helper that ends such a wait for annai's own site counted one silence for every request official.js made: a second caller could not take it without annai's site's traffic keeping a dead address's request open.
- Original design was wrong because: dl-limit-next placed the rule with its first caller and keyed its state by that caller's scope (one Set for "annai's site"), where what it is about is a server: the same rule for another server needed a copy or a second Set. Keyed by origin, the one helper serves both, and the same keying keeps two packages asked of one site at once from ending each other, which a clock per request would.
- Lesson: a rule about reaching servers lives with the module that says what went wrong with a server, from its first caller, and its state is keyed by what it is about (the server, by origin), never by who first used it: the first caller's scope ("annai's site") is one key of many, and a second caller then takes the rule as it is. What it throws when it ends a request is decided by its own state (its signal aborted), never by the error the engine or a wrapper threw, which differs by engine and by wrapper. Its end also never waits on the callee to honour the signal: every await it makes (the request, the read) is raced against its own end, as agent/tools.js and plugins.js race theirs, because a request that awaits something before it asks (the traced fetch reading the keys it blanks) or a body an engine leaves open when stopped would otherwise hold the tap for good (the review of the first build, Haiku's probe). And what it hands a reader keeps the answer's own status when it isn't ok (a Response can't be built with a body for 204, 205 or 304, which Chromium and WebKit hand a body for).
## 2026-11-18 A write's result says what it left out

- What changed (booking-time-next, the booking time path's design check of 2026-11-18, its Option 1, with the main session's two calls): in Shiori's writes and patches to a booking (`unalias`, `src/core/bookings.js`), an end written under another name with a place (a check-out, a drop-off, a return, an arrival) is filed whole, a stay's check-out too: its name and its address joined ("Airport lot, 9 Other Road"; an object name with no address of its own takes the address into it), as `fields.dropoff` for a stay and a car and `fields.to` for an arrival, compared with nothing. A blank name or address is not given, top-level ones too; removing the alias removes the field it stands for and nothing it filed under `fields` (a hand-written `fields.to` stays). The comparison that filed it only when it differed from the place, and the pick of the first of `location`, `place` and `address`, went. A flight's end is its last leg's arrival when that is a date with a time (`written`; placed or refused, as before), and otherwise none: a whole write stores none, a patch removes the stored one, and the `write` and `patch` tools' results say so, in words that say what is wrong with the arrival and ask for `end` ("The last leg's arrival isn't written as a date with a time (YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM), so the booking has no arrival time now: when you know when it arrives, write end as { … }"); only what carries the traveller (a flight, a train, a ferry, a bus, a coach) takes its start and end from legs, so any other booking given legs (a stay, a car, a restaurant, an event, an `other` booking) keeps its own, and says so when a leg has a time, and a whole write whose first leg is text gives no start and says so (the first leg's note: "no start or end" when the last leg's arrival is a time that is left out with it, else "no start", beside the last leg's note when that leg gave no arrival); the `try/catch` that refused some such legs and kept the old end for others went. `unalias` takes a `notes` list for those words, which the files plugin adds to its result, as it already adds a path's case and a confirmed booking's delete. Whether a drop-off names the booking's own place is one exported function (`dropsAtPlace`), read by the Plan's Return row, the details and the check (`checkOf`): a car returned where it was picked up has no Drop-off line, returns at its place with its position, and a check on its drop-off is the place's. A restaurant's or an event's arrival stays its end, as on main (written alone it is an end with no start, on no day of the Plan: a Deferred entry in docs/limitations.md; the build's own attempt to make it a start moved stored starts in two review rounds, and came out). No stored format changed.
- Why we had to change: the Deferred entry "A booking's end place written beside its own place". A stay's check-out patched with the hotel's own address drew "Drop-off: 1 Test Street" under its Place line (a patch gives no place, so the comparison saw none); a car returned to a name and an address lost the address; a flight leg appended with no time of arrival left "Arrives" at the leg before ("NH 8 · Nov 25 06:00" under "Arrives Sat, Nov 21, 3:40 PM"), while "HND" or "08:00" written as the arrival was refused, and a whole `flights` write with such a last leg too. booking-next's review rounds found a new shape in these two places each round (RULES, "Step back when findings repeat"), and fix round 3's two takes each lost a value silently.
- Original design was wrong because: two parts answered on half the facts. "Is the end's place the booking's own?" was answered by the writer, which sees only the write, with one of three values; "did the last leg give a time?" was a catch with a refusal on one side and a stale end on the other. The first belongs to the readers, which see the whole stored record and already shared a module to ask it in; the second has an answer that is one call, shows no wrong time and is not silent: no end, said in the result.
- Lesson: a comparison is made where all its facts are: a writer that sees only the write files what it is given whole and compares nothing, and the readers, which see the stored record, answer "is this the same as that?" once, in one function they all import, the check's reader too, so a line not drawn is never a part marked. When a write can't give a value others are derived from, it removes the derived one rather than keep it out of date, takes what it can, and its result says what it left out and how to give it, where the model reads (the tool's result): a refusal is for a value written that can't be placed, not for an arrival that is no date with a time, which is taken as none (a first leg's departure, which the start needs, is still refused), and keeping the old value is a silent wrong one.
## 2026-11-18 The card's spoken-yes code goes; what the no and a kept line use stays
- Changed (tap-only-next, design check option 3b): the code that only served a spoken yes to annai's card is removed. In `src/core/agent/loop.js`: `confirm`'s `yes` option (`ask.yes`), the card's own listening window (`ask.open`, `from`, `until`, `YES_S`, `inWindow`, `AskLine.inside`, `#route`'s clause for a line begun inside the window, `#listenAgain`'s `ask.open` clause), the "last thing put to the user" signal (`ask.passed`, `ask.over`, `over()`, `cardPassed`, `#passed` and its three callers: a notice that asks, a reminder, a line of hers), the window's release timer and `#remindWaiting`'s `over`, a yes waiting while someone speaks (`ask.speaking`, `armed.yes`, the settle's branch for it) and the later copy's yes guard (`confirmHeard`'s `again`). Outside it: `Half.passed` and `Half.sounding` with Halves' two hooks, `LiveKoe.sounding`, `Voice.onBegin` and `begins`, `SpeakerDeps.onBegin`, and live's `begins()` call (src/core/agent/halves.js, src/core/koe/live.js, src/core/voice/voice.js, src/core/voice/speaker.js). Kept, because a no or a kept line still uses them: annai's no list, `askSaid` as a no only, `cardTakes` and `#askHears` (a chat Koe's no is the card's alone, written down for it when heard), the 3 s settle (`SETTLE_S`, `armed`: a tap in it still wins) and its new start when a line was just said (`confirmSpeech`, which a no said then still needs), the newest-decides rule, a no's own line's other words dropping it, the lines written with the card's answer (`own`) or carried with it (`also`), the rule for a line begun over her voice, `Half.speech`, `Half.card`, `LiveHost.heard` and `speech`, and Halves' `confirmHeard` from Koe's entries. `spokenYes` stays: trip-tools' `lastLine` tells a typed yes to the card from a line of the user's own by it, and the realtime check reads its records with it. 42 of read-trip's 245 tests went (the window's edges, the signal, a cut, unsaid or failed question), each one's outcome pinned by a kept test on the same voice path; five kept parts that no test pinned since the yes was switched off got one each (the settle's new start, the newest-decides rule, a no's own line taking it back, a no a tap overrode written with the card's answer, two kept lines carried together), each failing under a mutation of its part. The final review found that three of the outcomes counted as pinned were not (open_trip's card on one Shiori's tap microphone, voice off and on again with the card up, and a line's end reaching the settle's new start on each of the three voice paths), and listed ten one-line changes to card code shared with main that no test caught; fix round 1 added a test for each of the three outcomes and a test that fails under each of six of the ten changes. Four of the ten could fail no test (three parts): the lines the card took in her result and the tap's 8 s end taking reminders again are reached by no input; the mark on a line the gate sets aside is reached only by a line a second tap cuts while it is being said, and nothing reads the mark of a line with no segment of the card's own. They were remains of the yes's window, and the touch before the merge removed them (with the two lines that kept a set-aside card line's mark and hold, and the checks of the mark in the two lists of a card's lines), the whole suite passing as before. The same touch removed the call that held the tap's 8 s again when a line's verdict came while someone spoke (one of the six the fix round pinned: its test failed only when the whole line went, and passed with the call alone gone), because a probe over the whole suite reached it once, with the timer already cleared by that speech's start; the re-read put it back: a tap that stops annai's voice while they speak keeps the microphone open and starts the 8 s again (`#limitTap`), and the call clears them, so the call stays, one line as on main, and a test pins it.
- Why we had to change: the user's pick, "Remove it (Recommended)" (2026-11-14), after the stop rule of 2026-11-13 ("No more fix rounds on the spoken yes: turn it off (about 5 lines) and the card takes taps only."), which switched the yes off by its inputs and left the machinery in place.
- Original design was wrong because: switching a feature off at its inputs left its state running on every card (a voice listener, marks, a timer per tap) with no effect, so every reader of the card had to work out which branches could still run. The design check's estimate of the removal (300 to 400 lines, `cardTakes`, the settle and the halves' hooks among them) counted parts that the spoken no, a kept line's end and a tap's grace still use; removing those would have changed what the traveller sees.
- Lesson: when a switched-off feature's code is removed, list what each of its parts still serves after the switch before counting it out: a part a kept behaviour shares stays and loses only its branch for the feature. A branch goes once reading shows no input can reach it and a probe over the whole suite agrees (a probe alone shows only that no test reached it: on main the newest-decides rule and a no's own words dropping it fired 0 times in the whole suite, and both are live); each part kept is pinned by a test that a mutation of it fails.
## 2026-11-18 An update check ends by its parts, and keeps an offer through any failure

- What changed (update-check-next, the update check's design check of 2026-11-18, its Option 1, with the main session's calls): `Updates` (src/ui/updates.js) loses its constructor option `limitMs` and the 45 s on a whole check, with what existed for it: the counter of checks (`#gen`), `current()`, the signal that ended the app check's clock with the check (`over`), the return that dropped a late answer, and the sentence "The check took too long. Check the connection, and try again." in the three languages. A check now ends when its parts have: annai's own check by its own 20 s (`QUIET_MS`, approved as C113), annai's catalog and its words and the addresses each request by its server's silence (answer.js). The day's address mark is set beside the 15-minute one again, as the check starts. `checkPlugins` (src/ui/plugin-updates.js) loses its fourth argument (`current`). `addressUpdates` (src/core/package-updates.js) puts every package it couldn't check into `unchecked`, so an earlier offer stays through any failure, a 404 included: `gaveNoAnswer` and the `silent` mark go, and `fetchPackage`'s errors for a linked file lose the `status` and `unreachable` tags round 2 of addr-limit-next gave them (src/core/packages.js). Settings → Plugins and a plugin's page read the offers the checks found (`knownUpdates`, as Settings → Updates and Settings do) and ask annai's catalog nothing; `knownAddressUpdates` goes. No stored format changed: the offers and the marks are in memory only.
- Why we had to change: the 45 s fired only on a check still getting answers, and threw away what it had found. On a slow working link, an address that answered each of its four requests 15 s late ended at 45 s with "The check took too long" and nothing found; annai's catalog late with a plugin's words silent ended with no card; an address whose files took 70 s each was given up on at 45 s and asked again at each return, its downloads still running (7 requests in 3 minutes, with no update found). The reading of a failure's kind, added so a 404 would drop an offer, cannot be right in a browser: a 404 sent without the headers that let web apps read it looks like a site annai can't reach, so the same removed file kept the offer on one host and dropped it on another, and every review round found another edge of it (a cut answer, 408, a linked file's error). Settings → Plugins asked annai's catalog at each draw while its doc said it showed what the last checks found: a second answer to "which updates are there", which could disagree with Settings → Updates and, on a dead link, kept the screen waiting for the catalog's 20 s.
- Original design was wrong because: the 45 s dates from 2026-10-18, when a fetch had no limit and it was the check's only end. Once every wait inside the check ended by itself (dl-limit-next, addr-limit-next), a limit on the whole was a limit on a slow working link, which answer.js already rejects for one request with a measurement, and it needed a counter and a late-answer path so a given-up check wrote nothing. The keeping rule was split by a fact the platform can't give, where one rule (a source that gave no result keeps what was found before) is already how annai's own plugins are kept through a catalog that can't be loaded, and how the addresses' offers are kept through a check that doesn't ask them. And the Plugins screen fetched for itself what the checks already hold.
- Lesson: when every wait inside an operation ends by itself, a limit on the whole fires only on work that is still getting answers: remove it, with everything that exists only for it (a counter of runs, a "still current" test, a path for a late answer), and let the operation end when its parts do. Keep what was found through a failure by one rule for every failure, never by reading the failure's kind where the platform can't tell the kinds apart; and a screen shows what the checks found, never an answer of its own fetched beside them.

## 2026-11-18 A tap's 8 s are held where they are armed, and a line is a tap's by the microphone that heard it
- Changed (tap-listen-next, the two Deferred entries the re-review and the re-read of tap-only-next found on main; fix round 1 after its final review): the rule "a tap's 8 s do not start while someone speaks" is kept in the timers' `arm` (`src/core/agent/loop.js`), not by every site that arms them: `if (this.#speaking) return;` follows the `clearTimeout` in the card's `arm`, in `#limitTap`'s `arm` (which every tap's listening for what her turn waits on passes: annai's card is the first, her question waiting by hand and the wait tool waiting for typing the others) and in `#armQuiet` (the plain tap's, a tap's window; the window after she spoke keeps its deadline, which chatter neither ends nor extends). No arm, from any site, then starts the 8 s with someone speaking, and the end of their line arms them again (`#speechStopped`, the verdict in `#route`); the one arm without the check is her question aloud's own (`#listenFor`'s, on purpose: talk radio must not hold the microphone open), which `#limitTap` holds after arming (new with this change: on main that microphone closed mid-line). A tap that stops annai's voice while the user speaks, speech begun while the tap's microphone opens (a card, her question, the wait tool or a plain tap), and a card line set aside by the gate while they speak into a tap's microphone no longer have the microphone close in the middle of their line 8 s later. The hold call that `#route` made after a card line's verdict goes, its `return` kept (it keeps the dock on "Hearing you"); the first version of this change put the hold in `#limitTap` after its arm and said the gate's set-aside branch could not be reached by a card line, and the final review found the line that reaches it (a line heard by her turn's microphone before the card, whose gate call was slow), so the rule moved into the timers. `#route` reads whether a line is a tap's from the microphone that heard it (`#heard` passes on the ear whose session heard it) instead of from the one open when the line is routed: a second tap closes the microphone before the line it cuts is routed, so that line read as no tap's and went to the gate, which could set it aside (annai's card then kept waiting and its line waited behind it; Shiori asleep never woke). A second tap that cuts a line being said (the speech still going on, or the segment heard and its words coming) ends the tap's listening once that line is routed, and only if no microphone is open by then: a microphone open by then is a later tap's (P5b: a second tap cuts a line whose words are still being written down, a third tap stops a reminder annai says and listens, the user speaks; the deferred end closed the third tap's listening, so the dock said "Waiting for your OK" and their line went to the gate). A speech begun as the microphone opened leaves the state at "listening" and not "hearing", so the second tap now also reads whether anyone speaks (`flushing`) before it closes the microphone. The card's own timer closes only the microphone of the tap its listening began with (`p.ear`, set where the listening starts): a verdict that finds the 8 s over while a later tap's microphone is still opening no longer closes that one. No stored format changed.
- Why we had to change: the two entries were Deferred, fixed at the source in the next version: "A tap that stops annai's voice while the user speaks can close the microphone mid-line 8 s later" and "A line cut by a second tap can be judged by the gate". The deferred end was found by running P5b, the second entry's own test, on the first two changes.
- Original design was wrong because: the 8 s were held where speech was heard to start (`#speechStarted`), not where they are armed, so an arm that came after the speech began found no hold; the hold put in at the caller that showed the gap (a verdict while someone speaks) covered that caller alone, and a tap that stopped annai's voice with no verdict pending still closed the microphone mid-line. The same hold put in `#limitTap` covered its callers and left the plain tap's `#armQuiet` and the gate's set-aside branch, which armed the card's timer by another road. And `tapped` was read from shared state (`#ear`) when the line was routed, which the act that cut the line (the second tap's close) had already changed.
- Lesson: a rule a doc states for every path into a timer (held while someone speaks) is kept in the timer's own `arm`, not by every site that arms it checking: three reviews in a row (tap-only-next's removed and restored hold call, the re-read's PIN2, this change's final review) each found one arm site that did not check, and a check at a site covers that site only. A step that runs later judges by the object it came from (the microphone that heard the line; the listening an end was deferred for; the microphone a timer was armed for), never by shared state read when it runs, which the act that caused the step, or a later act, may already have changed.
## 2026-11-18 unsure's departure and arrival are read where the marks are read

- What changed (booking-rest-next, the Deferred entry "An unsure departure or arrival on a flight, train or ride marks nothing when its time was written another way"): `checkOf` (`src/core/bookings.js`), the one place every screen and every tool result takes the marks from, reads a name for a time by the record: `departure` and `depart`, `arrival` and `arrive`, `checkin` and `checkout`, `pickup` and `return`, whatever the kind but a restaurant's or an event's (every name `unalias` reads, through `fieldOf`: leaving starts and arriving ends, a stay's the other way round) mark the part they stand for, where the booking has that part (its own, or a detail named like it, which the details draw as the part's line when the part draws nothing: a flight with `fields.start` draws "Departs: <the detail>") and no detail of the name's own (`fields.arrival` that is there is that detail, as before; a blank one is none). Never `dropoff`, which is a place too; a restaurant's and an event's are read as written (their arrival is the open Deferred entry). Nothing is written: the stored `unsure` keeps the names as they were written, and `unalias` still renames a name beside the alias it came with, as before. The Departs or Arrives line then carries the check, the Plan's chip counts it, and her read result names "the departure time" or "the arrival time". The write and patch tools' results are unchanged. A stay's or a car's `unsure` of a time name alone, in a patch, marks its part too (it marked nothing on main).
- Why we had to change: Shiori said she wasn't sure of a flight's arrival but wrote its time another way: through its legs, as `end`, under the other alias (`arrival` beside `unsure: ["arrive"]`), or in an earlier write (a patch of `unsure` alone). The booking was stored with `unsure: ["arrival"]`, which names nothing it has, so no line was checked, the chip didn't count it, and her read result said nothing was marked: she said she wasn't sure, and the traveller wasn't told. The same held for every such booking already stored (by an older annai, restored from a backup, imported, or edited by the user).
- Original design was wrong because: the rename was the writer's, which sees one write, and it had three homes, each leaving a route the writer can't see. In `unalias` a name was renamed only beside the alias it came with. booking-time-next's touch moved it into the legs block, and its re-read found three faults there (a `??=` chain that skipped the second name, the rename hanging on legs being present, a patch of `unsure` alone never reaching it). This branch's first build put it in `shioriBooking`, on the record as it will be stored: it covered every route a write comes by, and left every booking the writer never touches (stored before, restored, imported, edited by the user) unmarked until she next wrote it, with the Deferred entry that described them removed. Which time a name stands for is a question of the record as it is now (has it a start, an end, a detail of that name?), asked where the marks are read, which also answers for an end added after her write.
- Lesson: a name given for a part by another of its names is read where the marks are read, by the record as it is, so every booking takes the one reading, whoever stored it and however its time was written, and nothing is written back (a stored rewrite would be a second part: a restore or an import brings the old shape back). Only a name that would mark nothing is read as the time (a detail of that name is that detail), so the reading adds a mark and never moves one.
## 2026-11-18 A patch's lone item is read as the item the store will hold, or not at all

- What changed (booking-rest-next, fix round 2; the Deferred entry "A single flight given as `flights` (not a list) in a whole write gives no times and says nothing"): one line in the code. The rule built for that entry (a lone value under `flights` read as a list of one in a whole write, a blank one as none, a patch's lone value as no leg) came out of the branch, back to main's: only a `+` key's lone value is one leg (`unalias`, `src/core/bookings.js`), except that a blank `flights+` value is none (a stored end stays; main read it as a leg with no arrival and removed the end, saying so), which round 1's guard had and is independent of the lone-value rule. The Deferred entry came back with what both rounds learned, and its fix is for the next version.
- Why we had to change: the rule drew a finding in each of two review rounds, from one cause. Round 0 (the final review and the second opinions): a patch's partial object (`{ flight: "AS 2" }`, only a departure, only an arrival) was read as the whole leg, so it was refused for its missing departure or the stored arrival was removed with a false note, though the store merges an object key by key and the stored leg is what counts. Round 1 limited the rule to whole writes, and the re-review found the reverse: a flight written with a lone leg (its times set by the rule) and patched with a new lone leg kept its old Departs, Arrives and Plan day while the Flights line showed the new leg, and nothing was said. The re-review saw both come from the same part (the writer sees the patch), so under RULES' "Step back when findings repeat" the rule came out instead of a third patch.
- Original design was wrong because: the writer (`unalias`) sees the patch, not the leg the store will hold once the patch is merged into the stored one, and a lone value in a patch can be a whole leg or part of one only against that stored leg. Reading it as the whole leg or as no leg are two guesses at what only the stored record knows; each fixed the other's shape and broke its own.
- Lesson: a writer that derives from a patch needs the item the store will hold: the lone value is read as the item the merge gives (the stored item merged with the patch's value), in a whole write and in a patch, or not read at all. A rule that needs the stored item is built with the stored item passed to the writer (here from `ownOf`, as the kind already is); until then the value stays as it was, and one Deferred entry holds the tried fix, its costs and its tests, so the next version starts from what two rounds found, not from the one-line change that looked enough.

## 2026-11-18 One deadline per microphone, armed where speech ends; a line is a tap's by how it began
- Changed (tap-ear-next, the tap-listening design check's option 1, after two branches' reviews kept finding shapes on one part): in `src/core/agent/loop.js` the open microphone (`#ear`, typed `Ear`) carries its own end: `by` (a tap's 8 s from the tap, or from the end of the turn it served; the window after she spoke, her last words plus Listen for an answer; 0 for her turn's) and `timer`, armed by one function, `#armEar`, which replaces `#armQuiet`, `#limitTap` and annai's card's own `arm`, `hold` and `shut`. It is never armed while someone speaks, it is armed where speech ends (a line's segment in `#heard`, a click's end in `#speechStopped`), never by a verdict (the three arms in `#route` went), and it closes only its own microphone. Her own listening's time (`#listenFor`) follows the same rule, and since the listen-end design check (option C) it ends by kind: her question's microphone closes at its time (whatever opened it) and the question waits on by hand as the same pending; speak as her reply's last call closes its microphone too, if it is her turn's own; and speak's and wait's end waits only for the line being routed now (`#routing`, one verdict at most), so a line heard in time still answers and a radio cannot hold them open. `#closeEar` is the door every close passes: a microphone that closes under a listening ends that listening after the lines it heard, unless something ended it first (voice going off ends it at once) or a later tap's microphone serves it by then (`p.ear`, set wherever a listening starts, `#listenAgain` included); `tap()`'s deferred end, `flushing`, its `!this.#ear` guard and `#tapEnded` went with it. A line is a tap's by how it began (`#start.tap`, from `ear.tap`, which a tap sets, also on a microphone open as it is made, and which goes false when her turn takes the microphone, `#turnsEar`): such a line never goes to the gate, whether or not she is busy by the time it is routed. `#route` reads the microphone that heard a line for the after-window mark, and turns only that one, if still open, into her turn's. `byTap` reads what it means, "no listening of her own" (`!p.arm`), so `p.by` went; `#listenAgain` waits for `#hearing`. The size went down. No stored format changed.
- Why we had to change: two Deferred entries ("A tap's line can still be judged by the gate in three shapes", "`#limitTap`'s 8 s can close a later tap's microphone") and two approved limitations ("The tap's own window, with no turn running, starts its 8 seconds again on every noise", "A live session taking the microphone while someone speaks into a tap's listening for her question leaves it marked listening") all landed on the tap's listening after tap-only-next and tap-listen-next, and the design check found that three pieces of state carried every finding.
- Original design was wrong because: a timer armed by a verdict is armed later than the line it judges, when someone else may already be speaking (PIN1, race2, P5b, D1, shape c and the after-window regression were each "a verdict armed a timer while a later line was said"); two of the three tap timers lived on the wait and closed whatever microphone was open, not their own; and "is this a tap's line" and "which listening is it for" were read when the line was routed, after the owner had flipped (shape b), she had got busy (shape b), or the listening had been ended first (shape a). The previous lesson (a line is a tap's by the microphone that heard it) moved the read to the right object but still read it late: the microphone's owner changes while lines are on their way.
- Lesson: a limit re-armed by a later event (a verdict) is armed at a moment that event knows nothing of, when the next line may already be under way: arm a limit only where its own condition is known to hold (a line's end, a click's end: nobody speaks), keep one timer per resource, on the resource (cleared at its close, so it can only end its own), and judge an input by what was true as it began, never by state read when it is handled, which the resource's owner may have changed since; what depends on a resource ends at the one door every close of it passes, after the work already taken from it. When the findings of several rounds land on one part, find the state they all need before patching the next shape: here three timers, three verdict-time arms and a deferred end went for one deadline on the microphone.
## 2026-11-18 A draw again waits for what the traveller is doing

- What changed (form-keep-next; the open item "The plugin form's redraw can lose a typed address on a slow phone"): the shell's `render()` (`ctx.render()` to every screen, `ui.go` to the address on show and a provider setup's `ui.render()` to plugins, and `shell.render` to the app's start) keeps its signature and its promise (it ends when the screen is drawn, when a newer draw lands, or at once when a tap ended it), but a draw again of the screen on show now waits while it would take from under the traveller's hands what they did there since their last tap on a button, a link or a submit input. Capturing listeners on `main` number it all in order (`beat`): typing or choosing in a control (`typedAt`, until a save says the control is `stored`), the caret put in a field (`enteredAt`), a tap (`tappedAt`). The draw waits, looking every 500 ms, while a control typed or chosen in since the last tap is not stored, or the caret is in a field entered since then; a tap made meanwhile ends it without drawing (the tap's own action draws, if it does), a newer draw ends the wait when it begins and the draw's promise when that one lands, and a move is never held, nor is a draw asked while a move is being drawn. What a tap used up (a Save's key, an Add's model id) is not waited for. `redraw`, the draw nobody asked for (a plugin that finished starting or added a page, an update check that ended, a conversation that moved), passes `unasked`: it counts no tap, so it waits for all that was typed or chosen, the caret in any field, an error shown and a list being dragged, at the same door, and a tap does not end it: it lands once nothing holds it (a plugin that finished starting shows once the key typed meanwhile is saved). Its own wait outside the door (`typing`, with its unsaved-key clause) is gone. The app's two unsolicited draws in `src/app/main.js` (the plugins that finished starting, the sign-in that finished) call `shell.redraw` where they called `render`. `autosave` (`dom.js`) sends `stored` on its control once a save lands with nothing typed since it read the value (a refused or failed one sends none, so the screen with its words stays), and a plugin's key Save (`settings-form.js`), Shiori's quiet hours (`shiori.js`) and the plugin search (`finder`, `add.js`, whose query and chips are kept in the screen together, as a fold is, for the draws of the one screen on show) and a provider page's model filter send it too (the page keeps neither the filter's text nor the list it filters: a draw of the page drops both, and the list is one tap on Fetch model list away). The screens that disabled a button or showed busy words for their work (Add model, Save and test, Install it, the trip's Save) now put them back when that work ends, not when the screen is drawn again; a listed Add is not put back but replaced by "Added" once its draw has ended (and the list owns the model from the tap, so a row drawn again never offers it; and an add, from a list or by id, is refused with words when the provider's ability has a model with that provider model id, `refuseHeld` in `providers.js`, since a list drawn before another add cannot know it), and Update becomes "Updated" once the update went through (it comes back only when the update failed or asked first); a Save and test whose place was left before the check ended keeps its result for the place's next opening, and the trip's Save records what it saved; a key Save's "Saving…" (`savingNote`) still ends with its draw, which settings.spec.js pins (it must not go before the field shows the key saved). Nothing is copied from one screen to the next.
- Why we had to change: on a phone that starts its plugins slowly, the traveller opens Settings → Plugins → a plugin, types its server address, and the draw "show which plugins are now running" replaced the page: the text, the caret and the keyboard were gone (reproduced in Chromium and WebKit on main). The same happened when their own Turn on or Update ended while they typed in the plugin's setting, when MCP's Refresh tools ended while they typed a new server's address, to a key typed and not saved, and on Add to an address typed and let go. The first build kept the field with the caret by waiting and copied the other typed fields into the new screen by id; its review and three second opinions found nine more shapes, all in the same part: a draw that waited landed at the traveller's next field (the keys typed there lost) or their next tap (Add server's error and the typed text gone, Remove disarmed), a field entered and not yet typed in was replaced, a choice went back while the text came over, a token was copied into a field its choice hid, two fields sharing an id got each other's text, a refused value came back without its words, a value whose save was under way came over with its failure lost or stayed stale after the store changed, text typed again during its save was dropped, and a draw asked during a move copied the old screen's text into the new one or drew twice.
- Original design was wrong because: the guard against drawing over what is typed was a second verb that each caller had to pick over the plain `render`, and it looked only at the caret. Its first replacement still drew the screen while the traveller was working on it and tried to carry their work over: every kind of control, every id, every save in flight and every choice a field depends on needed a rule of its own, and a copy is still a new screen under their hands. A draw that waited also judged "typed since the last tap" by the live tap, so the traveller's next tap let it land on that tap's own result. The fix for that left callers behind: a screen that disabled its button for its work and left the draw to put it back (the draw replaces the screen) was left with a dead button when a tap ended the draw (a model's id typed after the tap on Add model, a backup place's field changed while Save and test ran), found by the re-review of the first fix round.
- Lesson: a rule about what a draw replaces is kept in the one function every draw goes through, decided from what the traveller has done there, not left to each caller to ask for. It keys on the traveller's own sequence: a draw waits while what they did since their last tap is on the screen and not stored, and the screen they are working on is left alone, never rebuilt and patched with a copy of their work. A tap is the traveller deciding: it uses up what came before it (no caller clears its fields), and it ends a draw an earlier tap asked for that waited, which would otherwise land on the new tap's result; what that draw would have shown appears at the next draw. A draw nobody asked for used nothing up: it waits for all of it, through taps, and lands once nothing holds it. A tap that left the caret in its field (a screen reader's press, a script's click: model-adds.spec.js's double tap) is a tap, and an Enter in a form taps its button (implicit submission fires a click on it: tested in both engines). A save says when what was typed is stored, and only then; the screen does not guess it from the field. A draw that waited can end without coming (a tap ended it): so what a caller set for its own work (a disabled button, "Testing…") ends with that work, not with the draw that was to replace it; where a second tap would do harm (a listed model added again as "name (2)"), the control is replaced by what is true ("Added", "Updated") once the draw has ended or the work has gone through.
## 2026-11-18 One model for each provider model id

- What changed (form-keep-next, touches 2 and 3, and model-list-next): `addListedModel` and `addModelById` (`src/core/llm/providers.js`), the two adds the Models screens call, are refused when the provider's ability already has a model with that provider model id (`refuseHeld`), in words the traveller sees: `"<id>" is already added here.` (`shown` and `message` alike, translated in ja, zh-Hans and zh-Hant). The check runs in `takingNames` after the config read and before `saveModel`, the one write, so a refusal writes nothing and the lock is free for the next add. It runs before the name check: an add by id of a model that is held now says "already added here", and "already used by another model" stays for a name that clashes under another id, or on another provider. The refusal is new behaviour of two API calls (`llm.addListedModel`, `llm.addModelById`), which used to add a second model: from a list, "name (2)"; by id, a model with its own record whenever the first one was not named by its id (it was added from a list under the list's name, or renamed). The refusal also says `held` (a property of the error beside `shown`), which no other refusal says; the provider page's listed Add reads it and replaces its button in its row by the words the list gives a model already added ("Already added, on <provider>"), and draws nothing else, so a field typed elsewhere on the page keeps its text. Any other refusal (a provider that is gone) puts the row's Add back, as before. Touches 2 and 3 had kept the fetched list and the filter's text across draws of the provider page, dropped the list at each change of its source (a Save, Remove key, the ability's own address) and drawn the page after a refused Add; the findings of two rounds were all that copy of the list against the rows on screen, and model-list-next took it out: the page is as it was before touch 2 (a draw of the page drops the filter and the list, and Fetch model list is one tap). Not changed, and open or on purpose: the model's own page can still save a provider id that another model has (the traveller typed it there), and a plugin update's sync of a provider's description (`syncProviders`) can still make a second model of one id (the Deferred entry "A model the traveller added can be added again under its id or its name", with the listed row's gate on the display name and the by-id add's refusal for a name; built by models-next, the entry "One rule, the provider model id" below). A restore or an import brings settings whole, with no merge.
- Why we had to change: a list drawn before another add can't know it. The traveller adds `qwen3.8-flash` by id while the provider's list is on show, then taps the list's Add for it: the list's own record of what is owned (a set it patches at each tap) was right for what it saw, and a second model "qwen3.8-flash (2)" of the one server model was made, with its own record, template layer and place in the roles. The reverse (a list added it under the list's name, then Add model by id of its id) passed the name check, which sees names and not ids. Found by the second opinion on touch 1.
- Original design was wrong because: the rule "each provider model id is one model on an ability", which the list screen shows by leaving out what is owned, was kept only by what a view knew when it was drawn, and by a name check that compares names. The one place that sees every add, the write inside the lock, did not hold it, so every route that did not go through the view that knew (a stale list, an add by id, a second tap) made a second model.
- Lesson: a rule the screens show (each id once on an ability) is kept where the model is written, in the lock the writes already take, never by what a view drawn earlier knows; the view keeps its own record only to be quick, and the write refuses in words the traveller sees. A tap that is refused is an action like any other, so what the screen shows agrees with the toast: the row that was tapped says so in its own words, and the page is not drawn for it (a draw takes what the traveller typed elsewhere on it). What the write's rule leaves open (a sync that adds a model from a description) is written down as Deferred, with its plan, not guarded again in the view.
## 2026-11-18 A key and a plugin's tap are the dock's tap

- What changed (key-next): one function makes a tap on Shiori, `tapShiori` (`src/ui/plus.js`), and every caller that taps with nothing open takes it (the + opens its own draft first, then taps): the dock's Shiori button, the headset and car keys (`mediaKeys`, now handed the shell's tap, `tap` in what `startShell` returns, and set in `src/app/main.js` once the shell is up, in place of the agent before it), and a plugin's `ui.shiori.tap()` (`src/ui/slots.js`). With a conversation open it is the agent's tap, as before, and says what the tap said at once. With nothing open it opens the standing draft first, as the dock's button did: the ready one at once, inside the tap, then taps; with none ready (the first moments), it waits for one, then taps, and what it returns is then a promise of what the tap said; when none can be made, the card says so, no tap is made, and the promise is of nothing. `ui.shiori.tap()`'s return, never written down before, is now in docs/plugins.md: the word when the tap is made at once (as before), a promise of it while the new conversation is still being made (on main, with nothing open, Halves' `tap()` said `'listening'`: with classic voice no microphone opened; with a live Koe one opened, in `me`). A plugin's `ui.shiori.send(text)`, which stands for the dock's box, takes the same road through `sendToShiori`, beside `tapShiori` over one helper (`inDraft`, `src/ui/plus.js`). `ui.shiori.send(text)` with nothing open opens the draft first, the ready one at once, else once one is, and writes the line there, where a turn answers it (on main the line was written into `me`, where no turn runs and nothing answers it); when no draft can be made the card says so and nothing is sent; with a conversation open it is the agent's send, as before, and it still returns nothing. `mediaKeys` takes `{ session, tap, voice }` in place of `{ session, agent, voice }`.
- Why we had to change: with nothing open (a removal whose draft didn't open, the gap after a removal before the next draft opens), no turn runs, so the agent's own tap opens no microphone there (tap-ear-next). The dock's button opened the draft first, but a key and a plugin's tap called the agent directly: a press of a headset or car key did nothing, and the dock went on saying "Tap to talk". A plugin's `ui.shiori.send` stood for the dock's box as `tap` for its button, and had the same gap: with nothing open the line went into `me` and was never answered (found by the final review). Run on main, with a live Koe the key did worse: it opened a live session in `me`, its setup with no trip, where no turn of Kokoro's runs, while the dock's button opened the draft and the session there. The Deferred entry "With nothing open, a headset or car key does nothing" planned this.
- Original design was wrong because: three callers made one act, a tap on Shiori, and only the dock's button knew that with nothing open the tap belongs in the draft. The draft-first rule lived in the button's handler, so the callers that came by another road (a key wired in main.js before the shell existed, the plugin object) reached the layer beneath it, which answers a different question (what a tap does in the conversation open), and the two disagreed exactly where nothing is open.
- Lesson: a hardware key or a plugin call that stands for a control of the app goes through the control's own function, never the layer beneath it; put the control's rules in that function, where every caller passes, not in one caller's handler. A call that must sometimes wait for what it acts on answers at once where it can (here the microphone's start and a phone's keyboard need the tap) and with a promise of the same answer only where it waits: the immediate answer callers already had stays as it was, and `await` takes both.

## 2026-11-18 One rule, the provider model id: a name takes a free one, and the sync skips what the ability has

- What changed (models-next; the Deferred entry "A model the traveller added can be added again under its id or its name"): every writer that adds a model to a provider's ability, and the provider page's list, keep one rule, one model for each provider model id on an ability, and nothing else. `syncProviders` (`src/core/llm/providers.js`) skips a model new to a provider's description when the ability already has a model with that provider model id (the traveller added it by id or from a list), as it skips one the traveller removed: theirs keeps its own layer and takes none of the description's values, and the description's key joins the template's list, so a later update skips it too, even once the traveller removes theirs. `addModelById` takes the first free name ("name (2)", `named`) where another model has the provider model id as its name, as `addListedModel` already did; it was refused there ("already used by another model"). The provider page's Add model then says so in a toast, `Added as "<name>": "<id>" is already used by another model.` (in ja, zh-Hans and zh-Hant too), read from the stored model. The page's fetched list leaves out the models its ability has by provider model id, and nothing more: a listed model whose display name another model has (one the traveller renamed, or another provider's) is offered with its Add, which takes a free name; the words "Already added, on another provider" went with that gate. `refuseHeld` stays the adds' one refusal of a second model. An API change: `llm.addModelById` no longer refuses a name another model has, and a sync no longer writes a model for that case. The model's own page still refuses a name another model has (`updateModel`: the traveller types the name there).
- Why we had to change: three shapes, each on main, found by the re-read and the second opinion of form-keep-next's touch 2: a plugin update that described a model the traveller had added made a second one ("x/new" and "X new"; "Y new" beside "Y new (2)") while the traveller did nothing on that page; a listed model whose name another model had showed "Already added, on <provider>" with no Add though its id was not added (an official provider's own model could not be taken from its list when another provider's model had its name); and Add model by id was refused when another model had its name, where the list's add of that id took a free name.
- Original design was wrong because: whether two models are the same was answered in three places by three rules: the sync by its description key or template id, the list by the display name (from 2026-10-21, before the id was what the adds refuse), and the add by id by the name check `saveModel` makes, which keeps names unique and was taken for "already added". A name is a label the screens need unique, not the model's identity.
- Lesson: an identity rule (one model for each provider model id on an ability) is kept by every writer that adds, a sync too, and by its key alone; a label the screens need unique (a display name) never refuses an add or gates a view's rows: it takes a free form ("name (2)"). A writer whose result differs from what the traveller typed says so where they look.

## 2026-11-18 A Fetch's answer lands in the part on show, under the connection it was asked on

- What changed (models-next; the Deferred entry "A model list fetched while the page changes"; reworded 2026-11-19 for model-list-a-next, which took out the drop below): the provider page's Fetch model list (`modelsPart`, `src/ui/screens/provider.js`) no longer draws into the part its tap was in.
