A recording refused by a model goes up once in each of the turn's calls (one per step) and once in its write-down (a call of its own), at most.
- Lesson: a choice a call can make from what it saw itself is made at its end, as one more pass over what it passed over, opt-in for the caller it serves (the turn's own call, as `askResting` is); it is never a mark, kept between calls, of which lines are past their turn. What a call learns about its own asks is the call's (held beside what its resolver holds, gone with it), never a flag on the stored record. A count of tries counts the unit it is told in (each half's own turns that worked), at the one place that unit ends, so what comes between turns counts nothing. A new optional part of a hook the modules share goes into its options object from the start, and a part added beside two positional ones moves them in too. What another call will do is a guess whatever it reads: read it from how that call's last turn ended, kept until its next turn that worked clears it, so a wrong guess lasts only until then; never from the bench, where a clock ends the record (a rest) and what the call could ask is one more guess.

## 2026-11-26 One counter decides when a plugin has failed enough, and no failure writes the traveller's switch
- Changed (plugin-fails-next; the design check plugin-fails-design.md, routes (a) and (b); the user's answer U148, 2026-11-26: "All three (Recommended)"; the Deferred entries "How a plugin's failures are counted still has several counters", "A live protocol's crashes add up across sessions" and "A plugin whose module fails as it loads counts a launch, whatever the failure"): the registry's run per plugin is the one answer. A start's failure is that launch's, and never counted, in a launch or in a run (`guard` logs a start's error as a `plugin.error` line with no count): `startInstalled` keeps nothing (`MAX_START_FAILURES`, installed.json's `startFailures`, the "was turned off … It didn't start the last few times annai opened" card and the host's own `plugin.disabled` line are gone; a failure is `{ id, error }`), and the module's load runs inside the guarded, timed start, so whatever it throws is that launch's card, a load that never settles is given up on at the start limit, and a load that ends after the limit runs no `activate()`. The dock's own runs per avatar call and its swap to annai's own after three throws are gone: an avatar's calls use the registry's door alone, and annai's own takes the place once the plugin is switched off, as the shell makes the dock again. A call of the plugin's that returned is reported (`succeeded`) where annai reads the return: a live session that is ready, each message its handler takes and each driver call, a trigger's check, a backup place's call, a provider's headers, classify, list and sign-in; `open`, `attach` and `reopen` end no run of their own (the session's ready does), a photo upload's build ends none (the chat call after it does), and a verdict on a return (a wrong `create()`, a hook loop) is no call. A return annai can't use is a crash, not a success: a provider's `list` that gives no array, its `headers` that give no object. A live protocol's `classifyClose` that throws, and a sign-in's `returned()` that throws before it returns a promise, count as the rejections do. `Registry.settled(pluginId, name, result)` reads a promise where annai reads no answer (an avatar's call, a live message handler and driver call, heard clips, a dock action): it resolves as a success, and what it rejects with goes to `threw`. A `plugin.error` line is written unless it is its plugin's last line over again (`#note`; a crash's line carries its count, 1 to 3, then one line with no count once the plugin is switched off), and a plugin already switched off counts nothing more. A live driver's `close()` that throws never stops annai's own close. WebKit's fetch words "URL is not valid or contains user credentials." joined `isBadAddress`, and a browser `Event` a plugin rejects with is an answer (`isPluginBug`), as a failed fetch is.
- Why we had to change: three counters answered "has this plugin failed enough" by three rules (the registry's run, the dock's runs per call with any error class and no card, and a launch count kept in installed.json), and the live path, a trigger, a backup place, a provider's setup and sign-in never reported a success, so their crashes added up across working calls (a live voice plugin off at its third crash in a run of working sessions). The kept count wrote the traveller's own on/off switch: measured in the design check, it turned annai's own plugins off where their files failed to load on three launches in a row (in WebKit's words and Chromium's alike, as every error outside `guard` counted), which then stayed off after the files loaded again, and `official.js` blamed the traveller for it ("which you turned off"). A module whose load never settled kept `startInstalled`, and so every model call, waiting for good. Four calls dropped the promise they returned, so an `async` crash there was the page's "Something went wrong", counted nowhere.
- Original design was wrong because: each counter was added to answer the one question at the place a finding landed (the launch count as a review's Minor on 2026-10-15, the dock's swap on 2026-10-17 and 10-18), and each kept state of its own: every start finding of three builds landed in the kept count, and the dock's count was the only bound on an avatar that throws at audio rate. A start that failed isn't running either way, so the kept count saved only the card at the fourth launch.
- Routes not taken: no success reports at all ("three crashes since annai opened": an endpoint type counts by position, so odd replies would add up over a page's life and take the user's own model off); a run per part (more state, every part must report its own successes, and a switch-off takes the whole plugin anyway); keeping the launch count and only moving the load inside `guard` (the engines' words for a failed import differ: WebKit's would still count); counting a start in the run (WebKit's failed-import words are a crash, so a launch and two setup taps would switch the plugin off, which U148's "a broken plugin shows its card at each launch" doesn't say).
- What it leaves (the known limitation C240, widened with U148): a plugin of which one part keeps crashing while another works is not switched off while a call of it that works comes between its crashes (an avatar whose `setState` throws between working `setLevel`s keeps its place); an error whose words change at each call, or two that take turns, is a Trace line each (chief's C260); a plugin's own bad address is an answer in every session; a start that keeps failing shows its card at each launch until the traveller turns the plugin off; an engine's sentence for a failed request that the words list doesn't know counts as a crash, for that run only.
- Lesson: when fixes keep landing on several counters that answer one question, keep one and remove the others with their state: a count kept past the run it counts has to be undone by something, and here it wrote a switch that is the traveller's alone. One rule for what ends a run (a call annai made that returned) is reported at every place that counts one, a promise included, at the one door (`settled`); an outcome that loses its old bound (the dock's count bounded an error at audio rate) gets a limit of its own in the same change: the same words repeating are one Trace line (words that change at each call have no limit). A stored count a newer version no longer reads is read as nothing, with no migration. This corrects "2026-11-25 Only a crash counts against a plugin wherever annai runs its code, through one door" for the four calls whose promise annai didn't read and for the launch count.
- API change: `Registry.settled(pluginId, name, result)` is new; `startInstalled()` returns `{ id, error }[]` (no `disabled`); `MAX_START_FAILURES` is gone; installed.json's `startFailures` is neither written nor read (one an older annai wrote is ignored, and a plugin it turned off stays off until its switch); `openLive`'s `registry` needs `settled`; `closeKind(type, code, reason, onBug)` takes an optional fourth argument; `isPluginBug` counts no browser `Event`; a provider's `list` that gives no array and its `headers` that give no object are crashes (an empty list and a success before); a `plugin.error` line is written once while its words repeat; for a plugin author (docs/plugins.md): a start is never counted, in a launch or in a run, the module's load counts toward the 10 seconds (a load that ends after them runs no `activate`), an avatar keeps its place while its plugin is on, and what a live handler's, a driver call's or an avatar call's promise rejects with is read and counted when a crash.
